# Changelog

All notable changes to the AITeamForge Homebrew Tap.

Format: [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/)
Versioning: [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html)

## [Unreleased]

- **XACA-0976 — The hostname-clipping guard on Fleet Monitor team cards now applies at every viewport width, in both skins.** XACA-0973 added `min-width: 0` on the `.session-detail`/`.session-value` chain plus `overflow-wrap: anywhere` on `.teams-grid .session-value`, but scoped them inside its own `@media (min-width: 1080px) and (max-width: 1200px)` tablet band. Nothing in the mechanism was specific to that band, so the two declarations are lifted out. The `repeat(3, 1fr)` column pin deliberately **stays banded** — de-scoping it too would pin 3 columns at every width and change desktop density, the opposite of what XACA-0973 argued for.

  **These rules are load-bearing in BOTH skins, not parity in one.** With the guard removed entirely, the lcars skin clips **37px at 1080 and 20px at 1133** — the iPad landscape widths XACA-0973 exists for. An earlier draft of this entry claimed lcars had zero pre-fix overflow; that measurement compared *develop* (guard banded) against *this branch* (guard global), so both states had an active guard at those widths and both read 0. It conditioned on the treatment being present and then concluded no defect existed. Post-fix: zero row overflow at 320/375/414/500/600/799/900/1080/1133/1210/1440/1920 in both skins, measured at the row rather than the leaf, mutation-verified in both directions.

  **Overflow on this surface is hostname-shape dependent, and that is the durable lesson.** The sample hostname used throughout this work contains a hyphen — a natural break opportunity, so `overflow-wrap: normal` already breaks it. Substitute an ordinary hyphen-free MagicDNS name and the lcars skin overflows pre-fix at **seven** widths (320, 375, 900, 1080, 1133, 1210, 1920), pushing the entire value column out of the card rather than just the hostname. Any future measurement here that varies only viewport will understate the defect.

  **Consumer-visible impact, and it is an accessibility fix rather than a cosmetic one.** Without the guard an unbreakable hostname could not shrink below its content and overflowed into `.team-card`'s `overflow: hidden`, clipping mid-word with no ellipsis and no signal — arguably a **WCAG 1.4.10 (Reflow)** failure at 320px, since the clipped content was unreachable with no scroll mechanism. The clipping was also silent *and plausible*: lcars2 at 320px rendered `mstudio.tail1234.ts.ne`, which reads as a complete different hostname rather than a truncated one, and a hostname is an identifier someone may type or compare.

  **Scoped correction to XACA-0973's figures.** Its `34px@1080 / 16px@1133` measurements are **broadly confirmed** — they reproduce against faithful chrome as 37/20. Only its phone-width table (145px at 320-414, 79px at 500, cards of 124px/194px) is a harness artifact: it came from a stub hardcoding a fixed 200px sidebar with 36px margin and 30px padding at every viewport, modelling neither the `@media (max-width: 480px)` content collapse in the lcars skin nor the `--sidebar-width` steps. Its causal story was **incomplete, not inverted** — the `.team-avatar-stack` really is the differentiator at 1080/1133, where both skins have identical 251px/268px cards and only lcars overflows; what it missed is a second regime at lcars2/320, where that skin's own 480px block keeps `margin-left: -1px; padding: 10px 10px 60px 0` and never collapses. Two defects, two regimes; the global scope covers both.

  Both declarations are kept. Measured in isolation at lcars2/320, either one alone eliminates the overflow (an open discrepancy is recorded in the stylesheet comments: two passes reported 14px and 4px for the both-removed control against the identical sample, and neither truncation nor a leaf-vs-row measurement difference has been verified as the cause) — which refutes XACA-0973's conclusion that `min-width: 0` is inert — but they cover different content shapes: the hyphen-free hostname needs `overflow-wrap` in lcars at 900-1920, where `min-width: 0` alone will not force a break. `text-overflow: ellipsis` + `white-space: nowrap` remains refused, and the reason is stronger than originally recorded: lcars2 emits **no** `title=` attribute at all and renders the raw hostname, so ellipsis there would have zero recovery path rather than an inert one.

  The `?v=` cache-buster is bumped `20260826b` -> `20260826c` on all 6 mirrored links to the changed stylesheet. The two remaining links in the canonical repo sit in `lcars2/lcars-doublenode.html` and `lcars2/lcars-mainevent.html`, which `sync-tap.sh`'s `-not -name` filters exclude from the mirror, so they have no tap copy.

- **XACA-0971 — Removes 9 dead JS files that were loaded by zero HTML pages, together with the 5 of them that were tap-mirrored (the other 4 are excluded from mirroring by the `doublenode-*`/`mainevent-*` patterns and had no tap copy).** Companion cleanup to XACA-0970: that ticket found the `finance`/`legal`/`medical` org-resolution bug copy-pasted into fourteen files across seven variants, nine of them dead, and fixed only the five live copies. This ticket removes the dead copies outright rather than leaving stale duplicates behind: `public/app.js`, `public/academy-app.js`, `public/doublenode-app.js`, `public/mainevent-app.js`, `lcars/js/lcars-academy-app.js`, `lcars/js/lcars-all-app.js`, `lcars/js/lcars-doublenode-app.js`, `lcars/js/lcars-finance-app.js`, `lcars/js/lcars-mainevent-app.js` — none loaded by any page, none carrying logic the live `lcars2/js/` equivalents lack. `lcars/js/lcars-credentials-ui.js` is also dead but is scoped to a separate ticket and untouched here.

  Of the 9, five were tap-mirrored (`app.js`, `lcars-academy-app.js`, `lcars-all-app.js`, `lcars-finance-app.js`, and `academy-app.js`, confirmed by direct inspection rather than inferred from `sync-tap.sh`'s exclusion patterns) and are removed here to match; the other four (`doublenode-app.js`, `mainevent-app.js`, `lcars-doublenode-app.js`, `lcars-mainevent-app.js`) were already excluded from the mirror by the `doublenode-*`/`mainevent-*`/`lcars-doublenode-*`/`lcars-mainevent-*` patterns and have no tap copy to remove.

  `fleet-monitor/server/tests/cache-busting.test.js` is also tap-mirrored; its root-static-mount probe is repointed from the now-deleted `/app.js` to `/all.html` — a live, root-mounted asset served by the same `express.static` mount — so the mount's `Cache-Control` header wiring stays under real coverage.

- **XACA-0979 — Fleet Monitor LCARS links no longer route through a hardcoded Tailscale Funnel host.** Consumer-visible on every tap machine that opens an LCARS terminal from the Fleet Monitor dashboard. `getLcarsUrl()` returned `https://darren.tail4637d5.ts.net<path>` for any port listed in `LCARS_PATH_MAP`, and only fell through to `http://${hostname}:${port}` for ports the map happened to omit. That host stopped resolving after a device rename, so every mapped link was dead; the links that still worked did so by accident of omission, not by design.

  Fixing the constant alone would not have worked: `tailscale serve` still advertises its routes under the old, unresolvable name, so the corrected hostname 404s on the same paths. The funnel config had additionally drifted from the port map on two further axes — declared paths (`/freelance-doublenode-workstats` vs the live `/freelance-workstats`) and ports (`legal-coparenting` mapped at 8230 against a canonical 8320, plus an 8220 `/legal` entry matching no team at all). Re-provisioning the funnel would have fixed one axis and left two live.

  Both constants are therefore deleted rather than corrected. XACA-0971 removed 9 of the 14 files carrying this defect as dead code in the same cycle, so the fix lands on the files any page actually loads — 3 of which are tap-mirrored. `getLcarsUrl()` now derives host **and** port from the reporting machine's own per-session payload, so neither can go stale on a rename: `http://${lcarsSession.hostname}:${localPort}`. If a session ever reports no hostname it warns and returns `null`, which callers already guard, instead of emitting `http://undefined:<port>`.

  **Badge affordance.** `fleet-monitor/plugins/main-event/public/mainevent-app.js` computed its green "OPEN" badge from online-state alone, BEFORE the link was built, so a session reporting no hostname rendered a card that looked actionable and silently did nothing on click — worse than a visibly-broken link, since a console warning is not a user-visible affordance. The badge now reads "UNAVAILABLE" and the card carries an explanatory tooltip, using only affordance classes the stylesheets already define. This file is tap-only (no dev-team canonical source), so it is patched here directly rather than mirrored. The identically-shaped bug in the dev-team dashboards was resolved by XACA-0971 deleting those files rather than by a fix.

  Verified live against the fleet payload before changing anything: every reported session across all machines carries a routable Tailscale MagicDNS hostname — none missing, none a bare `.local` name — so the funnel indirection was dead weight rather than a fallback covering a real gap.

- **XACA-0973** — Fleet Monitor ORGS team grid pinned to 3 columns across the 1080–1200px tablet band, fixing iPad landscape rendering 2 across. Includes the accompanying `min-width: 0` / `overflow-wrap: anywhere` guard that stops an unbreakable hostname being clipped mid-word by `.team-card`'s `overflow: hidden` at the narrower card widths, and the `?v=` cache-buster bump on the 6 mirrored pages that link the changed stylesheet.

- **XACA-0972 — `vault-keygen.js` no longer defaults the fleet server to `http://localhost:3000`.** Consumer-visible on every tap machine: the shipped vault client read `$FLEET_MONITOR_URL` and otherwise fell back to localhost, never consulting `~/.aiteamforge/fleet-config.json` where the real endpoint lives. On a box where that variable is unset — the normal case — every vault operation failed against a port nothing listens on, surfacing as `fetch failed`. A silent localhost default for a *fleet* service turns "not configured" into "connection refused", which reads to the operator as a network fault rather than a missing setting.

  New `resolveFleetUrl()` resolves `$FLEET_MONITOR_URL` first, then `.centralServer.apiEndpoint` from `~/.aiteamforge/fleet-config.json` (`~/.dev-team/` as fallback), stripping the `/api/...` suffix — including the bare `<base>/api` form, which the obvious single-pattern strip silently misses and which produced a malformed base during the sibling XACA-0885 fix. When nothing resolves it returns `null` and the caller fails with an actionable message naming all three remedies; it never invents a localhost target. `DEFAULT_SERVER_URL` is removed.

  **Tap-layout constraint held:** `vault-keygen.js` is the module the flattened `share/scripts/` layout has other clients require, so its top-level dependencies remain `os`/`fs`/`path`/`child_process` with libsodium still lazy. Verified by copying the file alone into an empty directory with no `node_modules` — it loads, and both `resolveFleetUrl()` and `defaultMachineSlug()` work. The resolver was deliberately placed here rather than in a new shared module for exactly this reason: a new file would have needed adding to both `sync-tap.sh` and the installer allowlist, or this copy would break on a top-level require.

  `share/scripts/package.json` is mirrored in the same cycle: its `test` script listed only two of the three canonical suites. As before, the paths it names do not resolve inside the flattened `share/scripts/` layout — that file is carried so the libsodium `npm install` bootstrap resolves, not so `npm test` runs there — so this keeps the mirror faithful to canonical without changing tap behaviour.

  **Round 2 also mirrors `share/scripts/msg-client.js`**, which carried the same `http://localhost:3000` default and now routes through `resolveFleetUrl()`. An explicit `--server` still wins, so the caller-side resolution shipped for the kb-msg relay is unaffected — this closes the last of the four clients that carried the localhost default.

  `resolveFleetUrl()` gained two corrections consumers should know about. It now **loops over every candidate config** and falls through one that exists but carries no `apiEndpoint`, instead of stopping at the first file that merely exists — a present-but-empty `~/.aiteamforge/fleet-config.json` previously masked a good `~/.dev-team/` one. And it now **validates the resolved URL**, rejecting non-`http(s)` schemes, hostless URLs, and credentials-before-host: the value is read from a config *file*, and the migration tool seals real API keys to machine public keys fetched from that host, so a corrupted config could otherwise redirect key material.

  Mirrors the canonical change in `fleet-monitor/client/vault-keygen.js`. The 404-vs-unreachable exit-code work and the `cc` credential-chain fix in the same ticket are in files that are not tap-mirrored.

- **XACA-0970 — teams whose division code differs from their team id no longer resolve to an UNKNOWN organization.** Consumer-visible: `finance` rendered under UNKNOWN in the Fleet Monitor ORGS view. Three compounding faults, only the first of which is structural:

  **1. The registry lookup could never match a suffixed team.** `getOrganizationGroup()` receives a *division code* (`session.division`, e.g. `finance`, `legal`) and looked up `teamConfig.teams[code]`, which is keyed by *team id* (`finance-personal`, `legal-coparenting`). For every suffixed team those differ, so that path had never once succeeded. Measured against the live registry: `teams['legal']` MISS while `teams['legal-coparenting']` exists. `legal` displayed correctly **only** via a hard-coded `startsWith` fallback — its registry entry was decorative. Resolution now falls back to a **registry prefix match**, so a newly-registered team resolves with no code change.

  **2. `finance` was simply skipped** when the `freelance`/`legal`/`medical` prefix fallbacks were added, and it was absent from the static map — so it fell through to `UNKNOWN`.

  **3. The function was copy-pasted into ELEVEN files across SEVEN variants, six of them dead.** The registry lookup existed in 2, `freelance` in 6, `legal` in 11, `medical` in 2, and `finance` in exactly **1 — a file loaded by no page at all**. The bug had already been "fixed" in dead code, which is precisely why it looked handled and never ran; `medical` works on the routed dashboard only by luck. All five *live* copies now delegate to a single `shared/js/lcars-org-resolution.js`; the six dead ones are untouched and removed separately under XACA-0971.

  **Gate-round corrections.** Both gates independently re-derived the file inventory and found my count wrong: it is **14 copies, not 11** — my scan globbed `lcars/js/`, `lcars2/js/` and a hard-coded `app.js`, omitting three more at `public/{academy,mainevent,doublenode}-app.js`. Corrected reachability: **1** on the routed path, **4** served-but-unrouted (`lcars2/` pages return 200 by direct URL but no route or link points at them), **9** dead. The test gate also caught that the prefix fallbacks used a bare `indexOf(prefix) === 0` with no dash boundary — so `financex` would have resolved as FINANCE — while the registry match one tier above correctly required `code + '-'`; both now require the boundary.

  **FINANCE also needed a colour.** `getGroupColor()` is duplicated across the same 14 files and FINANCE was absent from every one, so the org this ticket exists to surface would have rendered with a fallback colour. It now resolves through the shared module too, and `.org-finance` is defined. `.org-medical` was added at the same time: MEDICAL has been mapped to a class that never existed for as long as that mapping has, silently falling back — not introduced here, but fixed rather than left as a second instance of the same gap.

  Falling through to UNKNOWN now emits a `console.warn` naming the unresolved code (warn-once, so a render loop cannot flood), and a missing module emits `console.error` rather than silently degrading — every fault above survived because `|| 'UNKNOWN'` said nothing. Verified by executing the module and each wired file in a VM against the live registry: zero UNKNOWN across all live division codes on all five files, with negative controls confirming both the missing-module error and the unknown-team warning actually fire.

- **XACA-0963 follow-up — SETTINGS and ADMIN now show the active-section indicator bar.** The utility pills had no equivalent of `.sidebar-button[data-section].active::after`, so a selected pill inverted its colours but showed none of the 6px vertical marker every other nav surface uses. Added identically — `left: 0`, `width: 6px`, `background: currentColor` — matching both the sidebar and the Kanban's own `.legend-pill.mode-pill.active::after`, so SETTINGS shows tan and ADMIN shows orange. The base rule also gained the `position: relative` the absolutely-positioned bar requires; without it the bar anchors to the wrong ancestor. Excludes `.offline-pill` for the same reason the `.active` background rule does — that pill carries fleet state, not section state, and takes `.active` incidentally by sharing `data-section="machines"` with a real nav target. SOUND has no `data-section` and never matches.

- **XACA-0963 follow-up — sidebar and utility-bar labels reseated to match the dashboard buttons.** The dashboard links at the sidebar's foot (`.sidebar-link`) use `padding: 6px 12px` with **no** `line-height` override, so their labels sit slightly higher than the corner-seating applied earlier to `.sidebar-button` and `.legend-pill` (`padding: 6px 12px 3px` plus `line-height: 1`). Those two changes compounded: `line-height: 1` removes the leading that lifts the glyphs, and the 3px bottom padding dropped them further, leaving both sets visibly lower than the dashboard links they sit above. All three button families now share identical seating.

- **XACA-0963 follow-up — utility-pill type matched to the Kanban, and the SOUND label finally seats in the corner.** The pills were 0.65rem with 0.08em letter-spacing where the Kanban's `.legend-pill` is 12px / 600 / 1px; matched exactly. Separately, `.legend-pill.sound-pill` still carried layout declarations inherited from its `.candy-pill` ancestry — its own `font-size`/`letter-spacing`, `flex: 0 0 auto`, `min-width: 3.5rem` and **`justify-content: center`**. At 3 classes it silently outranked the 2-class base rule, so SOUND kept a centred label and its own width while every sibling sat corner-seated at a uniform 100px. The selector had been retargeted from `.candy-pill` to `.legend-pill` without revisiting its contents. That rule is now colour-only, with a comment recording why it must stay that way; layout belongs to the base rule. Labels also gained `text-overflow: ellipsis` so an unexpectedly long one degrades instead of breaking the row.

- **XACA-0963 follow-up — the utility-bar pills now match the Kanban: uniform width, label in the lower-right corner.** They had been authored with `align-items: center; justify-content: center` and content-based widths (`flex: 0 0 auto; min-width: 6rem`), so labels sat centred and `1 OFFLINE` rendered visibly wider than `ADMIN` or `SOUND`. The LCARS Kanban's mode bar — the pattern this was ported from — uses a uniform `flex: 0 0 100px` with `align-items/justify-content: flex-end`. Matched: every pill is now exactly 100px with its label seated in the corner, consistent with the sidebar buttons. Verified the longest realistic label (`12 OFFLINE`, ~62px) fits the ~74px of usable width. At ≤600px the uniform width is dropped and shrink re-enabled, so the cluster still cannot be clipped by the `overflow: hidden` parent.

- **XACA-0963 follow-up — stylesheets had no cache-buster, so CSS changes did not reach browsers.** Every JS asset carried one (`?v=20260520`, `?v=1.0`) but **all 22 local stylesheet links across both skins carried none**, so a browser holding a cached copy could keep serving it after a deploy. The server sends `Cache-Control: no-cache`, which requests revalidation but does not guarantee a refetch — and the LCARS cockpit runs inside a WKWebView that caches aggressively. Symptom: sidebar changes shipped and verified in the served CSS, yet still rendered stale on screen. All 22 links now carry `?v=20260825`, matching the convention the JS assets already used.

- **XACA-0963 follow-up — the ANALYTICS sidebar button was invisible, and sidebar labels now sit in the lower-right corner.** Consumer-visible: `ANALYTICS` had **no background rule at all** in either stylesheet, so it painted the sidebar's own colour and read as blank space between MACHINES and ENGINES rather than as a button. Pre-existing, but removing SETTINGS and ADMIN from the sidebar made it conspicuous. It now uses `--lcars-tan` — the slot SETTINGS vacated when it moved to the utility bar, a classic LCARS sidebar tone, 8.32:1 with black text, and carrying no status meaning (green now means "fleet healthy" on the OFFLINE pill, so reusing it in nav would have muddied that).

  Also: sidebar buttons were sized with `min-height` only, which does not guarantee identical rendering; they are now pinned to `height: var(--bar-height)` with `flex: 0 0 auto` so no button can grow or shrink relative to its siblings. And their labels read as vertically centred despite `align-items: flex-end` being correct — the default line-height's leading plus 6px of bottom padding left the text floating. `line-height: 1` and a 3px bottom padding seat it in the corner.

- **XACA-0952 (round 7) — mirror a corrected assertion message and a navigation aid.** `TestFixtureDyadicity`'s off-grid failure message claimed `round(x, 2)` **would** change the value. That is not true and the same round's own documentation says so: the quarter-grid rule is sufficient but not necessary, and 45.63 satisfies `round(x,2) == x` while sitting off-grid with denominator 2^47. The message now states what off-grid does NOT prove, carries the counterexample inline, and says what it does mean — the value is outside the conservative grid this fixture vouches for, so agreement under the buggy identity `round2` is no longer guaranteed — ending with the action to take. This was the third place one conceptual confusion had settled: comment prose, then the invariant documentation, then the runtime message a developer actually reads; each earlier correction fixed the layer in front of it and left the next one standing. `RECONCILIATION.md` also gains a Quick Reference so the construction procedure is findable ahead of three rounds of superseded design narrative — the narrative is kept deliberately, since it records why two earlier framings failed. Mirror-only; no shipped runtime behaviour differs.

- **XACA-0952 (round 6) — mirror a HOME-isolation fix for a test inherited from XACA-0954.** `test_generator_skips_self_entry_when_output_outside_home` never set `HOME` at all, so the generator subprocess ran against the ambient environment; on a clean `$HOME` the `devteam` domain's fail-loud guard cannot find `$HOME/dev-team/finance`, exits 1 with MISSING DOMAIN ROOTS, and the assertion fails before the self-entry logic under test is ever reached. It now points `HOME` at a synthetic root seeded by the file's own existing helper — the idiom its two sibling generator tests already used — and checks its outside-HOME sanity guard against that synthetic root so the guard is deterministic rather than dependent on where the runner puts temp files. The test was green on any developer machine and red on any clean one; it was invisible because nothing executed lcars-ui/tests in CI until XACA-0952 added that gate, and this was the first defect the gate caught in newly-merged code. Mirror-only; no shipped runtime behaviour differs.

- **XACA-0952 (round 5) — mirror the fix that makes `TestFixtureDyadicity` able to see the defect it guards.** The guard added in round 4 asserted on the server's already-`round(x, 2)`'d payload, so it read values snapped onto the quarter grid by construction and was blind to the exact desync it exists to catch: adding 1 ms to one fixture item produced four genuine CLI/server mismatches while all three dyadicity tests still passed. It now reads the RAW pre-rounding values, matching the method the reconciliation document's own proof already used. This is the SECOND time a verification on this fixture was applied to a value that had already had the transformation applied — re-derived against commit 588b6746, which shows round 1's proof ran a real substituted jq 1.7.1 binary against the real suite and so is not an instance of the class — the document was right each time and the mechanization was not, which is why the standing test now does what the hand-check did. Also: the quarter-grid invariant is documented as sufficient-and-deliberately-conservative rather than an iff (the double nearest 45.63 satisfies `round(x,2)==x` with denominator 2^47), and an XPASS(strict) now tells the reader to investigate why a probe-confirmed-buggy jq passed rather than to delete the marker. Mirror-only; no shipped runtime behaviour differs.

- **XACA-0952 (round 4) — mirror the dyadic parity fixture.** Round 3's fixture was built to be "exact to <=2 decimal digits", which is the WRONG invariant and reproduced XACA-0968 on CI (`CLI=45.629999999999995 vs server=45.63`). What matters is that the raw IEEE-754 double is bit-identical to `round(x, 2)`; 2.34 and 431.76 are exact decimals but not exact binary doubles, and round 2's uniform ratio=2.0 had survived only by the unnamed accident that its aggregates were exact multiples of 0.25. Note that "dyadic" alone is vacuous here — every finite double is dyadic by construction, including the buggy value above — so the operative condition is that the reduced denominator divides 4. The fixture now solves varied per-item ratios in quarter-integer units across buckets sized 7/7/7/8, keeping `handicap != median` per bucket and globally. A new `TestFixtureDyadicity` checks the property mechanically on the live payload every run rather than trusting a one-time hand-verification — which is what let round 3's malformed proof (it applied the rounding comparison to already-rounded literals, where both filters are no-ops) go unnoticed for two rounds. Mirror-only change; no shipped runtime behaviour differs.

- **XACA-0952 (round 3) — mirror the final review-round fixes.** The committed parity fixture's per-item ratios are now VARIED rather than uniformly 2.0, so `handicap != median` in every bucket and globally. Exactness (which is what decouples the fixture from XACA-0968's jq bug) never required uniformity, and uniformity cost real detection power: proven by mutation, patching the server's median to compute a mean PASSED against the old uniform fixture — the bug was invisible — and FAILS in all four buckets plus global against the new one. The four XACA-0968 xfails are also now `strict=True`: the condition is a live probe of jq's behaviour and each expected literal is unreachable under the buggy identity-function `round2`, so XPASS is structurally impossible and strictness is a no-cost tripwire for when XACA-0968 lands. Mirror-only change; no shipped runtime behaviour differs.

- **XACA-0952 (round 2) — mirror the gate-fix batch.** `TestLiveBoardParity` read a gitignored live board and was unconditionally red on any runner; replaced with `TestFixtureBoardParity` against a committed fixture whose points sit on a 0.25 grid so every computed value is exact by construction (a first draft sampled real board numbers and silently reproduced the jq bug below across ~15 fields — caught by empirical A/B testing, not review). Four parity tests are now conditionally xfailed on a runtime probe of the jq binary, tracked as XACA-0968: jq 1.7.1 parses `. * 100 as $s | ...` as `. * (100 as $s | ...)`, so `round2` degrades to an identity function and returns values unrounded. The probe tests the actual binary rather than `sys.platform`, because the bug is jq-version-specific — a Linux box on jq >=1.8 would be wrongly xfailed and a macOS box on a stale jq 1.7.x wrongly would not. Mirror-only change; no shipped runtime behaviour differs.

- **XACA-0952 — mirror the `lcars-ui/tests/` fixes that make the suite green under the new CI gate.** `lcars-ui/tests/` (1063 tests) was executed by ZERO of dev-team's CI workflows; the canonical side adds `.github/workflows/lcars-ui-pytest-suite.yml` to run it with zero tolerance for failures, errors, or skips. Eight test files needed fixes first, mirrored here: a hermetic dev-HOME so the port-collision integration tests resolve THIS checkout's tap rather than depending on `$HOME/dev-team` being the checkout root (they failed 5/5 on a CI runner); two `Content-Length`-mismatch flakes where a declared header disagreed with the bytes actually sent, leaving unread inbound data that sends RST instead of FIN on close; a cross-file environment-variable leak fixed at the polluter; a process-lifetime alias-warning reset for order-independence; and a stale assertion re-pointed at the real 50 MiB/413 boundary. Mirror-only change — no shipped runtime behaviour differs.
- **XACA-0963 — the Fleet Monitor's horizontal bar is now a right-aligned utility cluster, matching the LCARS Kanban UI's mode bar.** Consumer-visible: the six metric candy pills (TOTAL / ONLINE / OFFLINE / SESSIONS / AUX-1 / AUX-2) no longer render. In their place the bar carries a flex spacer, a separator tick, and a right-aligned cluster of `legend-pill` buttons — SETTINGS, ADMIN, SOUND — ported from `lcars-ui`'s `.legend-pill` / `.utility-cluster` / `.mode-bar-spacer` / `.mode-bar-separator` vocabulary so the two UIs read as one system. SETTINGS and ADMIN leave the vertical sidebar; both REFRESH controls (the sidebar button and the ENGINES panel's) are removed outright.

  **The metric pipeline is left INTACT AND DORMANT, not deleted.** All six `.candy-pill` lookups in `lcars-fleet-core.js` are null-safe (`if (!pill) return`, `if (pills.length === 0) return`, or `forEach` over an empty NodeList), verified by reading each site, so with the markup gone they no-op rather than throw and the metrics can be resurfaced without rebuilding the plumbing. Accepted loss: the at-a-glance readout and the OFFLINE pill's red-alert cue.

  **Three wiring traps, each of which would have failed silently:** (1) the pills first called a global `switchSection()` that does not exist in this skin — navigation is a *method* bound by selector, so the binding was widened to `.sidebar-button[data-section], .legend-pill[data-section]` (a shared `NAV_SELECTOR`, used by both the click handler and the active-state toggle; if those two ever diverge a pill navigates but never highlights). (2) The muted-state CSS was scoped to `.candy-pill.sound-pill.sound-muted`; the sound engine finds the pill by `id` and toggles `.sound-muted` regardless of class, so mute would have kept *working* while becoming invisible — retargeted to `.legend-pill`. (3) `lcars-sound.js` gated interaction sounds on `.candy-pill:not([data-candy])`, so the new pills would have fallen silent; the selector now also covers `.legend-pill`.

  The pills are `div`s with `role="button"`, so Enter/Space activation and a `:focus-visible` ring are provided explicitly — a div gets neither for free.

  `lcars2/` receives the subset it can support: candy pills removed, SETTINGS relocated to a right-aligned cluster, ENGINES refresh removed. No SOUND or ADMIN there — that skin never loads `lcars-sound.js` and has no admin section, and wiring in an audio subsystem is a feature rather than a header restyle.

  **Review-round fixes.** A `var NAV_SELECTOR` had been inserted *above* `'use strict';` inside the IIFE, which breaks the directive prologue — `'use strict'` becomes an inert string literal and strict mode is silently disabled for the whole ~1230-line module. Moved below the directive, with a comment at the point of temptation saying why it must stay there; verified behaviourally (an undeclared-global assignment now throws) rather than by position alone. Also: `lcars2`'s utility pill was focusable but inert on Enter/Space — only the click half of the binding had been widened, so it was reachable by Tab and did nothing; the keydown handler is now wired there too. And three orphaned `.sidebar-button.refresh-btn` rules in `lcars-fleet-theme.css` were removed, which the main stylesheet's cleanup had missed.

  **UX-round fixes.** The `:focus-visible` ring this PR introduced used `--lcars-cyan` on every pill, which measures **1.50:1 on tan, 1.27:1 on orange and 1.66:1 on rose** — all failing WCAG 2.1 AA SC 1.4.11's 3:1 minimum for focus indicators (measured against the real tokens, not estimated). The ring is now **state-conditional**: black by default (8.32 / 9.81 / 7.51:1 against those same three) and cyan only where the pill has gone black on `.active` (12.44:1). A single flat colour cannot satisfy 3:1 against both the pastel backgrounds and their inverted state, so the two rules must not be collapsed back into one — the comment says so at the point of temptation.

  Also: the pills were `flex: 0 0 auto` with a fixed `min-width`, so they could not compress — and `.lcars-main`/`body` both set `overflow: hidden`. On a ~375px viewport the three-pill (ADMIN-visible) cluster needs about 212px against roughly 209px available, and the overflow would have been silently **clipped** rather than scrolled. The candy-pill layout this replaced used `flex: 1; min-width: 0` and could never overflow; the narrow-width rule now allows shrink with ellipsis, restoring that guarantee.

  **Persistent OFFLINE indicator restored.** Removing the candy pills confined fleet-offline state to the OVERVIEW section's summary cards — a real glanceability loss for an ops tool, since the OFFLINE pill's red-alert cue had been visible from every section. A compact OFFLINE pill now lives in the utility cluster: hidden entirely while the fleet is healthy (so it costs no space in the common case), shown with a count and a slow pulse when machines are offline, and `prefers-reduced-motion` turns the pulse into a static brightness lift.

  It is deliberately **not** routed through `updatePill()`. That function early-returns when its `.candy-pill` is absent — which is now always — so anything hung off it would silently never run. The indicator is driven from the same live `data.offlineMachines` value instead, on the same tick.

  Its text is white rather than the black every other pill uses: black on `--lcars-red` (`#cc4444`) measures 4.48:1, just under WCAG AA's 4.5:1 for text this size (0.65rem bold does not qualify for the large-text allowance), while white measures 4.69:1. Rose and orange would both clear it too, but they are the SOUND and ADMIN pill colours and reusing either would blur what this pill means.

  **Review-round fix to the indicator itself:** it wrote to its `aria-live="polite"` region on every 100ms tick with no previous-value guard, so a screen reader would re-announce the offline count ten times a second — during precisely the incident the indicator exists to surface, and to precisely the user who most depends on it. `updatePill()` directly above gates on its previous value for the same reason; the new code had not followed that sibling pattern. Now guarded, with the sentinel seeded to `null` rather than `0` so a genuine first reading of zero still initialises the element. Verified behaviourally — 30 unchanged ticks produce zero writes, a real transition announces exactly once, and a negative control without the guard produces 30 writes, proving the probe detects the defect rather than always passing.

  **The indicator is ALWAYS VISIBLE, not hidden until alert.** The first cut hid it at zero, which the UX pass identified as a pattern inversion: the pre-XACA-0963 candy pill was always visible with a live count and only escalated above zero. Hidden-until-alert means no at-a-glance "monitoring is live" confirmation, and no way to discover the control before it fires. It now rests green with a live count (black text, 17.59:1) and escalates to red with white text plus a slow pulse above zero (4.69:1). Green and red carry the semantics; the count is always readable.

  **Final round: the status pill is excluded from the generic `.active` treatments.** Two findings, one root cause. `.legend-pill.offline-pill` and `.legend-pill.active` tie at specificity (0,3,0), so the status colour survived only because the offline rule sat later in the file — one reorder from silently regressing. Worse, `.legend-pill.active:focus-visible` genuinely *did* out-weigh it and flipped the focus ring to cyan when the pill was simultaneously the active section and keyboard-focused: 1.38:1 on green and 2.78:1 on red, both under the 3:1 minimum. Both rules now say what is actually meant — `:not(.offline-pill)` — since the status colour encodes fleet state, not which section is being viewed. Verified by resolving the cascade computationally across all four states (resting/alert × focused/active+focused), with a negative control against the pre-fix file confirming it reported the cyan override. A stale comment directly contradicting the one below it was also removed.

  Removing two sidebar buttons required **no stylesheet change at all**: XACA-0953 had just rebound the sidebar's terminating bar to `.sidebar-button + .sidebar-spacer`, and this is the first real exercise of that. Verified across all seven pages carrying a sidebar — each still draws exactly one terminator, correctly placed.

- **XACA-0954 — `team_transfer` exports silently omitted most of a team while reporting success.** Consumer-visible: an export produced by `kb-team-export` / the LCARS Export panel could carry a small fraction of the team it claimed to export, and print `Zero untagged gaps` while doing it. Measured on `medical-general`: **~286KB exported of a ~30MB team**; now 31,353,333 bytes. Three independent fail-open defects:
  - `share/lcars-ui/team_transfer/domain_devteam.py` resolved the product dir as `~/dev-team/<team>/` and **silently registered an empty block** when that path was absent. LCARS maps team slug → asset dir (`medical-general`→`medical`, `legal-coparenting`→`legal`, `dns`→`dns-framework`, `freelance-*`→`freelance`), so **11 of 17 shipped team configs pointed at a directory that does not exist** — 105MB of personas, avatars and terminal logos absent from every export made with them. New required `product_dir` config key; an unresolved root now reports a gap instead of exporting nothing quietly.
  - `share/lcars-ui/team_transfer/domain_knowledge.py` fell back to a hardcoded persona list when a config had no `personas:` block, and **none of the 17 shipped configs had one** — so every team exported the same wrong team's knowledge. Removed; `personas:` is now required in each config (absent = gap, `[]` = deliberate-empty). `~/knowledge-local` is now scanned as a second knowledge root — local-only team knowledge lived there by design and had never been exported at all.
  - A persona directory sitting *above* a team's configured root was invisible to every domain and **was not even reported as a gap**, because the gap check only counts files the scan reached. New optional `persona_dirs:` config key covers that layout.
  - `share/lcars-ui/team_transfer/generator.py` now prints a `MISSING DOMAIN ROOTS` section and **refuses the all-clear line whenever a configured root was never scanned**, exiting non-zero. `--allow-untagged` deliberately does **not** waive this — a missing root is a separate failure class with its own `--allow-missing-roots` opt-out — and `PRE_EXPORT_CHECKLIST.md` renders the warning above the first checkbox so it cannot be ticked past.

  **Round 2 — the LCARS Export Team button was still reporting success.** Consumer-visible: the CLI gate above does not protect the UI path. `share/lcars-ui/server.py`'s `generate_export()` passed `--allow-untagged`, gated only on `returncode == 2`, and set *"Export ready for download"* unconditionally — and since `--allow-untagged` deliberately does not waive a missing root, the generator exited 1 and the server treated that as warn-only. **An operator clicking Export Team saw success over an incomplete export**, the ticket's own symptom on its most-used surface. The job now reads `missing_roots` from the manifest, says `INCOMPLETE` in its message when any root went unscanned, and returns a `missingRootsSummary` beside the existing `verifierSummary`; a manifest that cannot be parsed is reported as unknown rather than assumed complete. Also: `--allow-missing-roots` no longer prints the unwaived "do not export" alarm at an operator who just waived (it now reads `INCOMPLETE BY WAIVER`, and still never reads as clean), the missing-root remediation names the config file to edit, and `product_dir: ""` is distinguished from an omitted key.

  **Round 3 — the Export Team panel still displayed READY.** Consumer-visible: round 2 made the backend emit `missingRootsSummary`, but nothing in `share/lcars-ui/js/lcars.js` read it — `onExportComplete()` set the status label to `READY` unconditionally, and the only trace of incompleteness reaching the page was the job message as plain text in a secondary line, after the phrase "Export ready for download". **An operator exporting a team with an unscanned root saw READY.** The panel now shows `INCOMPLETE` and renders each unscanned root with its domain, path and config key; the archive is still offered for download, because a partial export can be a deliberate choice, but the status never reads READY over one. `share/lcars-ui/index.html`'s `lcars.js` cache-buster is bumped so the change actually reaches a browser. `generate_export()`'s decision logic is also extracted into pure helpers and now fails **closed** on a manifest it cannot parse, rather than treating an unreadable manifest as "nothing missing".

  **Round 4 — the warning was rendering in the wrong place.** Consumer-visible: the incomplete-export warning had been appended *inside* the download panel, which is a no-wrap flex row, so it never dropped below the file metadata as intended — at narrow widths the DOWNLOAD button overlapped the filename text. It is now inserted **before** the panel (no stylesheet change, and the shared `.export-download` class used by the secrets panel is left untouched), gains `role="status"`/`aria-live="polite"` so a screen-reader user is actually told the export came back incomplete, uses the LCARS alert tokens instead of ad-hoc colours, and the panel's success-green chrome no longer stays green over an incomplete export.

  **Round 5 — a stale INCOMPLETE banner could survive into the next export.** Consumer-visible: moving the warning out of the download panel (round 4) also moved it out of that panel's lifecycle, so starting a new export did not clear it and a failed export never cleared it at all — the previous run's banner stayed on screen describing a run it was not about. A teardown now runs on both paths. The incomplete-state toning also extends to the filename and DOWNLOAD button, which previously stayed success-green inside a red-bordered panel.

  Operators who exported a team with one of the affected configs should re-run the export and re-verify: the old manifest is incomplete, and its `Zero untagged gaps` line was not evidence to the contrary.

- **XACA-0401 (round 4) — the regression guard for the 401 wildcard fix could not fail, and was not wired into the test runner.** No consumer-visible behaviour changes here; both items are about the guard protecting the round-3 fix. `fleet-monitor/server/tests/xaca-0401-cors-origin-pinning.test.js` targeted a route that does not exist, so Express 404'd before `sendUnauthorized()` ran and a soft early-return let the test report success without evaluating its assertion — it would have stayed green if the wildcard came back. It now targets the genuinely auth-gated `POST /api/status` and asserts its precondition rather than skipping on it, verified by reintroducing the wildcard and watching the guard fail. The file is also now listed in `package.json`'s `test` script alongside its `xaca-0395` sibling, so `npm test` actually runs it.
- **XACA-0401 (round 5) — two fleet-monitor test suites had been failing since this ticket's production fixes landed, and nothing caught it because no CI workflow runs them.** No consumer-visible behaviour change; production code was already correct in both cases and is untouched here. `tests/auth-middleware.test.js` still asserted a 401 carries `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, which round 3 deliberately removed; it now asserts the header is absent. `tests/xaca-0395-cors-allowed-headers.test.js` preflighted from a foreign Origin and expected `Access-Control-Allow-Headers` back, which the fail-closed `origin` callback correctly refuses — fixed by allow-listing that origin rather than relaxing the assertion, so the suite still proves what it was written to prove: that even an accepted origin is never told `Authorization` is permitted. `npm test` for `fleet-monitor/server` goes 351/355 → 355/355.

- **XACA-0948 — `kb-*` status displays and gates now resolve an absent/blank `.status` per `ITEM_STATUS_CONTRACT.md` §1.5 instead of a flat `"todo"`/`"unknown"`/`"backlog"` default.** Consumer-visible: `kb-backlog show/list/sub`, `kb-pick`, `kb-run`, `kb-work`, `kb-done`, `kb-epic show`, and the reopen/blocked-gate/item-box helpers in both `share/templates/kanban/kanban-helpers.template.sh` and `share/templates/aliases/kanban-aliases.sh` now report an unrecorded item as `in_progress` when it has evidence of active work (a live non-cancelled subitem, `activelyWorking`, or `startedAt`), and `todo` only when there is none — matching canonical's jq `_KB_ITEM_STATUS_JQ_DEFS` library. A recorded status (including non-canonical tokens and terminal states) always wins verbatim, and an unrecorded item can never resolve to a terminal state. Fixes a real bug in `kb-backlog demote`: its `todo)` case arm short-circuits with "already todo — nothing to do" and skips clearing `activelyWorking`/`workStartedAt`, so an unrecorded-but-actively-worked item previously kept its in-flight markers stuck open. Sites explicitly out of scope (activeWindows/release/epic-entity status, and blocker-gate equality checks whose result is invariant under any default) are left untouched and commented, matching canonical's own audit exclusions.

  Part of this port (the shared defs library and the `kb-done`/`kb-backlog` sites in `kanban-helpers.template.sh`) landed earlier in this same [Unreleased] window under the "XACA-0885 (mirrors only)" bullet further below (nested under the XACA-0953 entry), which mislabeled it as an XACA-0885 mirror — it is XACA-0948 content, unrelated to XACA-0885's relay-URL work. Recorded here for an accurate paper trail; that commit is left as-is per this repo's no-history-rewrite rule.

- **XACA-0401 (round 3) — a wildcard survived on the auth-rejected path, and the cache header that makes origin-pinning safe was missing from 53 of 55 call sites.** Consumer-visible:
  - `fleet-monitor/server/lib/auth-middleware.js` no longer answers **every 401** with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. That header overrode the fail-closed origin decision on every auth-rejected request, so a machine running Fleet Monitor handed a wildcard to any origin that asked, as long as the request was rejected. A wildcard on the auth-rejected path is the last place it belongs.
  - `share/lcars-ui/server.py` now sends `Vary: Origin` on **every** response path, not only when the origin is granted. The response for a given URL is origin-dependent either way, and these endpoints send `Cache-Control: no-cache` (revalidate) rather than `no-store`, so an origin-blind intermediary cache could otherwise store the header-less variant and replay it to a legitimate origin.
  - A CORS refusal now leaves **one** log line per `Host`/`Origin` pair naming what was refused and pointing at `AITEAMFORGE_LCARS_ALLOWED_HOSTS` — the same pointer `serve_auth_key()` already gave for the same check. Previously an operator saw only an opaque browser console error with no server-side signal. Warn-once and bounded: this path runs on ~53 endpoints on every response.
  - A userinfo-bearing Origin (`http://localhost:8203@evil.example`) is now refused. The old check reduced it to `localhost` via a first-colon split and echoed the whole origin back. Not reachable from a browser, so hardening rather than a live bypass — fixed anyway, because the function's job is validating hostile input.
  - `docs/LCARS-NETWORK-BINDING.md` gains a **Cross-Origin (CORS) Access** section documenting both `AITEAMFORGE_LCARS_ALLOWED_HOSTS` and `FLEET_MONITOR_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`, plus how to tell a CORS refusal (page loads, panels empty, console error) apart from a binding failure. Neither variable had been in any operator-facing doc before — only in a changelog.

- **XACA-0401 (round 2) — the CORS change could turn a working request into a 500, and the regression guard shipped last round was never going to run.** Consumer-visible: reading `self.headers` to derive the echoed origin made HEADER EMISSION a new way to fail a response. Two endpoints (`serve_calendar_items`, `handle_delete_epic`) returned **500 instead of 200** with `'LCARSHandler' object has no attribute 'headers'`. The line this replaced was a constant that touched no request state and could not fail. A response-header helper must never be the thing that fails the response — header derivation now degrades a missing or unusable headers mapping to "omit the header", the same fail-closed outcome as an untrusted origin, never a wildcard.

  Also removed `share/lcars-ui/tests/test_xaca0401_cors_origin.py`, mirrored here last round. The canonical file moved to the dev repo's `tests/` directory, which is not tap-mirrored, so the copy here was an orphan with no canonical counterpart. It moved because `pytest.ini` scopes the required CI gate to `tests/` — the suite's two source guards (which fail if `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` returns, or if `serve_auth_key()` gains an ACAO header) were sitting in a directory no gate executes. A guard that cannot fire is decoration; it is now collected and its negative control re-proved from the new location. Net effect for tap consumers: `share/lcars-ui/` no longer carries that test file, and the behaviour it guards is unchanged and enforced upstream.

- **XACA-0953 — the Fleet Monitor sidebar's terminating black bar was stranded on the wrong button, drawing a doubled 4px bar above ENGINES and none below it.** Consumer-visible: the LCARS sidebar stack visually failed to close at the bottom, and ENGINES sat 2px lower than its neighbours' spacing implied. The bar was bound to a *named* section that merely happened to be last when the rule was written; ENGINES was appended below it and the bar never moved, so the stale terminator's `border-bottom: 2px` stacked against ENGINES' own `border-top: 2px`.

  The stale target differed per skin: in `lcars/` (the skin `server.js` routes today) ADMIN owned it in CSS, and `lcars-dashboard-app.js` additionally re-drew it onto SETTINGS whenever ADMIN was hidden — that is, on every dashboard except ALL FLEET, painting the terminator *mid-stack*. In `lcars2/` SETTINGS owned it. Each stylesheet was wrong in three places, not one: the base colour rule, the 600px `border-bottom-width` override, and the 600px `+ .sidebar-spacer` adjacency rule — the last of which had never matched anything at all, since ENGINES sits between the stale target and the spacer.

  Ownership now sits on `[data-section="engines"]`, and the JS transfer is deleted rather than retargeted (ENGINES is always rendered — verified in `app.js` and both `lcars-engines.js` copies — so no runtime transfer is needed, and retargeting would have reproduced the same name-binding fragility in a second place). A structural `:last-of-type` selector was considered and rejected: it matches by element type, and the last `div` sibling in the nav is `.sidebar-spacer`, not a button.

  In `lcars2/` the border is declared beside the other sidebar borders rather than next to the engines *colour* in SECTION 18. That placement is load-bearing: SECTION 18 sits after the `@media (max-width: 600px)` block, both rules carry identical specificity, and source order therefore decides — a `border-bottom` shorthand there would have overridden the thin 1px mobile bar and shipped a too-thick bar on narrow viewports.

  No effect on the per-team LCARS cockpit, which shares this stylesheet by symlink: its sidebar uses different section names, and `[data-section="engines"]` is an exact-match attribute selector that does not match its `rag-engines`.

  **Follow-up in the same change: the terminator no longer names a section at all.** Rebinding the bar from one hard-coded `[data-section=...]` to another fixed the symptom but kept the fragility — append a button below ENGINES and it breaks again, in three places across two stylesheets. The bar is now drawn by the adjacent spacer instead: `.sidebar-button + .sidebar-spacer { border-top: 2px solid var(--lcars-black) }`. The spacer always follows whatever the last button is, so appending a button requires **no stylesheet change**. Plain CSS2 adjacency — no `:has()`, so no browser-support question in the iTerm2 WKWebView the cockpit runs in.

  This also deleted a scoping hack this ticket had itself introduced (a `body.lcars-scratch` class added so the two `lcars-test.html` scratch pages, which have no ENGINES button, could close their stack) — adjacency covers them with no marker class and no third section-name binding. Verified by walking all seven pages that carry a sidebar: each draws exactly one terminating bar, in the right place, and v1's `refresh-btn` — which follows a spacer rather than preceding one — correctly does not trigger the rule.

  At ≤600px the spacer now collapses to its bar (`flex: 0 0 auto; min-height: 0; border-top-width: 1px`) rather than `display: none`, which would have taken the terminator down with it.

- **XACA-0885 (mirror only) — `share/scripts/kb-msg-provision`** is mirrored here to match a canonical edit that ships in the same dev-team PR as XACA-0953 above (relay-URL resolution and persistence; an empty kanban skeleton no longer counts as a local team).

  **Correction to this bullet's original wording.** It also claimed `share/templates/kanban/kanban-helpers.template.sh` was mirrored for XACA-0885. That was false in both directions: the template carries no kb-msg content at all (before or after), so there was nothing of XACA-0885's to mirror into it — and the template change that commit `3c24a0d` actually carried was XACA-0948's `ITEM_STATUS_CONTRACT` logic, swept in from another session's uncommitted edit to this shared tree. That content is now properly owned by XACA-0948's own commit `349b496`, and the XACA-0948 author has annotated it above. Left in place rather than reverted, since reverting would undo their legitimate work; only this claim is corrected.

  Recorded because the failure mode is worth naming: the mirror was verified for one file (`kb-msg-provision`, genuinely in sync) and *assumed* for its neighbour. `sync-tap.sh --check` reports 0 drift here regardless, because this template is a hand-maintained port target rather than a `sync_file` mirror — a raw drift check cannot flag semantically wrong content in a file that is a deliberate subset of canonical. A claimed mirror needs `git show <inner-sha> -- <file>` read against the expected content; the gate's colour is not evidence.

  **Review/UX follow-ups addressed in the same change.** The scratch pages (`lcars-test.html`, both skins) have no ENGINES button, so the terminator above would never apply and their sidebar stack never closed — the same defect, found by sweeping every page with a sidebar rather than only the four dashboards. Both now carry `body.lcars-scratch`, and a page-scoped rule gives their last button (SETTINGS) the terminating bar without affecting the real dashboards, where SETTINGS sits mid-stack. A warning comment now sits at the ENGINES button in all five dashboard files, at the edit site where the next contributor would reintroduce this, stating that appending a new last button means moving the bar in three places per stylesheet.

- **XACA-0401 — LCARS and fleet-monitor no longer answer every request with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`.** Consumer-visible: any page in the operator's browser could previously read the response body of every LCARS API call, because a wildcard ACAO is exactly the grant that authorizes a foreign origin to do so. `share/lcars-ui/server.py`'s 54 wildcard sites now route through a single resolver that echoes the caller's own origin, and only after **both** the request's `Host` and the `Origin`'s host independently pass `_host_is_local_identity()` — the same anti-DNS-rebinding allowlist `GET /api/auth-key` already used. Rejection omits the header entirely; there is no path that falls back to `*`. Responses carrying ACAO now also carry `Vary: Origin`.

  Cross-**port** localhost is deliberately still allowed, because `redirect.html` and `agent-panel-router.html` fetch `/api/status` on the API port from a page served on a different port — the stricter "Origin must equal Host" rule would have silently broken both. Cross-**host** is refused, so `evil.com` resolved to 127.0.0.1 (where Origin and Host match each other but name neither this machine nor this server) still gets nothing.

  `do_OPTIONS` is now origin-conditional: a refused caller still gets a 200, but no `Allow-Origin` and no `Allow-Methods` — the preflight no longer advertises `POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE` to an origin the server is about to turn away. `Allow-Headers` stays pinned to `Content-Type`; it was NOT widened (XACA-0395-007 reverted that for a documented reason).

  `serve_auth_key()` is untouched — its *absent* ACAO is load-bearing, and `server.py` carries a comment predicting a future maintainer would re-add `*` "to fix CORS". A source guard now fails the build if that happens, and it asserts on the header emission rather than the substring, since that handler's docstring discusses the header at length precisely to explain its absence.

  `fleet-monitor/server/server.js`'s `cors()` had no `origin` option, so it reflected whatever the browser sent. Now a fail-closed callback with a `FLEET_MONITOR_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` escape hatch. Verified rather than assumed that nothing legitimate breaks: fleet-monitor's own pages make 96 `fetch()` calls and none uses an absolute URL, no page anywhere calls it cross-origin, and every `Authorization` sender is a CLI — curl and Node never preflight.

- **XACA-0397 (final five) — closes the last five protected review/test findings on the LCARS bind-control feature.** Consumer-visible: `LCARS_BIND_MODE=all` now prints a `[LCARS][bind] posture: mode=all listening=0.0.0.0 (all interfaces)` line to stderr like every other mode, so the widest and most dangerous posture is no longer the one exception to "every mode is greppable in the log." `docs/LCARS-NETWORK-BINDING.md`'s posture-verification step now reads the real log filename the launcher actually writes (`lcars-server-<team>.log`, not `lcars-<team>.log`). Non-behavioural: two test-rigor gaps closed via mutation testing — `TestAggregateDetectionDeadline`'s deadline test now tracks and asserts the CLI phase's subprocess-call count independently of the interface-scan phase's (a combined `< 6` bound could not detect a regression confined to one phase — verified: shipped code makes 2 combined calls, a CLI-phase-only regression still makes only 4, both under 6); `TestServeForeverPartialBindCleanup`'s real-socket test now spies on `LCARSServer.server_close()` directly instead of relying on a port-rebind probe that CPython's GC can satisfy on its own once the SystemExit severs the traceback (verified: removing the `finally: srv.server_close()` cleanup left the port-rebind probe green, but the new spy assertion correctly fails).

- **XACA-0397 — closes seven protected review/test findings on the LCARS bind-control feature in `share/lcars-ui/server.py`: an unbounded Tailscale-detection timeout, a duplicated candidate-binary list, interface-scan observability, an empty-list bind guard, a dangling doc reference, and new unit-test coverage.** Consumer-visible behaviour changes:
  - **Tailscale detection is now capped at a shared ~8s aggregate budget** across the CLI-candidate phase and the interface-scan fallback combined, instead of each subprocess call getting its own independent budget (worst case was previously ~30s of detection before the server even attempted to bind). Measured empirically against a genuinely hanging subprocess: 8.005s elapsed. On a machine with missing or hung Tailscale binaries, the dashboard now starts (in `auto` mode, narrowed to loopback) or fails fast (in `tailscale` mode) well within the launcher's 15s readiness window instead of risking the launcher declaring a healthy server dead.
  - **Interface-scan fallback now logs which network interface supplied a candidate Tailscale address** (to stderr, alongside the address itself), rather than silently trusting any interface with a CGNAT (100.64.0.0/10) address. Scoping the scan to a specific interface name (e.g. macOS's `utun*`) was considered and rejected — measured 12 simultaneously-up `utun` interfaces on the machine this was verified on, most unrelated to Tailscale, so a name-based filter would not have reliably distinguished it, and Linux's Tailscale interface is typically named `tailscale0` instead.
  - New `docs/LCARS-NETWORK-BINDING.md` — the four `LCARS_BIND_MODE` values, defaults, fail-closed behaviour, the `all` opt-in, and cross-machine dashboard access over Tailscale. `docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md`'s LCARS networking section no longer tells operators to hand-edit `server.py` to force `0.0.0.0`.
  - Non-behavioural: consolidated a duplicated Tailscale-binary candidate list; `_lcars_serve_forever_on([])` now fails with a clear message instead of an unreachable `IndexError`; removed a comment citing a doc path that does not exist in this repo. 8 new tests added to `share/lcars-ui/tests/test_xaca0161_bind_control.py` (36 → 44 passing).

- **XACA-0397 (follow-up) — relocates the bind-control block in `share/lcars-ui/server.py` out of a test's byte-slice span, plus two `[UX]` fixes.** The block mirrored just above (`resolve_bind_addresses_or_die()`/`LCARS_BIND_MODE`) had been inserted between `class LCARSServer` and `def main():` — the exact span `tests/test_xaca0889_mixin_allowlist.py` byte-slices and `exec()`s in an isolated namespace to test `LCARSQuietDisconnectMixin` — and its module-level `_LCARS_TAILSCALE_CGNAT_NET = ipaddress.ip_network(...)` call broke that test with `NameError: name 'ipaddress' is not defined`, erroring all 10 of its cases on the canonical side. Relocated the whole block to sit ahead of `LCARSQuietDisconnectMixin`/`LCARSServer`, outside the slice — verified behavior-preserving (`auto`/`loopback` mode resolution and `all` without the allow-flag still exiting non-zero, checked directly against the imported module). Also: the bind-failure FATAL in `_lcars_serve_forever_on()` now names a concrete next step (`lsof -nP -iTCP:<port> -sTCP:LISTEN` to find what already holds the port) instead of stopping cold, matching every other FATAL in this file; the three `posture:` info lines in `resolve_bind_addresses_or_die()` now print to stderr, matching every other WARNING/FATAL/NOTICE in the same function (they were the only lines going to stdout).

- **XACA-0951 — Project Planner can no longer emit a retroactive plan document that silently implies untracked work was tracked.** A plan doc written for an item that is already closed was indistinguishable from a real forward-looking one, so its subitems table read as a record of work that happened. It was not: those rows exist only as prose no workflow reads, and `kb-sweep` structurally cannot catch it, because such an item has ZERO board subitems and the protected-subitem gate has nothing to gate on. Nothing failed and nobody bypassed anything — which is why it went unnoticed for seven months.

  Measured on the worked case, `XFIR-0039` "Security Rules Audit" (closed 2026-01-15, plan doc written 2026-01-29): the doc's table carries seven rows whose status column reads `todo`. It does not merely imply tracked work — it displays **seven open todos on a closed item**, one of them a real least-privilege security decision that sat unactioned.

  **Refusing to emit the table was considered and deliberately REJECTED.** These documents exist to preserve a reconstruction that is often the only surviving record of a decision; refusing would delete the information rather than correct it. The section now requires a visible banner plus a machine-readable sentinel, and the subitems heading is retitled so it cannot be read as tracked work. The rationale is written into the skill as an anti-reversal record, because the failure mode here is a future editor removing the banner as clutter.

  **The sentinel fails OPEN, by design.** A malformed, partial, reordered or wrong-item marker does NOT whitelist a document — it stays flagged. A whitelist that failed closed would silently hide the exact defect this change exists to surface. The skill states this invariant explicitly and notes that only trailing whitespace is a safe relaxation. The skill also forbids striking or decorating the subitem ids (`~~XFIR-0039-001~~`): decoration breaks the detection regex, so the document stops being detected at all, and an undetected document is never banner-checked either. That is the edit most likely to be made in good faith as extra caution, so the warning sits at the point of temptation.

  **Gate 2 review round (PR #760).** Two blockers fixed, both the same defect one layer up from the one this ticket is about. (1) `share/lcars-ui/js/lcars-artifact-audit.js` headlined the audit banner off `data.violations.length` alone, while the audit now reports two finding kinds and sets `clean=false` when *either* is non-empty — so a run with 7 real findings would have rendered **"ARTIFACT AUDIT — 0 VIOLATIONS"** above an empty list (the list loop also read `.path`, which the new record shape does not carry). Both finding kinds are now normalised into one display list, with a comment at the merge point stating that any future finding kind must be added there or it inherits the same silence. (2) The "Detection contract" section claimed the guard "also flags a historical sentinel present on a now-live item". It does not — verified for `in_progress`, absent-status, and terminal-with-subitems. Rather than delete the sentence, the section now documents the scope limit explicitly and closes with: do not read a clean audit as evidence that no stale banners exist. A contract asserting coverage it lacks is this ticket's own defect, and quietly removing the claim would have left the next reader assuming the same coverage.

  Status detection deliberately does NOT use `kb-backlog show <ID> | grep -q 'Status: COMPLETED'`. That command resolves only against the current terminal's board — run against a cross-team id it errors and exits 1, and the pipeline reports grep's status rather than the command's, so the failure is invisible and the check concludes "not completed" for every cross-team item, omitting the banner in precisely the case that needs it. The skill ships a prefix-resolving snippet instead.

- **XACA-0297 (mirror catch-up) — the "Wiring is mandatory" guidance reaches the tap skill copy for the first time.** PR #741 added it to the canonical `skills/Project Planner/SKILL.md` and never hand-ported it here, so this copy sat 13 lines behind from that merge until now. Consumers' Project Planner has therefore been emitting plan documents with no Wiring section and no split-implementation-from-wiring guidance — the exact omission XACA-0296's retrospective identified, where two subagents wrote fully correct code that nothing ever called. No behaviour is authored here: this is the canonical text arriving unchanged, carried in the same pass as XACA-0951 above because both live in this file.

  **Why it survived:** `skills/` is not a `sync-tap.sh` surface at all — there is no `sync_file` mapping, `kb-edit-shared` refuses these paths as "not in the mirrored surface", and `sync-tap-check.yml`'s covered-sections list does not include `skills/**`. This copy is a hand-maintained port target (like `share/templates/kanban/kanban-helpers.template.sh`), so nothing structurally noticed the divergence. Recorded here rather than fixed silently, because the same gap can reopen the moment someone patches the canonical skill and forgets this file.

- **XACA-0397 — `share/lcars-ui/server.py` no longer binds all interfaces by default.** Mirrors the fail-closed bind-control implementation from the canonical `lcars-ui/server.py` (built during XACA-0161, deferred there, shipped standalone on XACA-0397): `resolve_bind_addresses_or_die()` replaces the historical `("", port)` bind-all-interfaces default with `LCARS_BIND_MODE` (`auto` default: loopback + host Tailscale IPv4; `loopback`; `tailscale`, strict — refuses if no tailnet address; `all`, opt-in only via `LCARS_BIND_ALLOW_ALL_INTERFACES=1` and prints a loud banner). **This matters to a consumer directly:** before this change, every Homebrew-installed LCARS instance was reachable from anyone else on the same LAN with no credential prompt — a shared office network, a coffee-shop hotspot, a hotel network. After this change a fresh install defaults to loopback-plus-tailnet only; a consumer who genuinely wants LAN-wide reach has to opt in explicitly and gets warned loudly when they do. `_lcars_serve_forever_on()` opens one listening socket per resolved address, since `auto`/`tailscale` modes need two and a single socket can't cover both without reintroducing the exposure. An unresolvable required address exits non-zero rather than silently falling back to `0.0.0.0` — fail-closed, not fail-open. Existing API-key auth, Host allowlist, Origin check, and DNS-rebinding guard are unchanged; this adds a network-layer control beneath them. New `share/lcars-ui/tests/test_xaca0161_bind_control.py` (36 tests, 29 subtests) mirrors the canonical coverage of mode resolution and fail-closed paths; the filename retains the `xaca0161` stem because that is where the work was originated and verified. Fleet-monitor is explicitly out of scope — it is a Fly.io-hosted Express app where binding all interfaces is correct and required, and its own exposure is tracked separately as XACA-0398.

- **XACA-0885 (follow-up) — re-mirror `register-claude-hook.py` after the canonical gained type guards.** The first mirror shipped the version whose `_commands_for_event` read `(data.get("hooks") or {})`, which only rescues a FALSY wrong type: a consumer whose `settings.json` held `{"hooks": ["x"]}` got a raw `AttributeError` traceback at exit 1 on **every session start**, instead of the clean exit-2 refusal. Caught by the PR's own `sync-tap-drift` gate — the canonical was fixed mid-review and the tap side was not re-synced, which is exactly the drift that gate exists to catch.

- **XACA-0885 — the kb-msg comms channel is shipped to consumers with a setup path for the first time.** Every kb-msg artifact was already mirrored here; none of the provisioning was, so a consumer box could receive the feature and had no way to turn it on. Adds `share/scripts/kb-msg-provision` (vault keypair + `team-machines.json` routing map, with `--check`/`--dry-run`) and `share/scripts/register-claude-hook.py` (idempotent `settings.json` hook registration that refuses an unparseable file rather than rebuilding it). Both are installed **executable** by `install-shell.sh`, unlike the `msg-client.js`/`vault-keygen.js` datafiles beside them — these two are invoked directly, by an operator and by `kb-msg doctor` respectively.

  `share/scripts/fleet-reporter.sh` is re-mirrored: its kb-msg pull had six silent `return 0` guards and now records why it skipped to `~/.aiteamforge/run/kb-msg-pull-status`, including on the success path so a stale reason cannot outlive its cause. Nothing is printed on the normal path — this runs every reporter cycle.

  **Consumer-layout note:** the tap flattens everything into `scripts/`, while dev keeps hooks under `claude-hooks/`. The canonical `kb-msg doctor` searches both, because a `claude-hooks/`-only lookup resolves on a dev box and nowhere else — it would have reported "cannot check" forever on precisely the machines that most need it.

- **XACA-0897 — Guard 5 round 20: fail-closed fixes in the release-cut version gate.** `kb-release-version-gate`'s board-resolved CR-divergence check squashed jq's exit code on two reads, so an unparseable board or manifest collapsed into the same empty string as "no CRs linked" and the gate was skipped entirely — while the identical corrupt file already halted on the sibling code path. Both reads now capture their own rc and halt with exit 3 on a genuine parse failure, so "I could not read it" is never representable as "there is nothing there". Also adds a `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]+$` allowlist on `--release-id`/`--platform` before they are spliced into the override-sentinel path. `kb-release-push-promote --dry-run` no longer forces a passing gate result without running the gate — it now runs `kb-release-version-gate` for real, so a dry-run preview can no longer report a pass it never computed; the auto-bump retry gains an explicit dry-run guard so it still never writes. **This introduced its own regression** — the gate itself had no concept of dry-run, so that real invocation also ran Step 0's CR-divergence check unmodified, including its unconditional consumption of a pending single-use human override sentinel on every preview. See the round 21 entry immediately below for the fix.

- **XACA-0897 — Guard 5 round 21: `kb-release-version-gate` gains a real `--dry-run` mode; charset allowlist now permits `.`.** Two follow-ups to round 20 above.

  **BLOCKING regression fix.** Round 20 made `kb-release-push-promote --dry-run` invoke `kb-release-version-gate` for real (see above), but the gate had no dry-run concept of its own — Step 0's `_cr_div_check_override_sentinel` unconditionally `rm -f`'d a matching override sentinel on every invocation, dry-run or not. A mere preview therefore silently burned a human's single-use CR-divergence authorization: a grant that requires a live TTY and refuses outright in any detected AI-agent shell, i.e. one an agent structurally cannot recreate once consumed. `kb-release-version-gate` now accepts its own `--dry-run` (forwarded automatically by `kb-release-push-promote --dry-run`): it still genuinely evaluates the divergence and reports the correct verdict (0/1/3, same as a real run — it does **not** revert to skipping the check), but makes no writes anywhere. The override sentinel is peeked, never consumed, under `--dry-run`; the best-effort `gateStatus` POST to LCARS (`_write_gate_status`, reachable from every one of Steps 0-4's exit paths) is skipped entirely rather than just non-fatal. Combining `--dry-run` with `--override-cr-divergence` is refused with an explicit warning rather than silently accepted or silently dropped, since recording an override is itself a write. The single-use property for real (non-dry-run) runs is completely unaffected.

  **Charset allowlist fix (follow-up to round 20's `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]+$` guard).** That allowlist rejected `.` outright, which is over-broad for a release/platform id (not a team name) — a dotted id is not itself a path-traversal vector; `..` is the actual concern. `.` is now permitted; `..` anywhere in the value, and a leading `.`, remain rejected. A rejection is now also reported explicitly on stderr rather than folding silently into a generic "no override found" / "could not resolve releases dir" outcome, so a human hitting a rejected id sees why the `--override-cr-divergence` escape hatch is unavailable instead of it just silently not working.

- **XACA-0897 — Guard 5 round 22: closes the override-sentinel TOCTOU race, verifies consumption, and three follow-ups.** `kb-release-version-gate`'s single-use CR-divergence override sentinel had zero locking primitives: it read the sentinel, matched it, printed, and only then deleted it, as four separate unguarded steps. QA reproduced this live against a fresh sentinel — 6, 8, and 7 of 8 concurrent real (non-dry-run) invocations all reported `OVERRIDE HONORED` across three trials, so N concurrent release cuts could pass on one human's single-use grant.

  **BLOCKING fix — atomic claim, not atomic delete.** A real run now first `mv`s the sentinel to a per-process-unique name before reading its content at all; `mv`/`rename(2)` is atomic within a filesystem, so of any number of concurrent racers exactly one succeeds and every loser's `mv` fails immediately (source already gone), returning "no override" with no window in which more than one process can see a match. Validation now happens *after* the claim (order flipped from round 21): a claimed sentinel that does not match this exact divergence is renamed back to its original path unchanged, preserving the pre-existing "a mismatch is not consumed" property. `--dry-run` is unaffected — it still only peeks, never claims, never renames, exactly as round 21 established.

  **XACA-0897-119 — consumption is now verified, not attempted.** The old `rm -f ... || true` reported "consumed (single-use)" and returned success even when the unlink silently failed (read-only/immutable/permission denial) — a second, independent route to two real passes off one grant. The delete is now checked; on failure the claim is restored to its original path so the grant is not silently voided, a hard error is printed, and the run falls through to the ordinary fail-closed halt — never a false pass. Disclosed residual: a process killed strictly between the claim-rename and the verified-delete leaves an orphaned `.claimed.<pid>.<rand>` file; every later run then sees "no sentinel" and fails closed (the same safe direction this script already treats absence-as-no-permission everywhere else), losing the grant rather than double-spending it. No automatic reclaim of that file is implemented — a heuristic time-based "presume abandoned" sweep cannot be guaranteed race-free against a merely-slow (not dead) claimant, so this is stated rather than papered over.

  **XACA-0897-116 — `--help` no longer truncates the `--dry-run` documentation.** `grep '^#' | head -20` cut the header comment block at a fixed line count that landed mid-sentence through the `--dry-run` paragraph (added after that budget was set), so the "no writes / peek not consume" contract was invisible to `--help`. Now captures the whole leading header-comment block (from the top of the file to the first non-`#` line) instead of a hardcoded count, so it tracks the header growing instead of moving the cliff.

  **XACA-0897-117 — the charset-rejection warning no longer fires on every ordinary divergence.** `_cr_div_check_override_sentinel` calls the sentinel-path resolver unconditionally on every divergence (to check for a pre-existing override), so a rejected `--release-id`/`--platform` shouted the round-21 loud warning even when nobody was anywhere near `--override-cr-divergence`.
