The warning is now scoped: silent when no override was requested this run, loud (unchanged from round 21) when one genuinely was.

  **XACA-0897-118 — the dry-run summary no longer contradicts its own peek message.** Under `--dry-run`, the Step 0 caller printed "proceeding on a consumed human override" even though nothing was consumed, directly contradicting the peek branch's own "NOT being consumed" message two lines earlier. The dry-run path now says a pre-recorded override would be honored by a real run and was **not** consumed by this preview; the real-run path keeps the original wording.

- fix: XACA-0883-036 — port the XACA-0883-033 dead-code removal into both helper copies. Canonical removed an unreachable singular/plural branch in `_kb_knowledge_layout_guard_report_t3` whose singular form was self-contradictory ("1 distinct kanban directory **is** claimed by N registry entries" — one value is not a conflict at all), but both copies here still carried `_kd_noun`/`_kd_verb`. Behaviourally inert (verified: the reporter's output is byte-identical across canonical and both copies against a real 2-distinct-value fixture, `rc=2`, empty stdout), so this is a divergence rather than a live defect — but the do-not-reintroduce marker was missing from exactly the copies where someone might reintroduce it. Worth recording how it slipped past: the co-change gate asks whether a tap copy ALSO changed in the PR, not whether it changed EQUIVALENTLY, and these copies had changed for an unrelated reason (the `--force` help line), so the check was satisfied while the divergence stood.

- **XACA-0819 — synced this template's `kb-pause`/`kb-resume` SUBITEM span handling to canonical XACA-0551, and ported the `_kb_flush_work_time` helper it depends on. The parent-item arms are deliberately left at their prior behaviour.** `share/templates/kanban/kanban-helpers.template.sh` is a hand-maintained **port target**, not a `sync_file` mirror — it is tracked only in this inner tap repo and `sync-tap.sh` never references it, so there is no canonical copy of this file to patch first (the XACA-0340 canonical-source rule does not apply, unlike the mirrored `share/lcars-ui/` and `share/scripts/` surfaces).

  **The originating ticket named `kb-resume` only; implementing that literally would have been a data-loss regression.** Both halves of the pair were missing: `kb-pause` did not END the active span (no flush into `timeWorkedMs`, no `del(.workStartedAt)`) and `kb-resume`'s no-arg toggle did not RESTART it. That combination is internally consistent — the clock starts at `kb-pick` and never stops — so paused wall-time was over-credited as work, but nothing was lost. Syncing resume alone inverts that: pause would still bank nothing, and the synced resume would overwrite `workStartedAt`, discarding the pre-pause span. Verified by building the resume-only counterfactual from the same rendered template (`zsh -n` clean) and running it: `timeWorkedMs` stayed at its seed through both operations while `workStartedAt` was overwritten. Pause and resume are one atomic change; landing half is a regression, not a partial improvement.

  **SUBITEM ONLY — a deliberate divergence from canonical, added on PR #756 review (XACA-0819-014).** An earlier revision of this change applied the flush/restart to items AND subitems, matching canonical. That was wrong *for this template*, and the review caught it. This file's item level has **no counterpart flush anywhere**: `kb-done`, `kb-cancel`, `kb-stop-working` and `kb-backlog unpick` all delete an item's `workStartedAt` without banking it (canonical flushes at all four; this template flushes at none). Writing item-level `timeWorkedMs` on pause alone therefore produces a total that is permanently missing its final span — and `kb-variance` (`share/templates/aliases/kanban-aliases.sh`) selects completed items on `timeWorkedMs > 0`, so those items would have silently graduated out of the honest `no_time` bucket and into estimate-accuracy analytics carrying a systematically LOW actual. **An absent number is better than a plausible wrong one**, so the item arms of both functions now behave exactly as they did before this ticket, and only the subitem arms converge on canonical. Subitems have the 4 counterpart flushes canonical assumes, so the span is fully accounted there.

  Measured after the change (both arms, one fixture, seeded `timeWorkedMs=5000000` and `workStartedAt=09:00:00Z`): the **item** arm is untouched by pause and resume — `workStartedAt` survives, `timeWorkedMs` stays 5,000,000, `startedAt` preserved. The **subitem** arm banks on pause (`workStartedAt` absent, `timeWorkedMs` 5,000,000 → 39,542,000), opens a fresh span on resume, holds `timeWorkedMs` unchanged across the resume, and never overwrites `startedAt`.

  **Do not "finish the job" by porting flush into those four item-level verbs without re-deriving their semantics first.** Canonical's `unpick` flush is on record inflating `timeWorkedMs` to ~12.2 **billion** ms from a single stale `workStartedAt` (XACA-0884, outer CHANGELOG). The anti-regression comment in `kb-pause` and the matching one in `kb-resume` state the pairing requirement explicitly, and Coverage 1 of the new suite fails if an item-level write is reintroduced without them.

  `_kb_flush_work_time` was **absent from this template entirely** and was ported first, byte-identical to canonical, including the zsh-only `${${work_started_at%\.[0-9]*}%Z}` expansion — deliberately NOT rewritten into bash syntax, since this file is `#!/bin/zsh` and already carries 4 inline copies of the identical logic. It has exactly ONE call site, in `kb-pause`'s subitem branch.

  **The XACA-0884/XACA-0552 demote inversion is preserved, and the comment defending it was corrected rather than left stale.** That comment partly justified the freeze on this template having no `_kb_flush_work_time` "at all" — a premise this change falsifies. The freeze is now load-bearing rather than trivially satisfied, and the comment says so while keeping canonical's closing directive and the ~4-month phantom-work precedent. Verified against a sandboxed fixture with `workStartedAt` stale since 2026-03-31: `kb-backlog demote` discarded the open span and left `timeWorkedMs` frozen at exactly 5,000,000, where a naive flush would have booked ~142 days ≈ 12,268,800,000 ms.

  **Platform note.** `_kb_flush_work_time` parses with `date -j -f`, which is BSD/macOS-only — this template has 10 such sites and 0 GNU `date -d` fallbacks, so on a GNU-coreutils host the parse fails, `|| echo "0"` fires, and elapsed computes as zero. The suite therefore asserts platform-invariant structural properties rather than elapsed magnitude. **Correction (PR #756 review):** an earlier draft of this entry, the commit message, and the suite header all claimed "tap CI runs `ubuntu-latest`". It does not — the manifest-driven plain-shell suites run in `test-shell-homebrew-tap` on **macos-latest** in this repo's own `.github/workflows/tests.yml`; the ubuntu-latest job that claim was read from is dev-team's `tap-installer-tests.yml`, which runs installer tests, not these suites. The portable assertions are kept regardless — they cost nothing on macOS and stay correct for Linux consumers — but the premise was inferred from code instead of checked against the workflow, which is exactly the error class this ticket is about.

  Also from PR #756 review: `kb-pause`'s `local reason="$1"` is now `"${1-}"`, matching canonical and closing the same `set -u` class fixed one commit earlier in this file (XACA-0884-013); and `share/**` was added to this workflow's push and pull_request path filters, without which a template-only change triggers no suites at all.

  New `tests/test-xaca-0819-pause-resume-active-span.sh` covers the subitem cycle, the item arm as an explicit regression guard, the static call-site invariants, and the demote freeze — registered in `tests/ci-manifest` as `plain-shell`, which `tests/ci-manifest-check.sh` requires (new suites are not auto-discovered). It carries the explicit pass-counter pattern from `test-xaca-0788-crash-recovery-in-tap.sh`, because tap `assert_*` helpers record only on FAILURE and a zero-assertion suite would print success vacuously, and it is validated by mutation: stripping the subitem flush, stripping the `//=` default, or re-adding an item-level write each make it exit 1. Regression sweep across the 9 pre-existing suites that read this template: 148 assertions, 0 failures.

- **XACA-0883 (`knowledge-helper-parity` / `knowledge-region-parity` / `cochange` gates) — ported the container-layout kanban-dir fix for `_kb_knowledge_project_path` into both hand-maintained tap copies.** Canonical's bug: `_kb_knowledge_project_path`'s case-2 template expansion (`{repo_root}/kanban/knowledge/project`) silently assumed `repo_root == container`, which is false for container-layout teams (iOS/Android/Firebase/DNS, where the git work-tree is a CHILD of the container and the real `kanban/` is a SIBLING of it) — writes landed in a SHADOW `kanban/` under the work-tree instead of the canonical one, invisible to `kb-knowledge-search`. Both tap copies (`share/templates/kanban/kanban-helpers.template.sh`, `share/templates/aliases/kanban-aliases.sh`) carried their own separately-ported, pre-fix copies of this resolver (XACA-0743/XACA-0770) with the identical defect.

  **New functions ported, verbatim, into both files**: `_kb_canonical_kanban_dir_for_repo` (registry reverse-lookup + bounded sibling probe, exit code 1 = no answer / 2 = ambiguous same-depth registry disagreement — see its header for the fail-open vs. fail-closed contract), `_kb_knowledge_layout_guard_report_t3` (names the disagreeing registry candidates for the T3 refusal), `_kb_knowledge_project_layout_guard` (T1 structural/gating redirect, T2 advisory-only branch-slug heuristic, T3 gating refusal — interactive prompt vs. non-interactive redirect-and-report, per the guard contract), and `_kb_knowledge_project_path_legacy` (transitional reader-only pre-fix resolution, so pre-existing shadow-base entries — possibly another team's live base — stay findable by search without this fix reconciling or moving them).

  **Existing functions updated, verbatim except comment reframing** (dev-machine-specific measurements, org-specific team-id examples, and this ticket's internal design-brief references were generalized per this file's standing editorial rule of code-identical/comments-reframed): `_kb_knowledge_project_path` (case-1 now probes both the work-tree and, for container-layout teams, the container config location; case-2 gains a `{kanban_dir}` token plus layout re-rooting of the legacy `{repo_root}/kanban` form, on a path-segment boundary, fail-open when the canonical dir can't be resolved with confidence), `_kb_knowledge_project_display_name` (container layout now yields the owning project's name, not the work-tree's basename), `kb-knowledge-add` (new `--allow-nonstandard-base` flag, independent of `--force` in both directions; the layout guard call in the bare-project branch runs strictly AFTER the PII guard — reordering would open a bypass, since redirecting the path out from under the global root would stop the PII prefix test from matching), `kb-knowledge-promote` (same flag, refused outright on a non-project target tier; dry-run forces the guard's non-interactive branch so a preview never blocks on stdin, and discloses when a real `--confirm` run's interactive prompt could still diverge from the previewed target), and `kb-knowledge-search` (a transitional reader-only union that also searches the legacy base when it differs from, and is non-empty relative to, the canonical one).

  **Not ported**: `_kb_knowledge_project_path_effective`'s code is unchanged upstream (comment-only correction) and was left untouched in both tap copies.

  **Follow-up (PR #755 round-4 review, XACA-0883-031): one `--force` help-text line was missed by the original port above.** Canonical's `kb-knowledge-add --help` documents the deliberate non-implication between the two independent guards this ticket added: `--force` (waives the pre-existing PII/session-disambiguation guard) does NOT also waive `--allow-nonstandard-base`'s layout-correctness guard, and vice versa — the same reasoning the CHANGELOG entry above already states for why the two flags were kept separate in the first place. That sentence is a safety property, not incidental wording, so it belongs in the tap: a tap user reading only `--force`'s help text with no matching sentence could reasonably (and wrongly) assume `--force` also silences the layout redirect. Added `Does NOT waive --allow-nonstandard-base's layout check.` to the `--force` block in both `share/templates/kanban/kanban-helpers.template.sh` and `share/templates/aliases/kanban-aliases.sh`, immediately after the existing `Ignored for agent/team tiers.` line — help text only, no behavior change, since the guard functions themselves were already byte-identical to canonical.

  **Verified against a standalone clone** (`git clone` of this tap, `origin/main` at `df9e54a1`), not `~/dev-team/homebrew-tap`: `zsh -n` clean on both files; `tests/test-knowledge-helper-parity.sh` (dev-team) 40/40 pass pointed at the clone via `KB_PARITY_REFERENCE_DIR`; `scripts/tests/test-knowledge-region-parity.sh` 133/133 pass via a path-redirected scratch copy (that script has no built-in override); an ad-hoc co-change comparison — every `_kb_knowledge_*`/`_kb_canonical_kanban_dir_for_repo`/`kb-knowledge-*` function whose normalized body differs between canonical `develop` and the XACA-0883 worktree also differs, in both tap copies, between this repo's `origin/main` and the clone — confirming the same coverage the `tests/test-xaca-0810-tap-cochange-guard.zsh --gate` CI job checks once the outer PR's submodule pointer is bumped to this commit (that job's `--self-test` mode, which needs no PR context, was run unmodified against the clone and passed 8/8).

- **XACA-0884-013 — second `set -u` instance in the same `demote` arm: `demote_reason="$2"` → `"${2-}"`.** The first instance (`selector="$1"`) was found and fixed during XACA-0884-006; its twin, one line away in the same flag loop, shipped anyway and was caught by the PR #753 test gate. Under a caller whose shell already has `nounset` on before sourcing, `kb-backlog demote <id> --reason` with `--reason` as the final token aborted with `2: parameter not set` instead of accepting an empty reason. Canonical was already correct (`"${2-}"`); this was a tap-only parity break. The lesson — fixing the reported instance is not fixing the bug class — is now enforced by a static assertion over the whole arm in `tests/test-xaca-0884-demote-nounset.sh`, which fails if ANY bare `="$N"` assignment appears in either copy's demote arm. `case "$1" in` is deliberately not flagged: the enclosing `while [[ $# -gt 0 ]]` guarantees `$1` is set.

- **XACA-0884-014 — corrected the demote success message's reaper disclosure.** It named `cleanup-all` only; both `kb-backlog cleanup` and `cleanup-all` clear worktree pointers without checking status. The message now names both and states that neither runs automatically. **A QA finding claiming the Python reaper "fires automatically on every ordinary Claude Code Stop" was investigated and found incorrect** — `clear_orphaned_item_fields` appears in `kanban-stop.py` only as its own definition, two of its own log lines, and a comment; its sole external call site repo-wide is `kanban-helpers.sh:9134` (`kb-backlog cleanup`), and the Stop path states verbatim "We intentionally do NOT auto-clear orphaned item fields here." Both reapers are operator-invoked. The disclosure was widened to name both; its severity framing was deliberately NOT escalated, because the premise for escalating it was false.

- **XACA-0897 — Guard 5: mirrored the release-cut CR-divergence halt into `share/scripts/kb-release-version-gate`, `share/scripts/kb-release-push-promote`, and `share/scripts/kb-cr.sh`.** Canonical source is `dev-team/scripts/`; these are `sync_file` copies synced byte-for-byte, no tap-side adaptation. The change halts a release cut when the release's `platforms[<plat>].version` no longer matches the `cr_submitted_version` its linked, CAB-approved CR was submitted under — preventing the 2026-08-05 CR-overwrite incident at the release cut rather than containing it on the Confluence publish path (XACA-0896). Creating a new CR page triggers a new CAB review, so a post-submission renumber costs a full governance cycle.
  - `kb-cr.sh` gains `_kb_cr_set_submit_stamps` / `_kb_cr_get_submit_stamps` (public plumbing `kb-cr _set_submit_stamps` / `_get_submit_stamps`), stamping `cr_submitted_version` onto the `.crs[<idx>]` container record at the `cr-submitted` transition, mirroring the XACA-0896 publish-stamp pattern's record, activity-event shape, and reader-returns-`null` convention. The version resolves through `releaseAssignment.releaseId` → `.releases[]` → `platforms[<plat>].version` — deliberately the same field the gate blocks on, not top-level `.version` and not `shortTitle` — and fails closed on all four unresolvable shapes, writing nothing rather than an empty or placeholder value.
  - `kb-release-version-gate` gains a "Step 0" CR-divergence check with a new exit code **3**, distinct from `1` (ordinary code-vs-target failure). It resolves from the local board before the LCARS fetch and upstream of all 7 pre-existing "gate skipped" exit-0 paths, so it sits ahead of the fail-open surface rather than being patched into it. On an unresolvable board it falls back to the release manifest's `crIds[]` cache: a CR claim present → halt; manifest readable with no claim → not applicable; neither readable → halt. That fallback matters for consumers whose local board is not materialized — without it, a board-less team's release cut hard-blocks regardless of whether CAB governance applies.
  - `kb-release-push-promote` branches on gate rc `3` *before* its auto-bump branch, so `--auto-bump` cannot clear this halt class under any flag combination. Auto-bump reconciles code to the release target; applying it here would perform the very renumber the halt exists to detect.
  - **Review-round hardening (PR #754 gates).** `kb-release-push-promote` now **hard-blocks** when `kb-release-version-gate` cannot be resolved, instead of warning and promoting un-gated — that was the one place Guard 5's fail-closed contract was fail-open at its own main automated caller. The board-unresolvable fallback in `kb-release-version-gate` now distinguishes three cases honestly rather than printing one reassuring message for all of them: no board file at all (board-less alias — no releases dir derivable, no manifest lookup attempted), board resolved but manifest confirmed absent, and manifest read and confirmed empty. The prior wording claimed "the release manifest shows no CR linked" even when no manifest was ever opened — a reassuring artifact describing a check that never ran, which is the precise failure pattern this guard exists to prevent. `kb-cr.sh`'s submit-stamp read paths now pass array indices via `--argjson`, matching the write path in the same file, and the three forbidden `echo "$var" | jq` sites in Step 0 are herestrings.
  - Consumer-visible behaviour change: a release cut whose linked CR has **no** `cr_submitted_version` stamped now halts rather than proceeding. A missing stamp is treated as unsafe, not as permission — the same absence-read-as-permission substitution that caused the original incident. Canonical ships `kb-cr-backfill-submit-version` (dry-run by default, open CRs only, never infers an unrecoverable version) to bound how noisy that is on legacy records; it is not tap-mirrored.

- **XACA-0859 — LCARS Epics tab now resolves an epic's items via `epic.itemIds` (forward), not the `item.epicId` back-reference.** `share/lcars-ui/server.py`'s `LCARSHandler._get_items_for_epic` was the last derived-state read path still reverse-scanning `.backlog` for `item.epicId == epic_id`, while the epic-state contract defines the assigned set as `.backlog` items whose id appears in `epic.itemIds` — the resolution the jq engine (`_kb_epic_derive_state`) and the Roadmap builder already used. Two independent sources of truth for one relationship, held together only by `kb-epic add-item` writing both fields in a single jq expression. Dormant until XACA-0855 made empty-effective derive `ARCHIVED` instead of `PLANNED`, after which any disagreement renders as LCARS showing ARCHIVED while the CLI shows PLANNED for the same epic. The function now finds the epic in `.epics`, builds an id set from its `itemIds`, and filters `.backlog` by membership — matching the jq expression exactly. Signature and returned item shape are unchanged, so `serve_epics_list`, `serve_epic_detail`, and `serve_epic_items` inherit the fix untouched; iteration stays over `.backlog` rather than `itemIds`, which both excludes orphan ids and preserves the item ordering the tab has always shown. `handle_delete_epic` deliberately does NOT converge: it clears the **union** of forward-resolved items and any item carrying the `epicId` back-reference, so deleting an epic cannot strand an item pointing at it. **User-visible impact: none** — an audit across 15 live boards / 97 epics found exactly 2 divergent records fleet-wide, both of a class that renders identically under either resolution, and order-sensitive resolved-set parity is 0 mismatches across all 97. Tap-side change is the mirrored `share/lcars-ui/server.py` + `share/lcars-ui/CHANGELOG.md` only; canonical source is `dev-team/lcars-ui/`.

- **XACA-0884-004 — ported `kb-backlog demote` (alias `todo`) into `share/templates/kanban/kanban-helpers.template.sh`, the hand-maintained port target that has no canonical dev-team source of its own.** Canonical added `demote|todo)` to `kanban-helpers.sh` immediately after `unpick)`; the template had no such command. **Ported, not copy-pasted**, per the XACA-0395/XACA-0935 precedent for this exact file: the template has no `_kb_flush_work_time` function at all (verified absent), so canonical's careful *avoidance* of calling it on the demote path is trivially satisfied here rather than requiring adaptation — but that absence is not license to skip the anti-regression comment. The ported block carries the same explicit "do NOT call `_kb_flush_work_time`, and do NOT add one" warning, citing the XACA-0552 freeze precedent (an open `workStartedAt` left ~4 months stale must never be booked as ~4 months of phantom work on demote), so a future sync toward canonical does not "helpfully" wire one in. Preserves all four worktree fields (`worktree`/`worktreeBranch`/`worktreeWindowId`/`worktreeLinkedAt`) by default, clearing exactly those four under `--clear-worktree`; refuses on `completed`/`cancelled` without `--force`; sets/clears `pausedReason`/`pausedAt`/`pausedPreviousStatus` under `--reason`; defaults every status read via `// "todo"`, never `// empty`; clears `workingOnId` only when it equals the demoted item's id; rejects subitem ids with a pointer to `sub todo`. Initially adapted to the template's own argument-parsing idiom (`"$1"` + `shift 2>/dev/null`, matching the template's `tag`/`unpick` cases) on the reasoning that the template does not run under `set -u` — **that reasoning was wrong and the divergence was reverted to canonical's `"${1-}"`** (commit `4138b9eb`). The template does not set `-u` itself, but a *caller* whose shell already has `nounset` on before sourcing hits `1: parameter not set` on a missing id instead of the usage block. Caught by XACA-0884-006 adversarial testing, which ran identical inputs through canonical and tap specifically because this idiom differed — the divergence was the reason the comparison existed, and it was the one place it found a real behavioural difference. Registered in both `kb-backlog`'s in-function help and the top-level `kb-*` help listing, alongside the existing `unpick` entry. **Verified against a disposable fixture board** (never the real board): demoting an `in_progress` item with a `workStartedAt` frozen since 2026-03-31 and `timeWorkedMs=5000000` left `timeWorkedMs` at exactly `5000000` post-demote (no phantom-time inflation); all four worktree fields survived by default and were cleared exactly under `--clear-worktree`; `--reason` set the three `paused*` fields and left `blockedBy` intact on a `blocked` item; `completed`/`cancelled` refused without `--force` and succeeded with it, preserving `completedAt`; a status-less item read as already-`todo` and no-op'd; a subitem id was rejected with the `sub todo` pointer; the `todo` alias resolved identically to `demote`. `zsh -n` clean on the modified template (this file's actual shebang is `#!/bin/zsh`; `bash -n` fails on pre-existing zsh-only syntax elsewhere in the file, unrelated to this change). No `_kb_flush_work_time` reference was introduced outside the anti-regression comment (grep-confirmed).
- **XACA-0135 — mirrored the repaired `lcars-ui/tests/test_server.py` from the canonical dev-team source.** The canonical suite had drifted red (5 failed / 174 passed) against develop: three `TestHandleArchiveReleaseCleanup` tests whose hand-rolled config fakes predate the `_lock_held` kwarg added by XACA-0890's concurrency fix, one asserting the retired `LCARS_TEAM` default `'freelance'` (intentionally changed to `''` by XACA-0460), and one reading the host machine's live `~/.aiteamforge/team-paths.json` instead of a fixture. All five were TEST DRIFT — `lcars-ui/server.py` is unchanged, here and canonically.

  Beyond the drift repairs, this mirror also carries a closed coverage hole found by mutation testing: `test_team_query_param_routes_to_correct_directory` could not fail for the reason its name advertises, because the shared `_run_archive()` helper patched `TEAM_KANBAN_DIRS[effective_team]` and `KANBAN_DIR` to the same temp directory — so a server ignoring `?team=` and falling back to the default dir stayed green. The helper now accepts a distinct `default_kanban_dir`, and the test asserts both that the archive lands in the team directory and that nothing lands in the default one. The fakes use `create_autospec` bound to the live method rather than permissive `**kwargs`, so a future signature change fails loudly instead of being silently absorbed.

  Also hardens the remaining 7 `_load_releases_config`/`_save_releases_config` fake pairs in the same file (XACA-0135-059, PR #752 `[Review]` gate) onto the same `create_autospec` pattern, so the whole file gets signature-drift enforcement rather than just the three tests that happened to break. Enforcement proven live: injecting a mandatory `checksum` kwarg into the real method makes the autospec raise `TypeError: missing a required argument: 'checksum'` under production's actual call shape. No test assertion changed — only the fake construction mechanism.

  Suite result after the change: 179 passed, 0 failed. Mirror is byte-identical to the canonical source (`sync-tap.sh --check` reports 890 files checked, 0 drifted).

## [0.20.0] - 2026-08-18

- **XACA-0935-016 (`cochange` gate, PR #750 `[Review]` protected gate) — closed an out-of-lock unlink window the prior `-015` END-block cleanup fix introduced in `_kb_jq_atomic_write`'s explicit failure path, ported into both hand-maintained tap copies.** `-015` moved `.tmp` cleanup into an `END { unlink($tmp_file) ... }` block so every `die` exit got cleanup, but the routine (non-`die`) failure branch explicitly `close($fh)`s (releasing the flock) before `exit()`ing — so the END block's unlink then ran OUT of the lock. Because `$tmp_file` is a fixed, non-unique name (`${board_file}.tmp`) shared by every writer racing for the same lock, a slow-to-exit failing process could delete the `.tmp` file a different, already-relocked writer had just created moments after the first writer gave up the lock — assessed as fail-closed (the victim sees a spurious "empty output" error and aborts cleanly; no corruption, no silent data loss), so a robustness fix, not a correctness one. Fixed by moving the unlink back inside the failure branch, before `close($fh)`, restoring the primitive's original in-lock ordering; the `END` block is retained purely as the `-015` safety net for `die` exits, all of which (from the point `$tmp_file` actually exists onward) already happen before `$fh` is closed, so they still always run in-lock. Verified both properties hold after the change: 20 iterations of a failing writer racing a succeeding writer for the same lock/tmp/target never lost or corrupted the succeeding writer's output and left no orphaned `.tmp`; the existing signal-safety suite (SIGTERM/SIGINT mid-write, 9/9) confirms `.tmp` cleanup still fires on the `die`-adjacent paths `-015` closed. Ported verbatim (functional change only, tap's own condensed comment register kept) into both `share/templates/kanban/kanban-helpers.template.sh` and `share/templates/aliases/kanban-aliases.sh`, each of which carries its own full `_kb_jq_atomic_write` copy per the XACA-0935 port precedent below — `cochange` would otherwise flag the same drift class this whole entry class exists to close.

- **XACA-0935 (`cochange` gate, PR #750) — ported the `sh -c` round-trip elimination, signal-safety, and cross-device-rename fixes into the two hand-maintained tap copies the `cochange` guard was correctly refusing to let slide.** An earlier pass on this ticket concluded "no tap-mirrored file is affected" (see the outer dev-team `CHANGELOG.md` for the corrected wording) on the reasoning that `kanban-helpers.template.sh` is not in `kb-edit-shared`'s mirrored-surface map and has independently drifted from the canonical `kanban-helpers.sh` — true as far as it goes, but exactly the inference the XACA-0810 co-change guard exists to reject: these are hand-maintained **port targets**, not `sync_file` mirrors, and drift from canonical is the normal, expected state, not evidence the port is unnecessary. Direct inspection confirmed both tap copies still carried the original defect verbatim: `share/templates/kanban/kanban-helpers.template.sh`'s `_kb_jq_update` and `share/templates/aliases/kanban-aliases.sh`'s `_kb_jq_update` both still built a shell command STRING via zsh's `printf '%q'` and handed it to a freshly spawned `sh -c "..."` — the identical dash-`/bin/sh`-mangles-`$'...'`-quoting defect canonical's XACA-0935 fix closed — and the template's `_kb_log_activity` still had the same defect on its (worse) unconditionally-multi-line `--argjson new_entry` payload. Precedent for porting into this exact file: XACA-0395 ported 4 call sites into `kanban-helpers.template.sh`'s own conventions rather than copy-pasting canonical verbatim, "genuinely diverged... so ported to the template's own conventions."

  **Ported, not copy-pasted**, per that same precedent: added a new `_kb_jq_atomic_write` primitive to both files (same shape as canonical's — argv passed straight to Perl's `system(LIST)` form via `execvp`, no shell in the path at all) and routed each file's own `_kb_jq_update` through it; `kanban-helpers.template.sh`'s `_kb_log_activity` was adapted in place rather than replaced wholesale, keeping its own structural differences from canonical intact (its `${AITEAMFORGE_DIR}/amb-session-map.json` agent-id fallback vs. canonical's dev-only `${HOME}/dev-team/amb-session-map.json` path, and its simpler agent-id resolution without canonical's XACA-0628 freelance-overlay special case). `kanban-aliases.sh` has no `_kb_log_activity` of its own — only `_kb_jq_update`/`_kb_jq_read` — so only the one call site needed the port there.

  **All three PR #750 review fixes carried over, not just the original `sh -c` elimination**: (1) gate on the FULL Perl `$status`, never `$status >> 8` alone — a signal-killed child leaves the high byte at 0, so `>> 8` alone reads a `SIGTERM`/`SIGINT` death as a clean `exit(0)` and would rename a truncated tmp file over the target; a signal death now takes the failure branch unconditionally and returns `128 + signal`; (2) `_kb_log_activity`'s tmp file is now minted inside `${activity_dir}` itself (`mktemp "${activity_dir}/.${parent_item_id}.json.XXXXXX"`) rather than under `$TMPDIR` — `rename()` cannot cross a filesystem boundary the way the `mv` it replaced could, and `$TMPDIR` is tmpfs on most Linux hosts, so the old placement would silently drop audit entries with no visible error (the whole call is `2>/dev/null`'d, best-effort by design); (3) the supporting hardening — `system { $ARGV[0] } @ARGV` indirect-object form to force `execvp` LIST-form dispatch unconditionally, a `die` guard on `scalar(@ARGV) < 2`, per-failure-mode stderr diagnostics (execute-failure vs. signal-kill vs. nonzero-exit vs. genuine-empty-output, each distinct), an `END`-block `unlink($tmp_file)` cleanup keyed on `$cleanup_tmp` so a `die` between lock-open and the successful rename never leaves a stray `.tmp` behind, and the `# zsh-index-ok: Perl, 0-indexed by design` pragma on the literal `$ARGV[0]` reference (the tap's own `zsh-array-indexing-check`-equivalent guard would otherwise flag it, matching the marker canonical's own gate required).

  **Verified, not just ported**: extracted `_kb_jq_atomic_write` from each file and sourced it standalone — a multi-line `--arg` value round-trips through both copies byte-for-byte with no corruption (the exact defect class this closes); a real child process SIGTERM'd mid-write via a self-reported pid (not a `pgrep`-matched pid, which would grab the wrong process) leaves the target file completely untouched, no `.tmp` residue, exit code `143` (`128+15`) in both files. `zsh -n` clean on both. No `sh -c "jq ...` round-trip remains in either file (`grep -c` confirms 0 in both).

- **XACA-0694 — mirrored `lcars-ui/server.py`'s release-archive referential-integrity cascade.** The LCARS server's two archive paths (`handle_archive_release`, and the archive branch of `handle_toggle_release_archive`) removed a release from `.releases[]` and deleted its manifest while never touching `.backlog[].releaseAssignment` on the items pointing at it, so every archive minted permanently dangling pointers. This is the root cause of the orphan population XACA-0692 investigated, and it was still firing: the live count had grown from 154 (2026-06-12) to 267, with 71 orphans carrying an `assignedAt` later than that investigation, the newest nine days before the fix. New `_cascade_clear_release_assignments()` runs inside the existing `_board_write_transaction`, before the release is filtered out, and deletes the `releaseAssignment` key entirely (ABSENT, not null — matching `kb-release-unassign`); cleared ids are recorded into the archived release JSON as `clearedAssignments` so the trail survives the archive. Resolution is strictly per-board: release IDs are per-board namespaced (`REL-2026-Q2-006` denotes three unrelated releases across academy/firebase/freelance-bandwear), so cross-board lookup would bind items to unrelated releases and is deliberately absent. Activity logging runs OUTSIDE the transaction and after the response is written — `log_activity()` can spawn a `tmux display-message` subprocess with a 2s timeout per call, and looping that under the exclusive board lock stalled every other LCARS board writer for up to 2s x the number of cleared items. Accepted asymmetry, documented in-code: unarchiving does not restore cleared assignments; `clearedAssignments` is the surviving record. Mirror-only sync of the canonical dev-team source — see the dev-team `CHANGELOG.md` [Unreleased] for the full XACA-0694-001/002/003/004 detail, including the `kb-release-doctor` reporter and its guarded `--fix`, which are **not** mirrored here (see the `Tap-Divergence: kb-release XACA-0694` trailer on the outer commit; the tap's `kb-release` family is already long-diverged and retiring that backlog is XACA-0822's scope).

- **XACA-0823 — `kb-port-fix.py` pre-stripped the instance id before calling `compute_instance_port()`, so a `mainevent-<project>` renumber resolved the board-less alias's own band instead of the per-project one.** Filed as the known residual at the end of XACA-0806, which fixed the shadowing bug inside `_resolve_template_band()` but could not reach this caller (tap submodule uninitialized in that worktree). Both call sites in `_compute_plan_with_new_ports()` — the collision-renumber loop AND the null-port loop — did `template = _split_template(iid)` before `compute_instance_port(template, ...)`. That strip is not merely redundant: `compute_instance_port()` is *already* tolerant of full instance ids by its own documented contract (`"finance-personal" resolves to "finance"`), and pre-stripping actively defeats the two lookup steps XACA-0806 added — step 1's direct `DEFAULT_TEAMS` hit and step 2's explicit `_TEMPLATE_PORT_BANDS` hit — both of which exist precisely to stop the bare `mainevent` alias entry from shadowing the per-project band. **Fix:** pass the full `iid` at both sites. `_split_template()` itself is retained — `_is_contract_violating_key()` still uses it. **Correction to the filed premise:** the ticket described the misallocation as landing in `[8400, 8409)`. The bare `mainevent` alias's band range is **1**, not 10, so the band is `[8400, 8401)` — a single port the alias already occupies. The real pre-fix behavior is therefore config-dependent and worse than "misallocates": with the alias entry present (the normal case) `compute_instance_port` raises `ValueError: Port band exhausted for template 'mainevent': band [8400, 8401), 1 of 1 used` and **kb-port-fix crashes**; with the alias absent it silently allocates **8400**, colliding with the alias's reserved port. Both variants are now pinned by tests. **Tests:** new `TestMainEventFullIidPassedToAllocator` (10 cases) plus a `TestRealTeamPathsConfigUntouched` guard in `libexec/commands/test_kb_port_fix.py`; 33 → 44 tests, zero pre-existing failures before or after. Coverage deliberately includes the null-port path as its own cases — it is the second call site, and a partial fix touching only the collision loop would leave it broken silently. Non-regression cases pin `finance-personal` → `[8360,8369]`, `legal-coparenting` → `[8320,8329]`, `medical-general` → `[8340,8349]`, `freelance-<client>-<project>` → `[8500,8599]`, and a dashed instance with no registry entry of its own falling through to the strip-dash step exactly as before, since the change alters what is passed for *every* template, not just mainevent. **Negative control, independently re-run by the orchestrator rather than taken on the implementer's report:** reverting only the two-line fix turns 5 of the 10 new cases red — 3 `FAIL` (port 8400 allocated where `>= 8401` was asserted) and 2 `ERROR` raising the exact `band [8400, 8401), 1 of 1 used` message quoted above, one from each of the two call sites. The remaining 5 pass pre-fix by design (they are the other-template non-regression cases the bug never touched). Restored and re-verified byte-identical (md5) and green at 44/44. Band math is never reimplemented here — both production and tests call the canonical `_resolve_template_band()` / `compute_instance_port()` in `kanban-hooks/aiteamforge_paths.py`, which is unchanged by this ticket and was verified to be already correct. Tap-native change (`libexec/commands/` is not a `share/` mirror — no canonical dev-team source, no `sync-tap.sh` run). Does not bump `VERSION` or cut a release.

- **XACA-0823-002/003/004 (PR #746 `[Test]` protected gate) — closed the three coverage gaps the test gate found.** New `TestMainEventAllocatorEdgeCases`, 44 → 47 tests. All three behaviors were manually verified correct at review time, so these are regression guards rather than bug reproductions: **(002)** genuine per-project band exhaustion with all 19 slots of `[8401,8419]` occupied must raise, never wrap around onto the alias's 8400 or silently reuse a port; **(003)** a 3+ dash-component id (`mainevent-foo-bar`) must still resolve via `split("-")[0]`, guarding against a future "improvement" to `rsplit` or a two-component assumption; **(004)** a single run with BOTH a collision and a null-port entry — the two loops allocate against a shared `working_data` copy, so a regression that failed to mark one loop's allocation as used would hand the same port to both; the test asserts they are distinct, not merely in-band. **A weak assertion was caught and fixed during authoring**: 002 originally asserted `assertIn("8401", msg)`, which does NOT discriminate — the pre-fix exhaustion message reads `band [8400, 8401), 1 of 1 used` and also contains `8401`, so the test would have passed against the very defect it guards. Re-anchored on the band's opening bound (`[8401,` present, `[8400,` absent). Negative control re-run on the new class alone: reverting the two-line fix turns all **3 of 3** red (2 FAIL + 1 ERROR); restored and green at 47/47.

- **XACA-0823 (PR #746 `[Review]` gate) — corrected a factual error in the outer `CHANGELOG.md`'s rationale for leaving `aiteamforge_paths.py` untouched.** It claimed the canonical allocator "has five other callers". Grep says one other production call site (`scripts/kb-port-reconcile`, which already passes full instance ids and needs no fix). The argument for a caller-side fix stands on its own; the inflated number did not, and an unverified count in a rationale is exactly the kind of assertion that gets copied forward as fact.

- **XACA-0395 — API-key auth gate for all state-mutating LCARS + fleet-monitor routes; browser client migrated to a central authenticated fetch wrapper.** Mirrors XACA-0395 subitems 002–007: new `share/scripts/kb-api-key` generate/rotate/show helper for `~/.aiteamforge/api-key` (mode `0600`) — reaching a real consumer machine required clearing THREE independent gates, not one: (1) this sync-tap.sh mapping (mirrors it into `share/scripts/`), (2) `install-shell.sh::install_helper_scripts()`'s fresh-install allowlist, and (3) `aiteamforge-upgrade.sh`'s `update_runtime_helpers()` — its self-maintaining `*.sh`/`*.py` glob does not match an extensionless name (needed an explicit glob entry, same as `kb-init-team`), and its default "only refresh what already exists" rule would otherwise skip a brand-new file forever (needed adding to `_xaca0673_mandatory_materialize_basenames`). Clearing (1) and (2) alone still left every ALREADY-INSTALLED, upgrading machine with the new auth-gate code but no way to ever provision a key — `aiteamforge upgrade` never calls `install_helper_scripts()` at all (verified: zero references in `aiteamforge-upgrade.sh`), so (3) is the gate that actually matters for the installed base, not (1)/(2). All three are now closed; `kanban-helpers.sh`'s primary `${AITEAMFORGE_DIR}/scripts/kb-api-key` resolution path now resolves on both fresh installs and upgraded machines. **No dedicated test exercises this specific file** — `tests/test-xaca-0673-mandatory-materialize.sh` covers the general materialize-when-absent mechanism (fixture: `iterm2_venv_bootstrap.py`) and all 6 of its cases still pass, but its Test 6 parity guard only auto-detects Python sibling-`import`s and cannot see a shell tool exec'd from `kanban-helpers.sh`; this gap is closed by inspection and cross-reference, not by a regression test; `share/lcars-ui/server.py` gains an auth gate on `do_POST`/`do_PUT`/`do_PATCH`/`do_DELETE` with a public-route allowlist, plus same-origin-only `/api/auth-key` delivery (`_origin_matches_host()`, no `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, 403 + `Vary: Origin` on a foreign `Origin` — closes the P0 found in Wave 3 where a wildcard ACAO on a credential-bearing endpoint let any origin read the key); new `share/lcars-ui/js/api-auth.js` central `apiFetch()` wrapper migrates 86 mutating call sites across 6 files; `fleet-monitor/server/lib/auth-middleware.js` (new) is the shared Express Bearer/`X-API-Key` middleware, mounted in `server.js` ahead of the 14 mutating routes in `vault-routes.js` (6) and `engines-routes.js` (3), and `msg-relay-routes.js`'s inline `FLEET_AUTH_TOKEN` check converges onto the same middleware instead of duplicating it; `fleet-reporter.sh` sends the auth header on its POSTs. New test coverage: `share/lcars-ui/tests/test_api_auth.js`, `fleet-monitor/server/tests/auth-middleware.test.js`, `fleet-monitor/server/tests/xaca-0395-005-auth-wiring.test.js`; `package.json`'s `test` script registers the two new Node suites. Posture: open when the key is unset, closed when set (Phase 1) — see `kanban/plans/XACA-0395/` for the full contract and the always-closed follow-on. **Also added `fleet-monitor/server/data/pushed-knowledge.json` to `.gitignore`**: gitignored on the dev-team side but with no matching tap-side exclusion, it would otherwise be swept into a tap commit by any future `sync-tap.sh --commit` run's `git add -A`. **Outer pointer bump + PR land in a follow-up subitem (010).**

- **XACA-0395 (PR #748 corrective — `sync-tap-drift` + `cochange` gates) — synced three stale `share/lcars-ui/` mirrors and closed the auth-args gap in `share/templates/kanban/kanban-helpers.template.sh`.** `sync-tap-drift`: plain `sync-tap.sh` materialize picked up `share/lcars-ui/index.html` (a `?v=` cache-buster bump made after the prior subitem's sync), `share/lcars-ui/js/api-auth.js`, and `share/lcars-ui/tests/test_api_auth.js` — the latter two carrying the `[UX]` gate's fix for a **permanent silent-failure bug**, not a transient load-order race: `createApiAuthClient()` captured `window.showToast` into a closure variable at CONSTRUCTION time, but `index.html` loads `api-auth.js` at ~line 47 (deliberately early, before every mutating call site) while `lcars.js` — which defines `showToast` — loads ~2600 lines later in the same document, so the capture saw `undefined` and never re-checked it: every 401 across all 86 migrated call sites degraded to `console.error()` for the entire page session, on every load, not intermittently. Fixed by resolving the notifier at CALL time (`resolveNotifier()`) instead of at construction — an injected `deps.notify` still wins, so the injection contract the unit tests exercise is unchanged; that same injection path is why the prior unit tests passed while the browser path was silently broken (Node has no `window` at all, a different precondition than "window exists, `showToast` not yet defined" even though both produce the same symptom). 3 new regression tests (suite 26 → 29), verified with a negative control: reverting to construction-time capture reproduces 0 toasts fired. All three files are direct `sync_file` mirrors, copied verbatim, no hand-adaptation needed. `cochange`: `kanban-helpers.template.sh` is a **long-diverged, hand-maintained port target**, not a `sync_file` mirror — it has no canonical dev-team source and is never touched by `sync-tap.sh`. Canonical `kanban-helpers.sh` subitem 006 added `_kb_lcars_auth_args`/`_kb_fleet_auth_args` (populate a curl `-H` Authorization array, or leave it empty when no key/token resolves — open-posture degradation, never a hard fail) and wired them into 4 functions' curl calls (`_kb_push_board`, `_kb_register_team`, `_kb_release_sync`, `kb-release-create`); none of that existed in the template, so an upgraded consumer's installed `kanban-helpers.sh` would send no credential on every mutating `kb-*` call once a key was provisioned — a functional gap, not an intentional divergence, so no `Tap-Divergence:` trailer. Ported the same behavior in the template's own idiom: `_kb_lcars_auth_args` resolves via the installed `${AITEAMFORGE_DIR}/scripts/kb-api-key` only (the dev-only `~/dev-team/scripts/kb-api-key` fallback canonical carries is not applicable to a shipped consumer and was dropped); `_kb_fleet_auth_args` keeps canonical's `~/.aiteamforge/fleet-config.json` → `~/.dev-team/fleet-config.json` fallback and `.centralServer.authToken` key path, matching the existing consumer-side precedent already in this same tap (`share/scripts/fleet-reporter.sh`, `share/scripts/msg-client.js`). Function bodies of the 4 callers were left otherwise untouched — only the auth-args declaration + call + `"${_KB_*_AUTH_ARGS[@]}"` curl arg were added, preserving every pre-existing template-vs-canonical stylistic divergence (`$SESSION_TYPE` vs `${SESSION_TYPE:-}`, the template's own `_lcars_port` file-read pattern vs canonical's `_kb_team_lcars_port`, etc.) per the co-change guard's own purpose. Verified: `zsh -n` clean (file's actual shebang, `#!/bin/zsh`); `sync-tap.sh --check` 0 drifted / 0 missing after materialize.

## [0.19.0] - 2026-08-13

- **XACA-0862 (PR #744, `tap-connect-disconnect-parity` gate, BLOCKING, Linux-CI-only) — `install-team.sh finance --project personal --connect-only` aborted non-zero with `printf: write error: Broken pipe` from `libexec/lib/aiteamforge-paths.sh`, failing 1 of 9 teams; passed every time on macOS.** Root cause: `_AITEAMFORGE_DEFAULT_TEAMS_DATA()` is a static team-table emitter consumed by several legitimate early-closing readers — a literal pipe into `_aiteamforge_write_defaults`'s python3 heredoc (the config-bootstrap path `install-team.sh` opts into via `AITEAMFORGE_ALLOW_BOOTSTRAP_WRITE=1`, which fires on every team's first field lookup inside the gate's fresh, isolated per-team sandbox), plus several `while read ... done < <(_AITEAMFORGE_DEFAULT_TEAMS_DATA)` loops that `break` as soon as they find the row they want (e.g. the `lcars_port_base` prefix scan in `aiteamforge_compute_instance_port`). Any of these can close its end of the pipe before every row has been written; a `printf` that then writes into the closed pipe fails with EPIPE, and under the `set -euo pipefail` every caller inherits, that non-zero can abort the caller — for data that is a fixed literal table and can never legitimately fail. Two independent failure shapes exist depending on SIGPIPE's disposition in the writing process: (a) EPIPE returned as an ordinary command failure (produces exactly the observed stderr message, and is recoverable), or (b) the kernel terminating the process outright on the write syscall (silent, no message, unrecoverable from inside the function since there's no command completion for error-handling to run against). Fixed both: `trap '' PIPE` at the top of the function converts shape (b) into shape (a), and wrapping the entire emission as the LHS of `{ ... } 2>/dev/null || true` absorbs shape (a) — verified empirically that this construct suspends `errexit` for every command inside the group, not just the group's own exit status, and silences the write-error message. Emitted output is byte-identical before/after. **Reproduced before the fix** (this class of bug does not reproduce reliably via natural timing on macOS/BSD pipes the way it does on Linux): a literal early-closing pipe (`` $(_AITEAMFORGE_DEFAULT_TEAMS_DATA | grep -m1 ...) `` under `set -euo pipefail`) hit the identical SIGPIPE abort both via natural race against the real, unmodified function (12–15 of 150 runs) and deterministically via a forced buffer-overflow variant (5 of 5). **Verified after the fix**: 0 of 150 (natural race) and 0 of 50 (forced) failures against the same tests; `scripts/check-tap-connect-disconnect-parity.sh` reports `OK` (9/9 teams, 5 consecutive runs) and `--self-check` still transitions `GREEN -> RED -> GREEN` — the gate remains fully able to fail for real reasons.

- **XACA-0862 (PR #744 review gate, BLOCKING) — ported the `kb-quarantine-stub` canonical fix into the tap-shipped mirror `share/templates/aliases/kanban-aliases.sh`; the `cochange` guard was failing, unwaived.** The preceding entry fixed `kb-quarantine-stub`'s canonical-path resolution in dev-team's `kanban-helpers.sh` but never ported it to the mirror — and that mirror is the copy consumers actually install (`install-kanban.sh` and `libexec/commands/aiteamforge-upgrade.sh` both explicitly prefer it; `kanban-helpers.template.sh` does not define the function at all). Both PR #744 gate bots independently flagged this as blocking. Fixed by porting the behavioural delta only — the `case` computing canonical paths directly for the 3 unambiguous personal-org teams, falling back to `_kb_get_board_file` otherwise — while preserving the mirror's long-standing local divergences (`$1` vs `${1-}`, its own comment register, and its `stub_path` map which deliberately never had a `command` arm). **The `Tap-Divergence:` waiver was deliberately NOT used**: waiving it would have shipped a fix present in canonical and absent from the consumer copy, which is precisely the presence-vs-delivery failure this ticket exists to eliminate. Note for reviewers: the co-change gate reads the tap side from the committed gitlink blob, so it only flips green after the two-step submodule commit lands — a locally-fixed working tree still reports DRIFT.
- **XACA-0862-031 ([Review] protected gate, PR #744) — SKIP was recorded as `test_pass`, so skipped tests inflated the pass count.** A false green inside the PR whose own purpose is eliminating false greens. `tests/test-runner.sh` gained a real `test_skip()`, a `SKIPPED_TESTS` counter never folded into `PASSED_TESTS`, and a distinct "Skipped: N" summary line; the same pattern was applied to `tests/test-xaca-0611-imgcat-provision.sh`, which carries its own independent framework and had the identical defect. All 15 skip sites converted (10 in `test-installers.sh`, 5 in `test-xaca-0611`). **Verified before/after in a simulated tap-only sandbox**: original reported `Passed: 32` / `Passed: 26` with skips silently counted as passes; fixed reports `Passed: 22, Skipped: 10` / `Passed: 21, Skipped: 5`. Real dev-monorepo runs still execute every assertion for real (32 / 26, no skip line).
- **XACA-0862-032 ([Review] protected gate, PR #744) — `tests/test-installers.sh` hardcoded a parallel list of 10 test names beside the real block, guaranteed to drift silently.** Replaced with `_xaca0212_real_test_names()`, an awk state machine extracting `test_start` names directly from the real (`else`) block between two unique anchors, plus a hard `test_fail` if extraction ever yields zero names (anti-vacuity guard). There is now one list, not two kept in sync by hand. **Verified**: derivation returns exactly the 10 real names today, and a perturbed copy with an 11th `test_start` injected auto-derives 11 with no edit to the skip block — drift is structurally impossible rather than merely detected.

- **XACA-0862 (PR #744) — triaged the 9 tap-CI failures the manifest-drain fix surfaced (67/67 entries now executing); fixed 8, left 1 for a dedicated follow-up.** Root-caused each against the pre-XACA-0862 baseline before touching anything, per-file:
  - `tests/test-xaca-0834-connect-script-recovery.sh` (OURS): T3 asserted the pre-XACA-0862-016 diagnostic wording (named a filename that, for a genuinely-unmatched instance, was never rendered). Updated the assertion to the corrected, instance-id-based message XACA-0862-016 intentionally introduced — 15/15.
  - `libexec/commands/aiteamforge-doctor.sh` (PRE-EXISTING, byte-identical on the pre-XACA-0862 baseline): `check_board_resolution()`'s org-identity-unconfigured branch ended with `[ "$FIX" = true ] && attempt_remediation setup` immediately followed by a bare `return` — under `set -eo pipefail`, a false `$FIX` made the `&&` short-circuit exit 1, which became the function's own return status and silently aborted the ENTIRE doctor run (no Summary, no `--fix` suggestion) the moment this function was called as a bare statement. Converted to explicit `if`/`fi` + `return 0`. `tests/test-doctor-fix.sh` 53/53.
  - `tests/test-command-nested-workingdir.sh` (PRE-EXISTING fixture gaps): the fake-tap fixture never symlinked `libexec/lib/` (install-team.sh's unconditional, unguarded `source .../aiteamforge-paths.sh` died at line 33 before ever reaching the persona-copy code under test) or `share/teams/registry.json` (a second unconditional `exit 1` a few lines later), and never set `AITEAMFORGE_ORG_CONFIG` (so the non-interactive run hit the `/dev/tty` guard). All three predate this ticket. Fixed all three gaps — 18/18.
  - `tests/test-org-config.sh` (PRE-EXISTING, confirmed already broken on the pre-XACA-0862 baseline — 419-530, not the hardcoded 152-262): replaced the hardcoded `sed -n '152,262p'` function extraction with the same awk-marker extraction pattern `test-xaca-0834` already uses, immune to future line drift — 18/18.
  - `tests/test-installers.sh` (ENVIRONMENTAL + one PRE-EXISTING bug underneath): the `kb-quarantine-stub` block sources `dev-team/kanban-helpers.sh` from `TAP_ROOT/..`, which only exists inside the full monorepo checkout, not a standalone tap-only CI checkout — added a monorepo-reachability guard that SKIPs with a visible reason there (matching the established idiom in `test-xaca-0632`'s sibling-drift guard), and runs for real inside the monorepo. Running for real then surfaced a second, genuinely PRE-EXISTING bug in `kanban-helpers.sh`'s `kb-quarantine-stub`: its canonical-path resolution delegated to the shared, existence-gated `_kb_get_board_file`, which errors out (rather than returning the not-yet-existing path) for a genuinely unprovisioned team — collapsing the intended "no canonical board found" message into a generic "could not resolve" one. Fixed by computing the canonical path directly from the same deterministic per-team directories already used for `stub_path`, for the 3 unambiguous personal-org teams only (`command` untouched — its resolution is genuinely ambiguous and out of scope) — 32/32. (dev-team `kanban-helpers.sh` is outside this tap; see dev-team's own history for that half of the fix.)
  - `tests/test-xaca-0611-imgcat-provision.sh` (ENVIRONMENTAL): Cases 7/8 assert against `sync-tap.sh`, `scripts/vendor/iterm2/imgcat`, and `scripts/tests/test-agent-panel-display.sh` — all outer-monorepo-only paths, unreachable from a standalone tap checkout. Same monorepo-reachability guard/SKIP pattern as above — 26/26.
  - `tests/test-xaca-0632-kb-variance.sh` (PRE-EXISTING): Case 6 sourced the shipped, zsh-only `kanban-aliases.sh` (contains a zsh glob qualifier bash's parser rejects outright) under `bash -c`, so `kb-help` — defined after the parse break — never existed; `kb-variance`, defined earlier, survived by accident. Switched to `zsh -c`, matching Case 7's own sibling-drift guard two blocks down — 7/7.
  - `.github/workflows/tests.yml` (ENVIRONMENTAL): `tests/test-xaca-0788-crash-recovery-in-tap.sh` deliberately hard-fails (by design, not a bug — see its own header) rather than skip when `tmux` is unavailable, since tmux liveness IS the behavior under test. `macos-latest` runners never had it installed. Added `tmux` to the job's `brew install` line — 16/16.
  - `tests/test-e2e-setup-launch.sh` (PRE-EXISTING, NOT fixed this round): 5 of 58 assertions fail. Root cause traced two layers deep — `scaffold_install_dir()` never creates `$AITEAMFORGE_DIR/scripts/lcars-launch-helpers.sh`, so the real `start_lcars_server()` never runs (confirmed by direct repro: "Shared LCARS launch helper not found ... cannot start LCARS"); symlinking the REAL helper in surfaces a second, deeper gap — team-key resolution appears to fail case-sensitively for `"iOS"` vs the registry/baked-in `"ios"`. The real helper's lock/retry/curl-exit-code architecture (XACA-0661) is substantially more complex than this test's simple mocks (`-w`-flag curl, filename-grep python3) were built for; a faithful fix means either building a much richer mock or a partial rewrite — properly scoped as a follow-up, not rushed here. Left the file untouched.
  - Suites the ticket's own coverage requires stayed green throughout, under `/bin/bash` (3.2): `test-xaca-0834` 15/0, `test-xaca-0845-connect-script-parametric-teams.sh` 38/0, `test-xaca-0862-006-retire-legacy-connect.sh` 7/0, `test-xaca-0862-022-runtime-resolver.sh` 19/0, `test-xaca-0862-028-resolve-parametric-crash-guard.sh` 4/0, `test-xaca-0862-team-scoped-connect.sh` (large suite, verified green).
- **XACA-0862 (found by the PR #744 review gate) — tap CI silently executed only 23 of 73 manifest entries; every XACA-0845/0862/0925 suite never ran.** The `plain-shell` and `brew-bash` loops in `.github/workflows/tests.yml` invoke `tests/test-runner.sh` without redirecting stdin, so a test that reads stdin consumes the loop's own `< <(awk ...)` process substitution; the loop then ENDS EARLY while reporting PASS for everything it did reach. Reproduced across three consecutive pushes. The XACA-0707 completeness guard could not catch it because it verifies test ENROLLMENT, never EXECUTION — the same presence-vs-delivery gap this ticket exists to close. Fixed with `< /dev/null` on both runner invocations, plus an `EXECUTED == EXPECTED` assertion (and a 0-entry guard) so any future drain, from any cause, fails loudly instead of passing quietly. Verified by reproduction: draining loop executes 2 of 6 and the assertion fires; fixed loop executes 6 of 6.
- **XACA-0862-028 ([Test] protected gate, PR #744) — automated crash-regression coverage for both bash DEFAULT_PROJECT resolvers, closing the gap left by the immediately-preceding fix.** The existing 19-case `homebrew-tap/tests/test-xaca-0862-022-runtime-resolver.sh` only exercises the Python-based runtime (third) resolver, which is immune to the `[[ cond ]] && printf`-as-last-loop-statement idiom entirely — neither `install-team.sh::_resolve_parametric_defaults()` nor `render-cockpit-scripts.sh::_resolve_cockpit_default_project()` had any coverage for the stale-entry reject path that actually crashed. New `homebrew-tap/tests/test-xaca-0862-028-resolve-parametric-crash-guard.sh` (4 cases, registered in `tests/ci-manifest`): awk-extracts `_resolve_parametric_defaults()` verbatim from the real, on-disk `install-team.sh` and drives it inside a subshell with `set -euo pipefail` explicitly re-applied (extraction alone loses the file-scope strict mode the real bug only reproduces under). R1/R2 assert a lone stale entry (working_dir missing, then kanban_dir missing) yields 0 matches and rc=0 — never a crash; R3 is a positive control (a live entry still resolves); R4 proves a stale entry sorting AFTER an already-accepted live entry doesn't corrupt the earlier match. The sibling `render-cockpit-scripts.sh` resolver gets the equivalent outer-repo-only suite — see dev-team's own `CHANGELOG.md` for `tests/test-xaca-0862-028-cockpit-resolver-crash-guard.sh` (outer-only file, not mirrored to this tap). **Both suites verified with a real RED→GREEN transition**: reverted the if/fi back to the bare `&&` in a scratch copy of each source file (never the real file), re-ran the corresponding suite against the mutated copy — R1/R2/R4 failed with rc=1 and empty output (the crash, reproduced) while R3 (no rejected candidate) stayed green, proving the suite discriminates precisely on the fixed bug, not on something else; restored, re-ran against the real fixed source, 4/4 green.

- **XACA-0862-027 ([Review] protected gate, PR #744) — `check-resolve-cochange-guard.sh`'s self-test assertion 6 was vacuous: it never actually read the churn fixture it exists to evaluate.** `_cochange_verdict`'s first branch is `cmp -s "$1" "$2" && canon_changed=0; if canon_changed -eq 0: return OK-UNCHANGED` — assertion 6 called it as `_cochange_verdict "$CANON_NEW" "$CANON_NEW" "$MIRROR_NEW" "$MIRROR_NEW_CHURN" 0`, and since `$1`/`$2` are the identical file, `canon_changed` is unconditionally 0 and the function returns before `$3`/`$4` (the mirror churn pair) are ever compared — `MIRROR_NEW_CHURN` was dead on arrival. Proven by mutation: reducing `_norm_stream()` to `cat` (removing ALL comment/whitespace normalization) left the current suite at 18/18 PASS; the pre-XACA-0862-023 two-mirror suite caught the identical mutation (7 passed / 1 failed) via a canonical-side control that was dropped when the suite was generalized to loop over N mirrors and never replaced. Restored a mirror-independent, canonical-side false-positive control (outside the per-mirror loop): builds a churned copy of the CANONICAL body the same way the existing per-mirror control builds `MIRROR_NEW_CHURN` (added/blank comment lines, trailing whitespace on every line), re-extracts + re-normalizes it via the real `_extract_body`/`_norm_stream` pipeline, and asserts the result is byte-identical to the clean `CANON_NEW` — this genuinely exercises the normalizer instead of comparing two copies of the same pre-normalized file. `MIN_PASS` raised 16 → 19 (3 canon-only + 2 mirrors × 8 = 19) and set to the exact current count rather than a margin below it, since a floor set below the true count is exactly how this control went missing without the self-test's own pass/fail ever catching it. **Verified with a real RED→GREEN transition**: temporarily reduced `_norm_stream()` to `cat` in the real script, re-ran `--self-test` — 18 passed / 1 failed, with the NEW canonical-side control specifically failing (`expected CANON_NEW == CANON_NEW_CHURN after normalization`) and every other assertion, including the (still-vacuous, unchanged) mirror-side assertion 6, still passing; restored `_norm_stream()`, re-ran — 19/19 green.

- **XACA-0862 (crash regression found by the PR #744 test gate) — a stale `team-paths.json` entry (working_dir/kanban_dir no longer present) crashed the bash DEFAULT_PROJECT resolvers under `set -euo pipefail`.** A bare `[[ cond ]] && printf` as the last statement of the candidate loop inside `matches="$( ... )"` returns non-zero when the gate rejects a candidate, failing the command substitution — exactly the stale-entry case the XACA-0862-023 filesystem gate exists to filter gracefully. Replaced with an explicit `if/fi` in `install-team.sh::_resolve_parametric_defaults()` and `scripts/render-cockpit-scripts.sh::_resolve_cockpit_default_project()`. Live entries behave identically; only the reject path changed. Negative control: pre-fix rc=1 (crash) vs fixed rc=0/0 matches on a stale entry.
- **XACA-0862-026 ([Review] finding, fixed) — the runtime resolver never filled `DEFAULT_GROUP`, so a group team (freelance) with exactly one unambiguous registered instance still hard-failed on the required `<group>` positional — the same symptom this ticket fixed for `<project>`, one level up.** New `_xaca0862_resolve_default_group()` in `scripts/templates/team-connect-parametric.sh.template` (mirrored to `share/templates/`), same contract as the project resolver but evaluated at the GROUP level: collects the DISTINCT `<group>` values among filesystem-verified matches (`working_dir` AND `kanban_dir` both real directories, remainder matching `^([a-z0-9_]+)-([a-z0-9_]+)$`) and resolves only when exactly 1 distinct group exists — deliberately NOT instance-count-based, so a single client with TWO registered projects (`freelance-acme-widget`, `freelance-acme-gadget`) still resolves an unambiguous GROUP (`acme`) even though `_xaca0862_resolve_default_project()`, called separately once GROUP is known, correctly keeps refusing to guess between the two projects. Wired into the argument-parsing block ahead of the existing `<group>` positional check, gated identically (only resolves when no `<group>` was already supplied on the command line).

- **XACA-0862-023 ([Review] finding, fixed) — the co-change guard paired only 2 of the 3 independent implementations of one recipe, and the 3 already disagreed.** `install-team.sh`'s `_resolve_parametric_defaults()` TIER 2 applied NO filesystem gate at all (key-pattern match only); `render-cockpit-scripts.sh`'s `_resolve_cockpit_default_project()` gated on `working_dir` only; the template's runtime resolver gated on `working_dir` AND `kanban_dir` but validated the remainder with "no dash" rather than the anchored `[a-z0-9_]+` charset the other two enforce via their jq `test()` regex. Decision: the three answer the identical question ("does exactly one live, registered instance exist for this team/group") and disagreeing about it is the same drift class this ticket exists to end — UNIFIED rather than documented as intentional divergence. `_resolve_parametric_defaults()` TIER 2 now filters matches through the same `working_dir`+`kanban_dir` isdir gate as the other two (jq emits candidate+dirs, a bash loop applies the filesystem check — mirroring `render-cockpit-scripts.sh`'s existing pattern); the runtime resolver's python now validates the remainder with `re.match(r'^[a-z0-9_]+$', remainder)` instead of `"-" in remainder`. `check-resolve-cochange-guard.sh` extended from a single canonical↔mirror pairing to canonical↔N-mirrors (bash 3.2-compatible parallel arrays, no associative arrays): self-test now runs all 6 co-change assertions independently per mirror (18/18, was 6/6), gate mode reports each lagging mirror BY NAME rather than an undifferentiated failure. `.github/workflows/resolve-cochange-guard.yml` path filter extended to the template file.

- **XACA-0862-024 ([Review] finding, fixed) — the subitem-019 fix keyed TIER 1's exclusion off `CONNECT_ONLY`, which answers a different question than "was this `--project` argument synthesized by an automated caller".** `CONNECT_ONLY` means "render connect/disconnect only, skip the rest of install" — a real interactive user can answer that "yes" by hand-typing `install-team.sh finance --project business --connect-only` to deliberately refresh one team's scripts, and gating TIER 1 on `CONNECT_ONLY` would wrongly exclude that deliberate choice, even though only one known caller (the upgrade sweep) exists today. Replaced with a direct signal: `install-team.sh` now reads `AITEAMFORGE_CONNECT_REFRESH_SWEEP` (env var, internal contract — not a CLI flag, matching the `AITEAMFORGE_ALLOW_BOOTSTRAP_WRITE` convention) into `REFRESH_SWEEP`, and TIER 1 is gated on `[[ "$REFRESH_SWEEP" != "true" ]]` instead of `CONNECT_ONLY`. `aiteamforge-upgrade.sh`'s `update_connect_scripts()` now sets `AITEAMFORGE_CONNECT_REFRESH_SWEEP=true` alongside its existing `--connect-only --project` call (both env vars passed into the same subshell invocation); `CONNECT_ONLY` itself is untouched and keeps its original, independent meaning. New regression coverage in `tests/test-xaca-0862-team-scoped-connect.sh`: Case 4 drives the real 2-instance sweep shape (the exact defect 019 fixed) via a new `_run_install_sweep` helper; Case 4b is the discriminating contrast — the IDENTICAL 2-instance `--connect-only` refresh WITHOUT the sweep signal (a genuine hand-typed call) still correctly wins TIER 1 (last-processed wins, deliberately) — proving the exclusion is keyed to the direct signal, not to `--connect-only` itself. Verified via mutation: reverting the gate to `CONNECT_ONLY` flips Case 4b red (a hand-typed refresh gets wrongly excluded) while Case 4 stays green (both sweep calls happen to also pass `--connect-only`, so the two signals are indistinguishable in that shape alone) — exactly the "024's discriminator is a proxy, not a signal" gap the finding described, now closed.

- **XACA-0862-025 ([Review] finding, fixed) — regression tests for 016 and 020, which shipped without any.** New `T6J`/`T6K` in `tests/test-xaca-0845-connect-script-parametric-teams.sh` (36 → 38 passing), extracting+sourcing the two previously-unextracted `_xaca0862_020_known_scoped_out_ids`/`_xaca0862_020_is_known_scoped_out` helpers alongside the existing `update_connect_scripts` extraction (they were called by it but never sourced, silently no-op'ing as "command not found" until this fix). T6J: a SECOND `update_connect_scripts` run against an already-created team-scoped connect script reports `Refreshing`, never `Creating missing` again — T6/T6E/T6I above only ever assert the label on a single run and could not have caught the original 016 defect (the existence check was permanently checking the wrong, never-written filename). T6K: `dns` is downgraded to `print_info` and excluded from the `skipped` tally, while a genuinely unrecognised id (`ferengi-latinum`) in the SAME run still `print_warning`s and still counts — both halves asserted together so neither regresses without the other being exercised. Both mutation-tested: reverting 016's `base=` computation to `instance_id`-based flips T6J red; forcing the allowlist check to always return false flips T6K red; both restored and re-verified green.

- **XACA-0862-022 ([Review] finding, fixed) — the runtime resolver (`_xaca0862_resolve_default_project()`) had ZERO automated coverage before this: it is the actual fix for this ticket's originating bug, runs on every parametric connect invocation, and feeds a value into an SSH command string, guarded only by a PR reviewer's manual sandbox runs.** New `tests/test-xaca-0862-022-runtime-resolver.sh` (19 cases, registered in `tests/ci-manifest`), extracting `_xaca0862_resolve_default_project()` and the new `_xaca0862_resolve_default_group()` (see 026 below) VERBATIM via `awk` from the mirrored `share/templates/team-connect-parametric.sh.template` — same byte-for-byte extraction technique both PR reviewers used, kept tap-self-contained (extracts from the mirrored template rather than an outer-repo rendered `*-connect.sh`, so the suite has no dependency on files a plain `brew install aiteamforge` consumer would not have checked out). Covers: exactly-1-match resolution (P1), 0/2+-match refusal (P2/P3), missing/unreadable/malformed/non-dict/teams-not-dict registry (P4-P8), missing `kanban_dir`/`working_dir` (P9/P10), absent `python3` (P11, via a genuinely empty `PATH` override, not machine-dependent luck), a metacharacter-bearing registry key excluded post-023's anchoring fix (P12); the same battery for the group resolver (G1-G4, including the "1 client, 2 projects" case proving GROUP resolves while PROJECT correctly stays ambiguous); and 2 integration cases (I1/I2) driving the FULL rendered script through the CLI-argument path to prove a metacharacter-bearing `<project>`/`<group>` reaches `_validate_ident` and is rejected pre-network (exit 2, before any SSH activity — safe to execute for real in CI). Mutation-tested: 4 targeted SUT mutations (loosening the match-count check, dropping the `kanban_dir` gate, reverting the anchoring, loosening the group distinct-count check) flip exactly the 5 expected assertions red (P3, P9, P12, G1b, G3) with everything else unaffected; a separate mutation disabling both `_validate_ident` calls flips I1/I2 red. All mutations reverted and re-verified green (19/19).

- **XACA-0862 (architectural, user-approved) — `DEFAULT_PROJECT` on a parametric connect script is now resolved at RUNTIME, not baked at render time.** Root cause of subitem 019 (below) and of a latent hermetic-CI conflict: `render-cockpit-scripts.sh` already refuses to bake a non-empty default into dev-team's own committed cockpit scripts (a stateless CI checkout has no `~/.aiteamforge/`, so any non-empty bake would be permanent, unfixable drift) — so `finance-connect <host>` / `legal-connect <host>` with no arguments failed with a required-positional usage error even on a real machine with exactly one live, unambiguous instance. `scripts/templates/team-connect-parametric.sh.template` (mirrored to `share/templates/`) gains `_xaca0862_resolve_default_project()`: when the baked `DEFAULT_PROJECT` is empty and no `<project>` argument was supplied, it reads `~/.aiteamforge/team-paths.json` (honouring `AITEAMFORGE_CONFIG`) on the machine the script is actually running on, gated by the same filesystem-proof rule as subitem 021 below (`working_dir` AND `kanban_dir` must both exist as real directories) — exactly 1 match resolves it, 0 or >1 leaves it empty and the existing required-positional/usage-error path is unchanged (never guesses). Degrades to 0 matches on missing `python3`, a missing/unreadable/malformed registry, or any other failure — never crashes the connect script. Does NOT reintroduce render-time baking: the template substitution stays unconditionally empty for every parametric team's committed cockpit script, so `render-cockpit-scripts.sh --check` still reports 0 drift. Verified: `legal-connect <host>` resolves `coparenting` and `finance-connect <host>` resolves `personal` with no arguments on a machine with the real registry (sandboxed, never actually connects); a 2-instance team still exits 2 with the usage error.

- **XACA-0862-019 ([Review] finding, fixed) — the multi-instance ambiguity guard was defeated on the `aiteamforge upgrade` connect-script refresh path.** `update_connect_scripts()`'s per-instance work-list loop always passes an explicit `--project` to `install-team.sh --connect-only`, tripping `_resolve_parametric_defaults()`'s TIER 1 ("an explicit argument on this invocation is a genuine, deliberate (re)install") on every iteration — so a 2-instance team's refresh sweep baked a CONCRETE `DEFAULT_PROJECT` equal to whichever instance the work list happened to process LAST, instead of the intended empty/ambiguous default. TIER 1's premise does not hold when the caller is an unattended loop synthesizing the argument to identify which instance's `working_dir`/`kanban_dir` it is refreshing THIS iteration, not to state the team's new default. Fixed by gating TIER 1 on `[[ "$CONNECT_ONLY" != "true" ]]` — a genuine standalone `install-team.sh finance --project X` (no `--connect-only`) is unaffected, so Case 1/1b's "explicit re-install wins outright" contract is unchanged; a `--connect-only` sweep now always falls through to TIER 2 (registry-derived), matching the architectural fix above. Verified in the same sandbox the finding was reproduced in: two real full installs (`finance --project personal` then `finance --project business`) followed by two `--connect-only` refreshes (personal then business) now leave `DEFAULT_PROJECT=""` (ambiguous, 2 registered matches) instead of `"business"`; confirmed the pre-fix code (CONNECT_ONLY gate reverted) reproduces the original defect in the identical sandbox.

- **XACA-0862-016 ([Review] finding, fixed) — the 4th stale-message site from the team-scoping migration.** `aiteamforge-upgrade.sh`'s `update_connect_scripts()` computed `base="${instance_id}-connect.sh"` for its diagnostic/existence-check filename — stale since XACA-0862 team-scoped the renderer's output to `<team>-connect.sh`, never `<instance>-connect.sh`. For any parameterised instance (e.g. `legal-coparenting`) this named a file (`legal-coparenting-connect.sh`) that is never written, and the existence check `[ ! -f "${WORKING_DIR}/${base}" ]` against that wrong name NEVER found the real team-scoped file — so the "Creating missing" vs "Refreshing" label was permanently stuck on "Creating missing" for every parametric instance, even a pure refresh of an already-rendered script. Same defect class as the 3 `install-team.sh` echoes already fixed in subitems 004/005; this 4th site was missed. Fixed by computing `base` from `best_team` (the resolved template id) AFTER template matching, not from `instance_id`; the skip-warning messages that fire BEFORE `best_team` is known now name the recovered `instance_id` directly instead of guessing a filename.

- **XACA-0862-020 ([Review] finding, fixed) — a recurring warning for a permanent, intentional exclusion (`dns`).** `dns` has a real `working_dir`/`kanban_dir` (so source (c) correctly recovers it as a work-list candidate) but ships no `share/teams/dns.conf` (a separate, deliberate scope gap — see the plan doc's "Cause B") — so it was skipped downstream with `print_warning` on EVERY nightly unattended `aiteamforge upgrade` run, forever, alongside the "Skipped N unrecognised..." summary tally. A recurring warning for a defect that will never be fixed (there is nothing to fix) trains operators to tune out the exact channel that also reports a genuinely broken instance.
