Added a known-scoped-out allowlist (`_xaca0862_020_known_scoped_out_ids`, currently just `dns`) that downgrades the per-instance message to `print_info` and excludes it from the `skipped` tally, without silencing a genuinely unrecognised id (anything not on the list still warns and counts exactly as before).

- **XACA-0862-021 ([Review] finding, fixed) — source (c)'s `working_dir` isdir() gate was narrower than bare membership but not airtight.** `aiteamforge-team-paths-wizard.py --accept-defaults` writes every `DEFAULT_TEAMS` `working_dir` verbatim with NO existence check at all, so a never-installed team whose catalog-default path merely happens to already exist on a box (e.g. a directory created for an unrelated reason) would satisfy `isdir(working_dir)` alone and be wrongly materialised — the XACA-0845-008 failure mode in reduced form. `T6B`/`T6C` only ever covered entries with NO `working_dir` field at all, a coarser shape that trivially fails regardless of gate strength. Strengthened the gate in both `aiteamforge-upgrade.sh`'s `update_connect_scripts()` source (c) and the new architectural template resolver above to ALSO require `kanban_dir` to exist as a real directory — `install-team.sh`'s persist step writes `kanban_dir` only as part of a genuine install and unconditionally `mkdir -p`s it before `team-paths.json` is ever written, so a bare coincidental `working_dir` will essentially never already contain one. New regression cases `T6H`/`T6I` in `test-xaca-0845-connect-script-parametric-teams.sh`: `T6H` proves a registry entry with an existing `working_dir` but no `kanban_dir` materialises nothing; `T6I` (mandatory negative control) proves the identical fixture WITH `kanban_dir` present does materialise, so `T6H` is discriminating on the right signal, not vacuously rejecting everything. `T6E`/`T6G` updated to also supply a `kanban_dir` so they keep modelling a genuine install under the strengthened contract.

- **XACA-0862-018 ([Review] finding, fixed) — T6D's contract was weaker than its own comment claimed.** T6D asserted `isdir` appears ANYWHERE in the stripped `update_connect_scripts` source, not that it is co-located with the `team-paths.json` read — so a second, ungated bare-membership read added anywhere else in the function would still have passed, because source (c)'s own `isdir` call kept the whole-file substring present. Rewrote T6D to assert CO-LOCATION: every executable `team-paths.json` reference must have an `isdir` occurrence within a bounded line window (40) after it, in the comment-stripped source — factored into a reusable `_t6d_colocation_ok()` helper. Added `T6D-NC` (mandatory negative control): a synthetic copy of the real extracted function with one extra, deliberately ungated `team-paths.json` reference inserted far from the real gate — proves the co-location check actually rejects it, i.e. that T6D is not itself vacuous in the way the version it replaces was. Behavioural guard via T6B/T6C is unmodified.

- **XACA-0862-017 (tester-bot finding, VACUOUS-GREEN fix) — `tests/test-xaca-0862-006-retire-legacy-connect.sh` R5 and R7 no longer pass on the exact failure they exist to catch.** R7 extracted `retire_legacy_connect_scripts()`'s body via awk and grepped it for `rm`/`unlink`/`shred`/`find -delete`, but the extraction silently yields an EMPTY string when the function is absent (renamed/removed) — an empty body has no destructive commands in it either, so the test PASSED on the exact condition (function vanished) it was meant to protect against. Fixed with a positive control: R7 now asserts the extracted body is non-empty AND contains the `mv` this function actually uses to relocate legacy files, BEFORE checking for absence of delete primitives — an absence-assertion is only meaningful once presence is established. R5 discarded `_run_retire`'s exit code (`>/dev/null 2>&1` with no capture), so it passed even if retirement errored out entirely, as long as the untouched-files check still happened to hold; now captures and asserts `$? -eq 0` alongside the existing remaining-files check.

  **Verified with actual RED→GREEN transitions, not asserted.** R7: temporarily renamed `retire_legacy_connect_scripts` to `retire_legacy_connect_scripts_RENAMED` in the real `libexec/commands/aiteamforge-migrate.sh` (working tree only) — suite went RED with the new, specific message ("not found ... awk extraction returned empty ... function missing/renamed"); restored via backup copy, confirmed `git status` clean, suite GREEN again (7/7). R5: isolated from R7 by keeping the function present but injecting `return 1` as its first statement (files still untouched, since the injected return fires before any `mv`) — suite went RED on R5 specifically with `exit=1` while the remaining-files half of the assertion still held, proving the new exit-status check is load-bearing on its own; restored, confirmed `git status` clean, suite GREEN again (7/7).

  Also re-verified XACA-0862-015 (R3's cosmetic broken-pipe fix, entry below) independently: ran the suite with stdout/stderr captured to separate files — exit 0, empty stderr, 7/7 pass. Confirmed genuinely fixed rather than taking a prior agent's report on faith.

- **XACA-0862 (tester-bot finding, cosmetic) — `tests/test-xaca-0862-006-retire-legacy-connect.sh` R3 no longer risks a spurious "write error: Broken pipe" on stderr.** R3 asserted its captured dry-run output twice via `echo "$R3_OUT" | grep -qF "..."`; `grep -q` exits as soon as it finds its match, which can SIGPIPE the `echo` mid-write. The cosmetic error had no effect on the assertion's actual pass/fail result but was noisy in CI logs. Replaced with native bash `case "$R3_OUT" in *pattern*) ;; esac` substring matching — no subprocess, no pipe, nothing to SIGPIPE. Re-ran standalone with stdout/stderr captured separately: 7/7 pass, empty stderr.
- **XACA-0925 — `aiteamforge upgrade` now refreshes working-dir persona CONTENT; previously no function owned it, so personas froze at install time while the Cellar advanced with every release.** Measured on a consumer box immediately after upgrading 0.17.8 → 0.18.0: 9 of 11 teams stale, 54 persona files differing from their Cellar counterparts, working-dir mtimes stuck at 2026-06-05..06-08. `dns` and `mainevent` had no working-dir persona directory at all. Root cause was an ownership gap, not a broken function: the upgrade run sequence had 23 `update_*` calls and none of them touched `<team>/personas/agents/`. (`update_claude_hooks` deliberately declines to call `install_claude_config()` — correct scoping for a hooks-only function, left unchanged; nothing else picked personas up.) Fifth instance of the "install-time provisioning the upgrade path cannot reach" class, after XACA-0578, XACA-0594, XACA-0771 and XACA-0814.

  **Diagnosis.** 48 of the 54 stale files are exactly the set XACA-0671 realigned (tap commit `514b543`), plus finance's 5 from XACA-0785 and one stray ios file — so consumer working dirs were not vaguely old, they were pinned at a pre-June-2026 state holding two already-shipped fixes hostage. This also explains the observed `name:` divergence: stale copies carry TERMINAL names (`chambers`/`filings`/`research`) while the Cellar and canonical master carry ROLE names (`advocate`/`courtclerk`/`lawclerk`). Role names are canonical and the refresh IS the reconciliation — `AGENT_TERMINAL_*` in each team `.conf` is keyed on the frontmatter `name:`, so a file carrying `name: chambers` resolves `AGENT_TERMINAL_chambers`, which does not exist, and `install-team.sh` skips such personas entirely. The stale names were therefore already inert, not a live binding at risk; no alias shim is needed or wanted.

  **New `update_team_personas()`** (`libexec/commands/aiteamforge-upgrade.sh`), called after `update_runtime_helpers` and before `update_claude_hooks` — deliberately after `update_aux_scripts`, which owns `deploy-worktree-personas.sh`, the primary CONSUMER of the directory being refreshed; refreshing content ahead of its own deployer would push new personas through an old deployer. Copies `${FRAMEWORK_DIR}/share/personas/<team>/agents/*.md` over the working-dir copy **unconditionally**, matching what `bin/aiteamforge-setup.sh` already does on the `setup --upgrade` path — this closes a sibling-drift gap rather than introducing new clobber behaviour. Teams are enumerated from `.teams[]` in `.aiteamforge-config`, never a `share/personas/*/` glob, so upgrade never materialises persona trees for teams the box never installed. A configured team with no working-dir persona directory gets one created (the `dns`/`mainevent` case).

  **Comparison is `cmp`, never mtime/`-nt`, and that is load-bearing.** `kb-sync-personas` re-stamps deployed copies with the current date, so recency is not evidence of freshness — the mtime actively concealed this bug for two months. The same conclusion was already reached independently for a different file class elsewhere in this script. `cmp` gates only (a) whether the run has work and (b) whether to back up first — it never skips a file. A content-hash "skip if locally modified" rule was explicitly rejected: the 54 stale files DIFFER from the Cellar, that difference IS the staleness, so such a rule would classify all 54 as user-modified, skip every one, and ship an inert fix that reports success.

  **Safety.** Before the first write for a team, and only when at least one file differs, the existing directory is snapshotted to `~/aiteamforge-backups/personas/<team>/<YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS>/` — out of tree, never a `.backup-<ts>` sidecar, because `install-team.sh` programmatically enumerates that directory. Identical content is a true no-op: no write, no backup. Nothing is ever deleted; working-dir files with no Cellar counterpart survive and produce one summary warning per team (orphan reaping deliberately deferred). `DRY_RUN` short-circuits ahead of both the backup and every write. Per-team failures are isolated so one team cannot abort an unattended nightly upgrade.

  **Durability** (`tests/test-xaca-0925-persona-refresh.sh`, 18 cases; `tests/test-xaca-0925-persona-parity-check.sh`, 8 cases; both `plain-shell` in `ci-manifest`). Verified as an authentic RED→GREEN transition: 0 of 12 pass against the pre-fix script, 12 of 12 after. Coverage includes a test that plants stale content with an mtime NEWER than the Cellar — the exact shape `kb-sync-personas` creates — so any future reach for `-nt` fails. Three structural checks assert the function is defined, is called as a bare line, AND sits in the correct run-sequence position; "defined but never wired in" is the precise failure shape of this ticket and of XACA-0771 before it. New read-only `share/scripts/aiteamforge-persona-parity-check.sh` detects working-dir↔Cellar content drift on future releases; it is **not yet wired into `aiteamforge doctor` or CI** — the recommendation is documented in the script header and ships dormant until someone picks it up.

  **XACA-0925-013/014/015/016/017 ([Test]/[Review] findings against PR #745, fixed) — the fix's own "always returns 0" contract reproduced this ticket's failure shape one level down.** `_xaca0925_refresh_team_personas()` ran `mkdir`/`cp -R` (backup) and the per-file `cp` (write) unchecked, then unconditionally printed `[team] Updated N persona file(s)` and returned 0 — reproduced by chmod'ing one dest file to `444`: the run logged "Updated 2 persona file(s)" and returned 0 while that file stayed stale. Worse, the caller's `if ! _xaca0925_refresh_team_personas "$team"; then print_warning ...; fi` fail-soft branch was DEAD CODE, since the helper could never return non-zero.

  **Behavior change — a failed backup now blocks the write (XACA-0925-013/015, the important one).** The backup is the entire safety property the unconditional-clobber design rests on; a failed `mkdir`/`cp -R` on an unattended nightly run must not be followed by an overwrite with no way back. `_xaca0925_refresh_team_personas()` now checks `mkdir -p "$backup_dir"` and `cp -R "$dest" "${backup_dir}/agents"` explicitly and returns 1 (skipping the write entirely, dest left untouched) if either fails. The subsequent `mkdir -p "$dest"` and every per-file `cp` are also checked individually: the reported/returned count (`_XACA0925_TEAM_UPDATED`) is now the TRUE number of files actually written, never the attempted count, and a partial failure prints a named warning instead of a false success message. The helper's return code is now live — non-zero on any backup or write failure — which makes the caller's `if !` fail-soft branch reachable for the first time; one team failing still does not abort the run, and later teams still refresh (verified: a read-only dest file on one team logs the new warning and the next team still completes cleanly).

  **XACA-0925-014 (fixed) — an unreadable shipped persona source directory no longer logs identically to a genuinely empty one.** Added an explicit `[ -r "$src" ]` check between the "no shipped personas" (missing dir) and "no .md files" (empty dir) branches; a permission-denied source dir now prints a distinguishable warning instead of silently reading as "nothing shipped, working as intended."

  **XACA-0925-016 (fixed) — bounded backup retention.** `~/aiteamforge-backups/personas/<team>/` had no pruning and would grow one timestamped directory per team per differing run, forever, on a box whose personas legitimately diverge nightly. New `_xaca0925_prune_persona_backups()`, called after each successful backup: keeps the 10 most recent `<YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS>` directories per team (lexical == chronological sort), removes anything older, no-ops if the team's backup root doesn't exist yet, and is hard-scoped by a `case` guard to never remove a path outside that one team's own backup root. Deliberately a single "keep N most recent" tier rather than reusing `kanban-backup.py`'s tiered hourly/daily/weekly/monthly retention — that policy is sized for a much higher-frequency backup stream; this one is at most once per team per differing upgrade run, not on a fixed clock, so the simpler shape is a better fit and is documented as a deliberate choice in the source comment.

  **XACA-0925-017 (fixed) — team-id path-safety validation, defense-in-depth.** Team ids are read from `.aiteamforge-config` and interpolated into src/dest/backup paths; traversal was already heavily mitigated (needs a matching `share/personas/<id>/agents` source dir AND `$HOME` write access to do anything) but was never validated. New `_xaca0925_valid_team_id()` checks against `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]+$` before any path is built from the id; a non-conforming id is skipped with a warning (a deliberate skip, return 0 — not treated as a failure).

  **XACA-0925-023 (fixed) — the DETECTOR itself made a positive false claim of parity.** `[ -d "$src_dir" ] || continue` in `share/scripts/aiteamforge-persona-parity-check.sh` tested existence only, so a shipped persona directory that exists but is unreadable (`0300`/`0000`) or non-searchable (`0600`) made the `*.md` glob yield zero iterations — every team silently contributed no comparisons and the script reported **"Persona parity check passed"** and exit 0 **with real drift sitting unexamined**. Strictly worse than the 021 masquerade it mirrors: 021 said "nothing to check", this said "everything matches". Same class as 014/019, fixed in the FIXER but never mirrored into the DETECTOR — the asymmetry was one-sided, since the destination-side probe already failed closed in all four modes. A detector that cannot inspect its input must report a check FAILURE, never a silent skip and never a pass. Regression test T12 (parity suite 11 → 12) plants real drift behind a `0600` source dir and asserts a non-zero exit with a diagnostic that survives `--quiet`, and that the words "parity check passed" never appear; verified RED pre-fix, where it reproduced the silent false pass exactly (`rc=0`, empty output).


  **XACA-0925-018 ([Review] finding against the fix round, fixed — data safety).** The 013/015 guard aborts when the backup FAILS; an unreadable `dest` was nastier, because the backup was never ATTEMPTED. The gate `[ -n "$(ls -A "$dest" 2>/dev/null)" ]` cannot distinguish permission-denied from empty, so the whole backup block was SKIPPED and execution fell through to the write. Reproduced at mode `0300`: the destination was clobbered, **no backup was taken**, the helper returned 0, and it printed `Updated 1 persona file(s)` — the guard defeated before it could fire, and the failure mode is exactly the invisible-staleness-plus-false-success shape XACA-0925 exists to eliminate. Now an unreadable `dest` aborts that team explicitly, symmetric with the src-side check from 014: an unreadable directory is an error to report, never an empty set to proceed past. Regression test T19 (suite 18 → 19) asserts preserved content, an absent backup directory, a non-zero return, and no success message; verified genuinely RED against the pre-fix implementation, where it reproduced the clobber exactly (`rc=0`, content replaced, `backup_dir_exists=no`).

  **Durability of the fix itself** (same `tests/test-xaca-0925-persona-refresh.sh`, now 18 cases — T13-T18 added). Each of the 6 new cases was confirmed to genuinely fail against the pre-fix helper before passing against the fix: T13 (failed backup) and T14 (failed per-file cp) required routing the direct-invocation test harness through the same `if ! helper; then` calling convention production uses — under this repo's target `/bin/bash` 3.2, `set -e` is suppressed for a function's ENTIRE body when the function call is the direct condition of `if !`/`while`/negation, not just its top-level call; an earlier, unwrapped harness call let `set -e` abort the pre-fix helper early and made T13 false-pass for the wrong reason (an interpreter abort, not the documented "unconditional overwrite after a failed backup" bug). Fixed by wrapping the harness call identically to the real call site; re-verified RED (6/6 new cases fail; T1-T12 unaffected, still 12/12) against the pre-fix helper, then GREEN (18/18) against the fix. `tests/test-xaca-0925-persona-parity-check.sh` (8/8) and `tests/test-xaca-0771-upgrade-materialize-missing.sh` (12/12) re-run clean; real `~/aiteamforge-backups/personas` confirmed absent after every sandboxed run.

  **XACA-0925-019/020/021/022 ([Review] findings against PR #745 round 3, fixed) — the review bot deliberately hunted for further instances of ONE defect class and found four more.** The class, first named at 014/018: a precondition check that silently degrades to "proceed" (or "all clear") instead of "abort" when its input is unreadable or unparseable. An unreadable or unparseable input is an ERROR to report, never an empty set to proceed past. All four fixes below are instances of that same class, not four unrelated patches.

  **XACA-0925-019 (fixed) — the 014 src guard checked `-r` but not `-x`.** A source dir at mode `0600` (readable, not traversable) passed the `-r`-only guard, then the glob below failed `[ -f "$f" ]` for every entry — `stat()`/`open()` on a path INSIDE a directory needs the SEARCH (execute) bit on that directory; the read bit only permits `readdir()`, not resolving names to inodes — so it fell through to the generic "No .md persona files shipped" empty-set message. Verbatim the masquerade 014 exists to prevent, reached through a different bit. Now checks both `-r` and `-x`. Investigated whether the 018 `dest` guard needed the same treatment: empirically (macOS/BSD), a `dest` at mode `0600` does NOT fool the `ls -A` non-empty gate the way a fully-unreadable (`0300`, no read bit) `dest` does — `ls -A` still emits the entry names before erroring on the stat pass, so the existing `cp -R` failure path already catches a missing `-x` bit on `dest` and fails closed. Added the `-x` check to `dest` anyway, for a precise diagnostic instead of a generic "Backup ... failed partway" and to avoid ever attempting a doomed backup — not because the 018-style masquerade reproduces there.

  **XACA-0925-020 (fixed) — the 018 `dest` readability/accessibility guard sat AFTER the `DRY_RUN` early return.** `--dry-run` against an unreadable `dest` printed `Would update N persona file(s)` (success) and returned 0, while a real run on the identical fixture aborted with rc=1 (T19). A preview that contradicts what it previews is worse than no preview. Moved the guard (now `-r` OR `-x`) BEFORE the `DRY_RUN` early return, so a dry run reports the same abort a real run would. `DRY_RUN`'s existing no-write/no-backup guarantee is unaffected — the guard now simply returns 1 before either return path is reached.

  **XACA-0925-021 (fixed, highest priority of the four) — the parity checker (`aiteamforge-persona-parity-check.sh`) failed OPEN on a broken config.** `get_configured_teams()` (`lib/config.sh`) reads `.aiteamforge-config` via `jq -r '.teams[]? // empty' "$config_file" 2>/dev/null | tr '\n' ' '` — jq's own exit status is discarded by `2>/dev/null`, and the pipeline's reported exit status is `tr`'s (always 0), never jq's. An unreadable OR malformed config therefore came back exactly like a legitimately empty one: `"No configured teams found ... nothing to check"`, exit 0 — silent even under `--quiet`. That is the exact fail-open shape this script's own header proposes wiring into `aiteamforge doctor` plus a fleet cron alerting on exit 1 — which would have been permanently GREEN on exactly the machines that cannot prove themselves healthy. Rather than change `get_configured_teams()`'s shared fail-soft contract (also relied on by `aiteamforge-status.sh`, `aiteamforge-doctor.sh`, `aiteamforge-start.sh`, and `update_team_personas()` itself, all of which already treat any nonzero as "nothing configured" by design), the parity-check script now validates the config file itself, explicitly, before calling `get_configured_teams()` for extraction: missing config file stays the legitimate "nothing to check" (exit 0); an existing-but-unreadable config now prints a diagnostic to stderr UNCONDITIONALLY (bypassing `--quiet`) and exits 1; malformed JSON (or a `.teams` key that isn't an array) is now detected by checking `jq -e`'s own exit status directly — never inferred from `tr`'s — and also exits 1 with an unconditional diagnostic. A well-formed config with `"teams": []` remains the legitimate empty case: exit 0, silent under `--quiet` (regression-guarded, T11).

  **XACA-0925-022 (fixed) — a failed backup left a phantom empty timestamped directory behind.** When `mkdir -p "$backup_dir"` succeeds but `cp -R "$dest" "${backup_dir}/agents"` fails partway (e.g. one file inside an otherwise-accessible `dest` is individually unreadable — `ls -A` still reports `dest` as non-empty since `readdir()` doesn't need per-file read permission, so the backup IS attempted), `backup_dir` was left on disk containing zero files — indistinguishable from a genuine (if coincidentally empty) backup, and it counted toward `_xaca0925_prune_persona_backups`'s `keep=10` retention window exactly like a real one. A persistently failing nightly run could fill the window with empties and cause the next successful prune to evict real restore points. New `_xaca0925_cleanup_failed_backup_if_empty()`, called from both the `mkdir` and `cp -R` failure branches: removes ONLY the exact `$backup_dir` path the current invocation just constructed (never a glob, never anything pre-existing), and ONLY when confirmed to contain no regular files anywhere within it (`find "$dir" -type f -print -quit`) — a partially-successful backup (some files copied before the failure) is left in place rather than guessed at, since it might still be useful for manual recovery.

  **Durability of round 3** (`tests/test-xaca-0925-persona-refresh.sh` 19→22 cases, T20-T22; `tests/test-xaca-0925-persona-parity-check.sh` 8→11 cases, T9-T11). Each new case was verified as an authentic RED→GREEN transition against the pre-fix code (code changes stashed, test changes kept): T20 (src `0600`) failed with the generic empty-set message reproduced verbatim; T21 (`--dry-run` + unreadable `dest`) failed with `rc=0` and `Would update 1 persona file(s)` logged, exactly the false-preview defect described; T22 (single unreadable file inside an otherwise-accessible `dest`) failed with a leftover empty `<ts>/agents/` directory on disk after a `cp: ... Permission denied` backup failure; T9 (unreadable config, `--quiet`) failed with `rc=0` and empty output; T10 (malformed JSON) failed with `rc=0` and the misleading `"nothing to check"` message. T11 (valid config, zero teams) already passed pre-fix and stayed green post-fix, confirming the legitimate-empty case never regressed. All cases GREEN after the fix (22/22, 11/11); `shellcheck` clean on both modified scripts (identical to pre-change baseline — zero new findings); `ci-manifest-check.sh` still reports 71 entries; `test-xaca-0771-*.sh` still 12/12; real `~/aiteamforge-backups/personas` confirmed absent after every sandboxed run.

- **XACA-0862 (subitems 001-003) — parametric connect/disconnect scripts are now TEAM-scoped (`<team>-connect.sh`, not `<team>-<project>-connect.sh`), and `DEFAULT_PROJECT`/`DEFAULT_GROUP` are DERIVED from the team's registered instance(s) in `team-paths.json` instead of a static conf literal.** Settles the design question XACA-0845 deferred: the templates only ever substitute TEAM-level placeholders, so rendering `finance --project personal` vs `finance --project business` produced byte-identical scripts under the old `${INSTANCE_ID}-connect.sh` naming — only the filename pretended to be per-instance. Project (and, for freelance, group) selection now belongs in the argument, which the script already parsed.

  **Templates** (`share/templates/team-{connect,disconnect}-parametric.sh.template`, mirrored from canonical `scripts/templates/` per XACA-0340 — `sync-tap.sh --check` reports 0 drift after this change): rewrote the stale DEFAULT_GROUP comment that documented the old per-instance tap behaviour; added an explicit "no unambiguous default" guard on `<project>` (and `<group>`) in both HAS_GROUP branches — previously an empty `DEFAULT_PROJECT` fell through to `PROJECT=""` and a malformed `${TEAM_ID}-` instance id with no diagnostic; `usage()` now shows the positional as required (not `[optional]`) and omits a blank `(default: )` when no default is derivable.

  **Renderer** (`libexec/installers/install-team.sh`): `_render_connect_disconnect()`'s output filenames changed from `${AITEAMFORGE_DIR}/${INSTANCE_ID}-{connect,disconnect}.sh` to `${AITEAMFORGE_DIR}/${TEAM_ID}-{connect,disconnect}.sh`. The function has exactly one call site per invocation (the `--connect-only` early exit and the main install flow are mutually exclusive), so this is genuinely once-per-team, not a rename papering over a per-instance loop; installing a second instance of an already-installed team re-runs install-team.sh and re-renders (overwrites) the same team-scoped file, which is the intended one-file-per-team end state.

  New `_resolve_parametric_defaults()` derives `DEFAULT_PROJECT`/`DEFAULT_GROUP` at render time by pattern-matching the team's registered instance keys in `${AITEAMFORGE_CONFIG:-~/.aiteamforge/team-paths.json}` (component-count-anchored regex, so a group team's 2-component keys never match a non-group team's 1-component pattern). Fallback contract: **0 registered instances** → fall back to the instance being installed right now (`team-paths.json` is written by this installer only *after* this render runs, so on a team's first-ever install nothing is registered yet); **1 registered instance** → use it (fixes the field defect: `legal` baked `DEFAULT_PROJECT="default"` while the only live instance is `legal-coparenting`, so `legal-connect.sh <host>` with no args silently targeted a nonexistent `legal-default`); **>1 registered instances** → deliberately AMBIGUOUS, leave both empty rather than guess — a missing default fails loudly at the command line, a wrong guessed default fails later against a live but unintended instance. Verified end-to-end in a sandboxed `AITEAMFORGE_DIR`/`AITEAMFORGE_CONFIG` (never the real `~/.aiteamforge`): installing `legal --project custody` while only `legal-coparenting` is registered still bakes `DEFAULT_PROJECT="coparenting"` from the registry (proving derivation, not coincidence); zero-registered `freelance --client acme --project widgettracker` bakes both `DEFAULT_GROUP="acme"`/`DEFAULT_PROJECT="widgettracker"` from the install-time fallback; two registered `legal-*` instances bakes an empty default, logs a warning, and the rendered script hard-fails on a missing `<project>` with a clear message rather than a malformed instance id.

  Out of scope for this entry (tracked as XACA-0862 subitems 004-007): consumer-layout parity (6-of-11 teams gap), `aiteamforge upgrade` regeneration, migration of existing per-instance scripts, and the unwaivable tap parity gate + regression test.

- **XACA-0862 (subitem 007, DURABILITY) — regression test for the team-scoped-naming + derived-`DEFAULT_PROJECT` contract, and `test-xaca-0845-connect-script-parametric-teams.sh` updated in place for it.** Three prior tickets in this exact code path (XACA-0845, XACA-0814, XACA-0661) were all marked completed while the underlying defect remained observable in the field; this subitem is the mechanism that makes "completed" mean something here.

  New `tests/test-xaca-0862-team-scoped-connect.sh` drives the real `install-team.sh` end-to-end and asserts only observable OUTPUT (rendered filenames, a script's `DEFAULT_PROJECT=` line, `team-paths.json` content) — never an implementation symbol from subitems 001-003 — so it is a genuine acceptance test for the contract, not a mirror of one implementation of it. Covers: (1) a parametric team renders `<team>-connect.sh`/`<team>-disconnect.sh` with no instance-named twin; (1b) re-installing the same team with a different `--project` overwrites the team-scoped pair in place; (2) `DEFAULT_PROJECT` on a `--connect-only` re-render (no `--project` flag — exactly what a later regeneration does) is DERIVED from the already-registered instance in `team-paths.json`, proven for finance/legal/medical with a registered project deliberately different from each team's conf default, so the check cannot pass by coincidence; (3) the same two properties hold under a tap-CONSUMER directory layout (the doubled `libexec/` nesting Homebrew's Cellar produces), not only the flat dev-team layout every other suite in this repo exercises. Verified this suite FAILS (7 of 8 cases) against the pre-XACA-0862 codebase and PASSES (8 of 8) once subitems 001-003 land — an authentic RED→GREEN transition, not a synthetic one. Registered in `tests/ci-manifest` as `plain-shell`, auto-enrolling it into `.github/workflows/tests.yml`'s manifest-driven `test-shell-homebrew-tap` job.

  Case 1b's derivation check surfaced a genuine, narrow ordering defect in subitem 003's `_resolve_parametric_defaults()`: it reads `team-paths.json` BEFORE this install's own persist step runs, so a SECOND full install of an already-registered parametric team with a NEW `--project` value bakes the STALE prior project instead of the just-supplied one. Filed as a `[Review]` subitem on XACA-0862 for follow-up rather than silently weakened — a QA suite that hides a bug it found to stay green defeats the point of this ticket.

  `test-xaca-0845-connect-script-parametric-teams.sh` updated in place (not duplicated) for the six cases whose assertions hardcoded the now-retired instance-scoped filename (`finance-personal-connect.sh` etc.): T1/T2/T3/T4 (direct `install-team.sh` renders), T6 (upgrade-path union render, transitively affected since it calls the same renderer), T10 (freelance client+project composition, same reason). S1's structural predicate-branching check was also de-brittled — it previously grepped a fixed 6-line window after `_render_connect_disconnect()`'s definition, which subitem 002's added rationale comment pushed the `_is_parametric_team` call past, producing a false failure with no change to the actual branching logic; it now scans the full function body via the same awk-bounded technique this file already used for S3.

- **XACA-0862 (subitem 007) — new unwaivable tap-parity CI gate: `scripts/check-tap-connect-disconnect-parity.sh` (outer dev-team repo), wired as job `tap-connect-disconnect-parity` in `.github/workflows/sync-tap-check.yml` alongside `sync-tap-drift` and `tap-changelog-completeness`.** Renders EVERY team this tap ships a conf for (all 9: academy, android, command, finance, firebase, freelance, ios, legal, medical) via the real `install-team.sh --connect-only` in a throwaway sandbox and asserts each produces both a non-empty, executable connect AND disconnect script. Deliberately carries **no bypass trailer and no bypass label** — unlike the two sibling tap gates, every prior failure in this path was a missing script, not a legitimately-scoped edge case, so an escape hatch would just be a fourth way to ship the same regression. Self-check mode (`--self-check`) proves the verification logic itself is not vacuous, independent of the renderer: synthesizes three teams' worth of stub scripts (GREEN), deletes one team's connect script (RED, naming that exact team), restores it (GREEN) — observed run: GREEN → RED (`FAIL: beta — missing/empty/non-executable connect script`) → GREEN.

- **XACA-0862-014 (review finding above, fixed, then CORRECTED same-day) — first fix (union `INSTANCE_ID` into the candidate set) made an explicit re-install of a second project AMBIGUOUS instead of letting it win; replaced with an explicit-argument-always-wins tier ahead of the registry lookup.** The union fix closed the staleness gap but broke the ALREADY-PASSING subitem 007 acceptance suite: Case 1b expects `finance --project business` then `finance --project ops` to flip `DEFAULT_PROJECT` to `"ops"` outright (an explicit re-install is a deliberate choice, not an ambiguous one, even though `business` remains a separate live instance) — the union fix instead made two distinct EXPLICIT instances collide and rendered an empty default, which is a real regression the test suite caught before this reached a PR. `_resolve_parametric_defaults()` now resolves in two tiers: **TIER 1** — if the caller passed `--project` (and, for group teams, `--client`) on THIS invocation (`ARG_PROJECT`/`ARG_CLIENT`, set only from the CLI flags), that explicit instance wins outright, no registry lookup, no ambiguity check. **TIER 2** — no explicit `--project` (a `--connect-only` regen/re-render from `aiteamforge upgrade` or the setup wizard, which never supply one): `RESOLVED_PROJECT` in this branch came from the untrustworthy `TEAM_DEFAULT_PROJECT` conf literal, so derive from what's actually REGISTERED instead — 0 registered → fall back to the conf-literal-derived value; 1 registered → use it (the legal-coparenting fix, for the no-argument path); >1 registered → deliberately ambiguous, empty default. Re-verified: subitem 007's full 8-case suite (`tests/test-xaca-0862-team-scoped-connect.sh`) now passes 8/8 (was 3/8 under the union fix, matching the reviewer's own re-run); the updated `tests/test-xaca-0845-connect-script-parametric-teams.sh` passes 28/28 of its connect/disconnect-relevant cases (2 unrelated pre-existing failures — D3/D4, `aiteamforge doctor --check connect`'s CLI dispatch — predate this ticket by three weeks, commits `0a8766c`/`0acefce` from 2026-07-24, and belong to subitem 006's scope, not this fix).

- **XACA-0862 (subitem 006) — `aiteamforge doctor --check connect` updated for team-scoped naming, plus a new one-shot `aiteamforge migrate --retire-legacy-connect-scripts` to retire the PRE-XACA-0862 per-instance files it now correctly reports as orphaned.** `check_connect_scripts()` still composed its "installed" identifier the pre-XACA-0862 way (`<base>-<project>` / `<base>-<client>-<project>`), so once subitems 001-003 landed it diverged from what the renderer actually writes: every parametric team's team-scoped script (`finance-connect.sh`) read as a false "missing", while the OLD per-instance file, if still on disk, read as a false "present". Fixed by discovering PARAMETRIC bases (`TEAM_HAS_PROJECTS="true"` AND a shipped `share/scripts/teams/<base>-startup.sh` — the identical predicate `_is_parametric_team()` uses in `install-team.sh`) and collapsing their expected identifier to the bare team id; non-parametric bases are unaffected. These were exactly the two pre-existing D3/D4 failures subitem 014's entry above named and deferred here; both now pass under the new contract (verified: 30/30 in `test-xaca-0845-connect-script-parametric-teams.sh`, 8/8 in `test-xaca-0862-team-scoped-connect.sh`).

  **The widen-the-glob trap, deliberately avoided.** The obvious retirement mechanism is to widen the XACA-0483 stale-migration glob in `install-team.sh` (the one that moves legacy `*-startup.sh`/`*-shutdown.sh` aside on every re-install) to also sweep connect/disconnect. XACA-0845 deliberately NARROWED that exact glob to stop doing precisely that — at the time connect/disconnect were themselves instance-keyed, so a recurring install-time sweep would move every freshly-rendered script aside on every single re-install, manufacturing churn and re-creating the "live team has no connect script" state XACA-0845 exists to fix. The team-scoped filename shape (`finance-connect.sh`, one dash) does not collide with the legacy glob shape (`finance-*-connect.sh`, two dashes) — so widening it would not literally re-trigger 0845's bug — but it would still make a one-time cleanup into a permanent, recurring, install-coupled mechanism, in a file outside this subitem's ownership. Chosen instead: a genuinely one-shot, explicitly-invoked migration (`aiteamforge migrate --retire-legacy-connect-scripts`), never run automatically from install or upgrade.

  **Safety.** Never deletes — every retired file is `mv`d into `~/aiteamforge-backups/xaca-0862-connect-migration-<timestamp>/`, never `rm`d. Only retires a legacy file when its team-scoped replacement (`<base>-connect.sh`/`<base>-disconnect.sh`) already exists on disk — a team that has not yet re-installed/upgraded since XACA-0862 keeps its only script. Anchored per known team id from `share/teams/*.conf` (never a bare prefix match, so a short id cannot swallow a longer one). Idempotent by construction: `mv` empties the source, so a second run finds nothing to move — no state file needed. `aiteamforge-doctor.sh`'s own connect check stays report-only (test S4 still pins no `rm`/`unlink`/`shred`/`find -delete` path there); its orphan message for a superseded legacy file now names the specific replacement and points at the new migrate flag instead of a generic "remove by hand".

  Verified in a sandboxed `AITEAMFORGE_DIR` (never the real `~/.aiteamforge`): built a mixed-state box (4 parametric teams each with both an old per-instance pair and a new team-scoped pair on disk) — `doctor --check connect --verbose` before retirement reports 5 present + 4 orphans, each orphan carrying the new "Superseded by team-scoped `<base>-connect.sh`" hint; `migrate --retire-legacy-connect-scripts --dry-run` previews exactly the 8 legacy files with no filesystem change; the real run moves exactly those 8 into a timestamped backup dir and leaves the 9 team-scoped/non-parametric files in place; a second real run finds nothing left to retire (exit 0); `doctor --check connect` afterward reports 6/6 passed, 0 warnings. A separate sandbox proved the safety guard: a team with ONLY the legacy file (no team-scoped replacement rendered yet) is left completely untouched by the migration.

- **XACA-0862 (subitem 006, QA VERIFICATION + regression coverage) — independently re-verified the above `check_connect_scripts()`/`retire_legacy_connect_scripts()` implementation (inherited uncommitted from a stood-down session) and added `tests/test-xaca-0862-006-retire-legacy-connect.sh`, the suite that was missing for it.** Neither `test-xaca-0862-team-scoped-connect.sh` nor `test-xaca-0845-connect-script-parametric-teams.sh` exercised the `--retire-legacy-connect-scripts` flag at all — the only prior evidence for it was the hand-run sandbox narrative in the paragraph above, which is a claim, not a gate. Re-ran both existing suites independently under `/bin/bash` (3.2): 8/8 and 30/30, confirming the D3/D4 flips are the new team-scoped-identifier contract taking effect, not a masked assertion. Reproduced the field scenario in a fresh sandbox from scratch (never the real `~/.aiteamforge`/`~/aiteamforge`): a lone `finance-personal-{connect,disconnect}.sh` pair alongside the rendered `finance-connect.sh`/`finance-disconnect.sh` — `doctor --check connect` starts at 1 warning, `migrate --retire-legacy-connect-scripts` moves (never `rm`s) exactly the legacy pair into a timestamped `~/aiteamforge-backups/` directory, and `doctor --check connect` afterward reports 0 warnings / "All checks passed", matching the live M4 Mini orphan this subitem exists to clear. `shellcheck -x` on both changed files: no correctness findings, two pre-existing/minor style notes only (SC2129 predates this change; SC2155 on the new `backup_dir` assignment is cosmetic — the masked `$(date ...)` exit status is never checked or needed). New suite (7 cases, registered in `tests/ci-manifest`): R1/R2 doctor's orphan-hint text (replacement-named vs. generic, so a team with no rendered replacement never points at a migration with nothing to retire it into); R3 `--dry-run` mutates nothing; R4 (headline) a real run retires exactly the legacy pair, backs it up, and leaves `doctor --check connect` at 0 warnings; R5 the no-replacement-yet safety guard; R6 idempotency; R7 a static scan of `retire_legacy_connect_scripts()`'s own function body for any `rm`/`unlink`/`shred`/`find -delete` path (none found), the same S4-style invariant `aiteamforge-doctor.sh`'s connect check is already pinned to. Combined connect-family total: 45/45 (7 + 8 + 30) via `tests/test-runner.sh`.

- **XACA-0862 (subitems 004/005) — `aiteamforge upgrade` now materialises connect/disconnect scripts for a parametric instance registered AFTER a box's one initial-setup run (Cause A of the "6-vs-11 gap"); plus a small `install-team.sh` reporting cleanup left over from subitem 002.** `update_connect_scripts()` (`libexec/commands/aiteamforge-upgrade.sh`) already unioned two sources — on-disk `*-connect.sh` files, and `.aiteamforge-config`'s `.teams[]` — but `.aiteamforge-config` is written ONCE, by `bin/aiteamforge-setup.sh`, from that one run's `SELECTED_TEAMS`, and never updated again. A parametric instance added later (a second project registered by re-running `install-team.sh` directly, or via the team-paths wizard, without re-running full setup) reaches `team-paths.json` — `install-team.sh`'s own XACA-0463 persist step runs on every full install — but never touches `.aiteamforge-config`. Neither existing source could ever see it, so its connect script stayed missing through every future upgrade, permanently. This was the live field gap on M4 Mini (tap 0.18.0, the then-current release): `legal-coparenting` and `medical-general` were both registered, both had a shipped conf, both had a real `working_dir` on disk — and both were structurally invisible to this function.

  **A third work-list source, gated by filesystem proof, not bare registry membership.** `update_connect_scripts()` now also reads `team-paths.json`/`AITEAMFORGE_CONFIG`, but a candidate is only added to the work list if its recorded `working_dir` exists as a real directory on disk (`os.path.isdir`). This is deliberately NOT the approach XACA-0845-008 already tried and reverted — that attempt trusted bare key presence as proof of setup, and was wrong: `aiteamforge-team-paths-wizard.py` can write an entry for a team never installed (its "path does not exist — use it anyway?" prompt defaults YES; `--accept-defaults` writes the full catalog verbatim with no existence check at all). Gating on `working_dir` existence closes that hole: `install-team.sh`'s persist step only ever runs after `mkdir -p`'ing the working/kanban dir on a genuine full install, so a real instance's directory is guaranteed present, while a wizard-catalogued-but-never-installed entry's is not. `mainevent` needs no special case — its registry entry (XACA-0727: deliberately board-less) carries the literal sentinel string `"null"` as `working_dir`, which fails the same directory-existence test as any other absent path.

  **Cause B (dns, mainevent — no shipped conf) is deliberately NOT closed by this fix, and that is a considered decision, not an oversight.** `mainevent` is architecturally board-less by design (XACA-0727) — there is no working directory to render a connect script against, so exclusion is correct, permanent behaviour, not a gap. `dns` is a real team with a real working directory, but ships no `share/teams/dns.conf` and is not even listed in `share/teams/registry.json`'s installable catalog; the connect-script templates substitute `TEAM_AGENTS`/`AGENT_WINDOWS_*` directly into the rendered script, so authoring a conf requires genuine DNS-team persona/session data (bridge-station names, tmux window layout) this ticket does not have — fabricating it risks shipping a connect script with wrong session names, a worse outcome than not shipping one. A `dns` registry entry is still recovered as a work-list candidate by the new source (proven by test T6G below) but skipped downstream with a "no team template matches" warning — the same non-fatal, non-silent handling any other id with no matching template already receives. Left for a separate, deliberately-scoped follow-up ticket with real DNS-team input.

  **`install-team.sh` reporting cleanup (XACA-0862-002 leftover).** Three `echo` lines still announced the pre-team-scoping `${INSTANCE_ID}-connect.sh` / `${INSTANCE_ID}-disconnect.sh` paths — files that no longer exist since subitem 002 renamed the renderer's output to `${TEAM_ID}-*`. A parametric install's success message named a file that was never written. Fixed at the two `_render_connect_disconnect()` echo sites (flat-template branch) and the end-of-install summary line to report `${TEAM_ID}-*`, matching what the code actually writes.

  **Regression coverage.** `tests/test-xaca-0845-connect-script-parametric-teams.sh`'s T6D — originally a blanket "must never reference team-paths.json/AITEAMFORGE_CONFIG" structural ban from XACA-0845-008 — is revised to assert the NARROWER, now-correct contract: the registry may be read, but only ever paired with a directory-existence gate (`isdir`) in the same source; `.aiteamforge-config` must still be read too. T6A/T6B/T6C (unchanged) continue to prove a registry entry with no `working_dir` field — their exact fixture shape — still materialises nothing. Three new cases added: T6E (headline) — a registry entry NOT in `.aiteamforge-config`, with a `working_dir` that genuinely exists on disk, gets `legal-connect.sh` created (the actual Cause-A field gap, proven end-to-end through the real installer, not asserted from reading the code); T6F — `mainevent`'s `"null"`-sentinel entry materialises nothing, no special-case code required; T6G — a `dns` entry with a real `working_dir` is recovered then skipped with the expected warning, never silently dropped. Verified: `tests/test-xaca-0845-connect-script-parametric-teams.sh` 33/33 (was 30/30; 3 new cases, 0 regressions), `tests/test-xaca-0862-team-scoped-connect.sh` 8/8 — both run under `/bin/bash` (3.2), not PATH bash 5.x.

## [0.18.0] - 2026-08-12

- **XACA-0920 (protected-subitem round) — re-mirrored `share/lcars-ui/js/lcars.js` and `share/lcars-ui/tests/test_xaca0920_copy_to_clipboard.js`; bumped `share/lcars-ui/index.html`'s cache-buster to `?v=3.58`.** Resolves the five PR #743 review-gate subitems: (013/016) the guard `if (!text) return Promise.resolve(false)` made the relnotes COPY button a silent dead click — now emits a `showToast('Nothing to copy', 'info')`, guarded on `null`/`undefined`/`''` only (NOT a bare falsy check, since `copyToClipboard(item.id || index)` can legitimately pass the Number `0`); (015) `attemptExecCommandFallback()`'s `textarea.setSelectionRange(0, text.length)` assumed a string — a Number `text` (same `item.id || index` callers) made `.length` `undefined`, collapsing the selection to `(0,0)` and copying nothing; `text` is now coerced once via `const value = String(text)` and every downstream use (toast, `textarea.value`, `setSelectionRange`) reads `value`; (014) the failure-toast diagnostics were shortened to two discriminating one-liners — `Copy failed: ${err.name} (focus=${hasFocus}) — see console` (API rejected) and `Copy failed: clipboard unavailable — see console` (API absent) — while the FULL payload (err name/message, focus, visibility, fallback reason) stays in `console.error`/`console.warn`, since the cockpit is an iTerm2 WKWebView where a dev console may be unreachable and these diagnostics are the only mechanism for field-diagnosing this ticket's still-unconfirmed root cause; console-only (the subitem's literal recommendation) was deliberately NOT done, as it would have discarded that signal entirely. Test suite grew from 12 to 16 cases (Number coercion regression guard, `0`-is-valid-content guard, `null`/`undefined`/`''` toast, console-payload assertions for the shortened toasts); all pass. Verified byte-identical to canonical post-copy; `index.html`'s two sibling-owned lines (`lcars-cr-tab.css?v=1.2`, `lcars-cr-tab.js?v=1.3`) untouched.

- **XACA-0920 — mirrored `share/lcars-ui/js/lcars.js`: `copyToClipboard()` two-tier fallback chain.** Canonical's clipboard-copy path gained a Tier 1 (async Clipboard API) / Tier 2 (legacy `execCommand('copy')`) fallback chain: control now falls through to the legacy path on either an absent Clipboard API OR a rejected `writeText()` promise, where previously a rejection had no recovery and the operation just failed silently. This copy carries the full two-tier `copyToClipboard(text, options)` helper — including the `options.successMessage` override (used so the release-notes copy path doesn't echo a multi-line blob back into the toast) and the return-value contract (`Promise<boolean>` resolving true only on a confirmed copy, never rejecting) — plus the RELNOTES copy call site now routed through the shared helper instead of calling `navigator.clipboard.writeText()` directly. **Scoped, single-file copy — NOT a `sync-tap.sh --commit` run.** `./sync-tap.sh --check --source-dir <worktree> --reference-dir <worktree>` reported 7 files drifted; the other 6 (`share/lcars-ui/index.html`, `css/lcars-cr-tab.css`, `js/lcars-cr-tab.js`, `tests/test_cr_tab_publish_regression.js`, a new XACA-0895 test file, and `share/scripts/kb-cr.sh`) are pre-existing drift from XACA-0297/XACA-0895/XACA-0896 in-flight work unrelated to this ticket — a blanket sync would have clobbered that sibling state, the same hazard XACA-0297's entries above document repeatedly for this same tap. Verified byte-identical to canonical `lcars-ui/js/lcars.js` post-copy.

- **XACA-0920 (corrective follow-on) — one cache-buster line in `share/lcars-ui/index.html`, plus mirroring the new test file, both left out of the entry above.** The `lcars.js` mirror above shipped the new `copyToClipboard()` code, but the tap's `index.html` still referenced `js/lcars.js?v=3.56` — canonical had bumped to `?v=3.57` (a repo pre-commit hook enforces the bump whenever `lcars.js` changes) — so tap consumers would keep serving their cached pre-fix script indefinitely: merged but not shipped. This is a **surgical one-line edit**, not a copy of canonical's `index.html`: the tap's copy is behind canonical on two sibling-owned lines (`css/lcars-cr-tab.css?v=1.2` vs canonical `1.3`, `js/lcars-cr-tab.js?v=1.3` vs canonical `1.4`, both XACA-0297/0895/0896 in-flight work) and those are deliberately left untouched — confirmed the resulting diff against the pre-edit tap copy touches exactly one line. Also adds `share/lcars-ui/tests/test_xaca0920_copy_to_clipboard.js` (byte-identical to canonical), mirroring the pattern of the tap's other already-mirrored JS tests (`test_cr_age_helpers.js`, `test_cr_metrics.js`, `test_cr_tab_publish_regression.js`) — the test existed canonically at the time of the entry above but was not yet copied. `sync-tap-drift` will still report `index.html` (on its two untouched sibling lines) as drifted; that remainder is expected and not this ticket's to fix.

- **XACA-0297-048 — targeted port of the 2 blocks that never reached `share/scripts/kb-cr.sh`; the fix was merged but only partly shipped.** Measured against gitlink `ddaca83`: 76 of 85 canonical lines were present, 9 missing. Deliberately NOT a re-mirror — the tap copy is mirror-AHEAD with XACA-0896's Guard 4 work while PR #737 is still open, and a sweep would clobber it; both hunks were diffed function-by-function first and the port asserts it touches no `_kb_cr_set_publish_stamps` / `publish_stamp` content (verified unchanged after: 6 / 29 / 7 occurrences). **Hunk 1** restores the `|| return 1` on `_kb_cr_lifecycle_advance`'s `_kb_jq_update`: without it the function's status came from the trailing `|| true` on the activity append, so it ALWAYS returned 0 — a failed board write reported success and every caller's guard was decorative. **Hunk 2** restores the `cr-drafted` branch's `cr_state_changed` emission: the LCARS endpoint stopped appending its own event and now relies on this helper, so on tap machines a transition to `cr-drafted` produced NO event at all — a silent hole in the activity feed, which is the same log this ticket's backfill tool reads to recover lost timestamps. **Verified by running the real 14-case `cr-transition-timestamps.bats` against the TAP copy** (scratch repo root with `scripts/kb-cr.sh` = the tap file), not by counting lines: 13/14 pass. The one failure is `cr-published stamps cr_published_at`, which is NOT this port's scope — **`cr-published` is absent from this file entirely** (0 occurrences here vs 32 in canonical), XACA-0895's state having reached the tap in `server.py` but never in `kb-cr.sh`. Tracked separately; that gap means a release cut from this state rejects `cr-published` as an invalid state.

- **XACA-0297 (re-mirror) — `share/lcars-ui/server.py` was stale by exactly one commit; also carries the field-update fatal guard.** The previous mirror (`de61acb`) was taken BEFORE the commit that extracted `_build_cr_field_update_shell_parts()`, so the tap copy still held the pre-extraction inline block and lacked the new builder entirely. Caught by review round 6 by diffing the gitlink blob against canonical rather than trusting the drift gate's label. **A mirror is valid as of a COMMIT, not as of a ticket** — this one was accurately reported as done and then quietly became false when one more commit touched the file. This copy is verified byte-identical to canonical. It also brings the round-6 correctness fix: the field-update `_kb_jq_update` had no `|| exit`, so a failed write was masked by the activity appends after it and the endpoint answered 200 `ok:true` with the state written and the fields silently dropped. `share/scripts/kb-cr.sh` remains deliberately unmirrored (XACA-0896's in-flight Guard 4 work); all 6 of its publish-stamp references verified intact.

- **XACA-0297 — mirrored `share/lcars-ui/server.py`: CR transitions stamp their lifecycle timestamp, and the shell assembly is extracted so tests can call it.** The endpoint's transition handler now routes through the shared `_kb_cr_stamp_state_entry` helper instead of an inline `_kb_jq_update` that wrote `crState` + `updatedAt` and stamped no `cr_*_at` at all. Measured before the fix across the three live mobile boards: 24 CRs in `cr-closed` carrying only 3 `cr_closed_at` values, and **0 of 26** carrying both `deploy_window_planned` and `cr_deployed_prod_at` — the two inputs to the bi-weekly summary's estimate-accuracy section, which had therefore been rendering empty. The assembly is extracted as `LCARSHandler._build_cr_transition_shell_parts()` so tests call the production code rather than a hand-written mirror; a mirrored harness let six of eight planted defects through, including swapped positional arguments that would have stamped the wrong field. This copy also carries XACA-0895's `cr-published` work, which is on develop but had not reached the tap (12 references verified present after the copy). It was checked for unmerged sibling content first: every tap-only line relative to develop was either a stale pre-`cr-published` version or this ticket's own earlier mirror — the only ticket id in that content is XACA-0297 — so the wholesale copy destroys nothing.

  **`share/scripts/kb-cr.sh` is DELIBERATELY NOT mirrored, and the resulting `sync-tap-drift` failure on it is expected rather than an oversight.** The tap copy carries XACA-0896's in-flight `_kb_cr_set_publish_stamps` / `_kb_cr_get_publish_stamps` Guard 4 plumbing across 27 sites in six regions — dispatcher, help text, the publish flow, two function bodies and the CLI help. It is published to consumers here but absent from develop. A wholesale copy deletes all of it; hand-grafting it would place a sibling's unmerged work into a tap commit under this ticket's name. Verified after this edit that all 6 publish-stamp references remain intact. This is the same wall XACA-0895 documented and conceded to for the same file. It reconciles when XACA-0896 merges; until then no ticket can mirror `kb-cr.sh` without either destroying or appropriating that work, which is worth fixing at the source: mirroring unmerged work into the shared tap blocks clean mirrors for everyone.

- **XACA-0297-010 — mirrored the `kb-cr summary` section list so it names View 4 (Standard-Change-Eligible).** `share/scripts/kb-cr.sh` help text only: section 9 becomes Standard-change-eligible and Footnotes becomes 10, matching canonical after View 4 was wired into the summary assembler.

  **Deliberately a one-line mirror.** An audit of what this ticket actually changed found that only two of its files are in the sync manifest at all — `scripts/kb-cr.sh` (`sync_file`) and `lcars-ui/server.py` (via the `lcars-ui` `sync_dir`). `kb-cr-audit.py`, `kb-cr-audit-render.py`, `cr-lifecycle-monitor.py` and `kb-cr-backfill-timestamps.py` are **not** mirrored, so View 4's generator, the audit renderer and the Auto 3 grace-period tuning are canonical-only and ship nothing here. `share/lcars-ui/server.py` required no change: XACA-0297-006's `_kb_cr_stamp_state_entry` call was already mirrored (5 references both sides) and every remaining difference against canonical is XACA-0895's `cr-published` work, which that ticket deliberately left unmirrored. `share/scripts/kb-cr.sh` likewise still carries XACA-0896's in-flight `_kb_cr_set_publish_stamps` / `_kb_cr_get_publish_stamps` Guard 4 plumbing (verified: all 6 references intact after this edit), and its wider divergence from canonical is XACA-0895's to reconcile, not this ticket's. A blanket `sync-tap.sh` run was therefore NOT performed — it would have carried one ticket's work under another's name and clobbered a third's.

  **Pre-existing gap noted, not fixed here:** the tap mirrors `kb-cr`'s `summary --help` section list but ships neither `kb-cr-audit.py` nor `cr-lifecycle-monitor.py` (only `cr-confluence-poller.py` is in the manifest), so consumers see a report described that the tap cannot generate. Out of scope for XACA-0297; flagged for whoever owns tap packaging.

- **XACA-0775 (PR #739 review follow-ups) — closed the upgrade-path gap the `has_iterm_gui` refactor opened, and stopped the source guard from swallowing real errors.**
  **`lcars-launch-helpers.sh` added to `_xaca0673_mandatory_materialize_basenames()`.** The rendered `*-connect.sh` scripts now CALL `has_iterm_gui` rather than inlining the probe, so that helper became a runtime dependency of connect. It reaches a machine three ways and only two were covered: `aiteamforge-setup.sh` copies `share/scripts/*` unconditionally, so both `setup` call sites of `install-team.sh --connect-only` are fine — but the third call site is `update_connect_scripts()` (XACA-0814), reached from `aiteamforge upgrade`, which never runs setup's copy step. Under `update_runtime_helpers()`'s default "only refresh what's already there" rule, an upgrade could therefore hand a machine a freshly-rendered connect script depending on a helper the same run declined to create. Failure is quiet rather than fatal (`has_iterm_gui` → command-not-found, the `if` takes its else branch, connect completes via the Terminal.app fallback) — it simply reports "iTerm2 not running" on a machine where iTerm2 is running. Materialising here rather than special-casing the connect-only early exit (the literal review suggestion) also covers `team-startup.sh`, which has hard-depended on this same file since XACA-0563 — same one-line cost, strictly wider coverage. Sixth sighting of the install-vs-upgrade gap class (XACA-0608 / 0673 / 0751 / 0771 / 0814).
  **Dropped `2>/dev/null` from the template's source line and added an else-warn branch.** The `[[ -f ]]` guard already proves the file exists, so the only errors that redirect could suppress were real ones — a parse error or truncated file — and swallowing them made the failure resurface ~150 lines later as a misleading "iTerm2 not running". `|| true` alone preserves best-effort. The new `else` branch names the missing file and points at `aiteamforge upgrade`, so the degraded path announces itself instead of silently misreporting. Deliberately NOT a fallback re-definition of `has_iterm_gui`: that would be a third copy of the same predicate in a ticket about duplicated content drifting from its source.

- **XACA-0775 — back-ported three rendered-only fixes into the flat `share/templates/team-connect.sh.template` and `share/templates/team-disconnect.sh.template`, so `scripts/render-cockpit-scripts.sh` stops silently reverting them on the 5 flat teams (academy, android, command, firebase, ios).** These templates are tap-native (tracked only inside `homebrew-tap/`, no dev-team canonical copy) but the renderer's committed output had drifted ahead of them three separate times (XACA-0785, XACA-0890, and this ticket) because hand-applied fixes to the rendered `*-connect.sh`/`*-disconnect.sh` scripts were never ported back into the templates that generate them — a re-render silently reverted the fixes each time. This is the fourth and hopefully terminal fix: it closes the gap by editing the templates themselves rather than the generated output. Changes: (1) shebang `#!/bin/zsh` → `#!/usr/bin/env zsh` in both templates (XACA-0684); (2) `team-connect.sh.template` now sources `$AITEAMFORGE_DIR/scripts/lcars-launch-helpers.sh` (guarded, best-effort) immediately after `AITEAMFORGE_DIR` is set, matching every committed flat `*-connect.sh`; (3) both inline iTerm-GUI probes (`[[ "$TERM_PROGRAM" == "iTerm.app" ]] || pgrep -f "iTerm.app" > /dev/null`) in `team-connect.sh.template` replaced with the `has_iterm_gui` helper that (2)'s sourced file defines. Verified via `scripts/render-cockpit-scripts.sh --check`: before, all 5 flat `*-connect.sh` drifted 13 changed lines each and all 5 flat `*-disconnect.sh` drifted 2 changed lines (1-line shebang) each; after, `*-disconnect.sh` drift is fully gone and `*-connect.sh` drift drops to a single template-ahead comment block (XACA-0774-002, intentionally not yet in the committed scripts) that a later re-render will add — not a regression.

- **XACA-0297-005/006 — synced `share/scripts/kb-cr.sh` and `share/lcars-ui/server.py`: CR state changes now stamp their lifecycle timestamp, from both the CLI and the LCARS transition endpoint.** Neither force-write path recorded a `cr_*_at` value. `_kb_cr_container_transition` wrote `crState` + `updatedAt` behind a stale "that is subitem 004's job" comment, and the LCARS endpoint (XACA-0328-005) open-coded its own `_kb_jq_update` doing the same while the comment above it claimed to delegate to `_kb_cr_container_transition` — a delegation the code never performed. Because operators drive state changes from the CHANGE REQ tab rather than the CLI, the endpoint was the primary leak. Measured across the three live mobile boards before the fix: 24 CRs in `cr-closed` carrying only 3 `cr_closed_at` values, and **0 of 26** carrying both `deploy_window_planned` and `cr_deployed_prod_at` — the two inputs to the bi-weekly summary's estimate-accuracy section, which had therefore been rendering empty since Phase 6. Adds `_kb_cr_state_entry_ts_field()` (canonical state→entry-timestamp map, all ten states) and `_kb_cr_stamp_state_entry()` (resolve field → preserve any existing value → delegate to `_kb_cr_lifecycle_advance`) as the single shared force-write path, routing both call sites through it — replacing a private copy rather than adding a third. The endpoint's duplicate `cr_state_changed` append was removed because the shared helper emits that event itself; keeping both would double-log every manual transition into the feed the pushback and cycle-time readers consume. Existing timestamps are preserved rather than overwritten: a force-write must not rewrite when a CR first entered a state, and preserving is recoverable via `revert` where overwriting is not.

  **Targeted mirror, not a `sync-tap.sh` run — and `share/scripts/kb-cr.sh` was patched on top of the tap copy rather than overwritten.** The tap's `kb-cr.sh` carries XACA-0896's in-flight `_kb_cr_set_publish_stamps` / `_kb_cr_get_publish_stamps` Guard 4 plumbing, published to consumers here but absent from this branch's canonical — the same hazard XACA-0895 documented above and resolved by conceding the file. Conceding was not available here: `share/lcars-ui/server.py` **calls** `_kb_cr_stamp_state_entry`, so mirroring the endpoint without its helper would ship consumers a tap whose server invokes an undefined function; the two must co-change. The canonical delta was applied as a patch instead (clean, one 2-line offset absorbing XACA-0896's additions), leaving the tap strictly additive — verified the resulting copy retains all 6 publish-stamp references AND gains this ticket's two helpers, with the tap's `100644` mode preserved. `share/lcars-ui/server.py` was copied wholesale only after this branch was rebased onto develop, so it carries XACA-0890's ThreadingHTTPServer/`_DeferredResponse` work (46 references verified present post-copy) rather than reverting it. A blanket `sync-tap.sh` was deliberately NOT run: `sync_dir` over `lcars-ui/` would have reverted XACA-0895's mirrored CR-tab assets (`lcars-cr-tab.js`, `lcars-filter-bar.js`, `lcars-cr-tab.css`, two tests). The `kb-cr.sh` divergence reconciles once XACA-0896 merges.

- **XACA-0894 — synced `share/scripts/lcars-health-check.sh`: re-entrancy hardening for the restart path (EADDRINUSE bounded wait/retry + a whole-invocation run lock).** The parent ticket's primary fix is a launchd plist key (`AbandonProcessGroup` on `com.devteam.lcars-health.plist`) and does NOT ship through the tap — consumers already received that key via the three tap templates in XACA-0763, so consumer machines were never exposed to the SIGTERM-before-ready failure (status 143) this ticket exists to fix; only the dev-team repo-root plist was missed, because `test-xaca-0763-processgroup-detach.sh` SECTION 2 linted an allowlist of exactly three tap templates and never globbed the repo root. What DOES ship here is the secondary hardening: `_hc_wait_for_port_release` (bounded, wall-clock-based wait using a fast `nc -z` probe rather than per-iteration `lsof`, which measured 2.3–6.8s on a loaded host and silently inflated a 10s bound to 19–30s+), a one-shot EADDRINUSE retry keyed off the server's own stderr signature (`Address already in use` / `[Errno 48]`) rather than a guess, and `_hc_acquire_run_lock`/`_hc_run_lock_release` guarding against overlapping sweeps. The run lock's stale-reclaim hinges on a single atomic rename — a second mkdir-based mutex was deliberately rejected because an orphaned reclaim mutex would wedge the health checker permanently, strictly worse than the under-protection being fixed; release is ownership-aware (pid-file match) so it is safe to call unconditionally from the daemon's INT/TERM trap, and an age-bounded sweep clears `.stale.<pid>` orphans. **Mirror ordering note:** this sync was deliberately deferred until XACA-0890 (PR #732) merged. Until then the tap copy of this same file was 71 lines AHEAD of canonical, carrying XACA-0890-013's unmerged `--team` filter, so any mirror from XACA-0894's branch would have silently reverted a sibling's published work. Now that #732 has landed, this branch rebased onto develop and the mirror carries BOTH changes — verified the resulting tap copy retains all 10 `TEAM_FILTER` occurrences and that the only removed line is the daemon trap replaced by its ownership-aware form. See the dev-team root `CHANGELOG.md` XACA-0894 entries for the full breakdown, including the controlled launchd probe (3/3, `AbandonProcessGroup` as sole variable) that confirmed the reap mechanism rather than assuming it.

- **XACA-0890-025 — synced the fix for the `--team` mapping XACA-0890-013 got wrong for 4 of 5 parametric teams.** `share/scripts/lcars-health-check.sh`: `--team` now compares via the new `_hc_team_filter_matches(team, filter)` (exact match OR `$team` starting with `"$filter-"`), used identically by the eager validation and the sweep filter; the eager validation also now fails loudly if `--team` matches more than one entry (ambiguity), never silently picking one. `share/templates/team-connect-parametric.sh.template`: the remote health-check invocation now passes the bare `--team ${TEAM_ID}` instead of the project-qualified `--team ${INSTANCE}` XACA-0890-013 shipped, which only happened to work for finance by coincidence. See the dev-team root `CHANGELOG.md` XACA-0890-025 entry for the full breakdown, including live verification against this host's real registered teams and the fail-before/pass-after test evidence.

- **XACA-0895 — mirrored the new `cr-published` lifecycle state into the tap: `share/templates/kanban/cr-schema.json`, `share/scripts/cr-confluence-poller.py`, the three LCARS CR-tab assets, the daily-overview test, a new CR-tab publish regression test, and both `docs/` guides.** XACA-0895 splits the overloaded `cr-drafted` state in two: `cr-drafted` now means only "a local CR markdown draft exists, nothing published", while the new `cr-published` means "the CR markdown is live on Confluence (`cr_confluence_url` set) but the CR-Proper/IT Connect link has not yet been appended". The schema gains `cr-published` at index 1 of `crStates` (11 states total) and `crTimestamps.cr_published_at` — the sole declaration of that timestamp, which XACA-0896-001-fix above deliberately deferred to this ticket. The poller's Pass 1 becomes a dual-state scan: Pass 1a walks `cr-published` (preferred — reads `cr_confluence_url` directly via the new `fetch_published_page_content`), Pass 1b walks `cr-drafted` as the XACA-0465 legacy one-stage fallback so CRs that acquired a Confluence page without `kb-cr publish` are never stranded; both converge on `cr-submitted`. This mirror is a **superset** of XACA-0678's approval-provider seam already in this file — the `ApprovalProvider` / `ConfluenceApprovalProvider` / `ITConnectApprovalProvider` / `resolve_approval_provider` Pass 2 machinery is carried forward untouched (verified: the only tap-side lines replaced were the old `cr-drafted`-only Pass 1 text this ticket rewrites). Verified after rebasing onto develop: bats 531/531, `tests/test-kb-cr.sh` 159/159, `node --test` 58/58, pytest 984 passed with only the 11 pre-existing unrelated failures.

Verified before copying: the only tap-side lines replaced were the three pre-images of those edits.
