Deliberately NOT added to the tap's own `_aiteamforge_default_teams` seed: that is a positional 7-column tab table (`printf '%s\t%s\t…'`) with no slot for a named field, and adding one would break the `IFS=$'\t' read` parser contract — the read-path migration converges BOTH seeds onto the marker shape instead, which removes the two-sources-in-lockstep drift risk rather than doubling it. `get_team_kanban_dir()` / `get_team_working_dir()` now check the marker FIRST and name the alias target in the `KeyError`, retaining the legacy `None`/`""`/`"null"` inference as a fallback for un-migrated overlays. **Live crash fixed:** `share/scripts/aiteamforge-paths-init.sh` used an unguarded `entry["kanban_dir"]` subscript, so `aiteamforge-paths init` wrote the config and then died with `KeyError: 'kanban_dir'` while listing teams — non-zero exit, truncated output. It now renders the absence in words (`mainevent  (board-less alias — use 'command')  (LCARS :8400)`). 29 regression tests lock the contract — including that `board_less: true` WINS over any `kanban_dir` value re-added beside it — so a fourth "cleanup" attempt fails loudly instead of silently resurrecting the phantom `mainevent-board.json` bug XACA-0727 fixed.
- **XACA-0794 — the shell resolver on CONSUMER boxes never had a board-less guard, and silently answered with Academy's board (XACA-0794-013).** The two tap-side copies of `_kb_get_kanban_dir` (`share/templates/kanban/kanban-helpers.template.sh`, `share/templates/aliases/kanban-aliases.sh`) are INDEPENDENT resolvers from the dev tree's `kanban-helpers.sh` — and XACA-0727's board-less guard only ever landed on the dev copy. So on an installed box a `kb-*` call for `mainevent` read `kanban_dir: null` from the registry, failed Strategy 1's `[[ -d ]]` existence test, matched no case arm (there is no `mainevent` arm), and fell through to the Strategy-3 catch-all — silently resolving to `$AITEAMFORGE_DIR/kanban` and operating on **Academy's board**. Not a clean failure: a wrong answer against a bystander team's data. Both copies now carry a Strategy-0 board-less guard that refuses BEFORE any resolution strategy runs and names the alias target (`kb: 'mainevent' is a board-less alias … Use 'command' instead.`, rc=1). The guard's `_kb_board_less_alias_of` mirrors Python's `board_less_alias_of()` exactly — explicit overlay marker first, built-in `DEFAULT_TEAMS` table second — so an overlay that marks a team `board_less: true` but omits `alias_of` still resolves the target instead of dropping the most useful half of the error message. Applied mechanically to both copies from one source string, because this function's own header names it a known three-copy sibling-drift site. All 14 pre-existing cases in `tests/test-xaca-0649-kanban-dir-resolver.sh` still pass — no existing team's resolution changes.
- **XACA-0794 — the three self-healing config-rewrite passes shared three bugs, so they now share one write path (XACA-0794-008/-009/-012).** `_backfill_a1_fields_on_disk` (XACA-0522), `_scrub_contract_violating_keys_on_disk` (XACA-0643), and `_backfill_board_less_markers_on_disk` (XACA-0794) each rewrite `team-paths.json` during `load_config()`, and each was written by cloning the previous one — so each carried its own copy of the same lock/TOCTOU/backup/atomic-write skeleton and therefore its own copy of the same three defects (sibling-heuristic drift, knowledge k501; patching three copies would only have seeded a fourth). The skeleton now lives once in `_rewrite_config_on_disk` / `_atomic_write_json`; the passes supply only a predicate, a transform, a backup tag, and a log label. **(008) File mode** was reset to the process umask on every self-heal: the tmp file was created fresh and `os.replace()` carried its mode onto the config, so a `0600` `team-paths.json` — which holds per-team Anthropic account ids and API-key env-var names — silently widened to `0644`. The original's mode is now copied onto the tmp file *before* the replace, so content and mode land atomically together. **(009) A symlinked config was destroyed**: `os.replace(tmp, config_path)` replaced the *symlink itself* with a regular file and orphaned the real target, so a config deliberately symlinked into (say) a dotfiles repo silently stopped tracking. The write now resolves the path first and replaces the real file, leaving the symlink intact. **(012) The lock file was unlinked while the flock was still held**, so a racing process could create a fresh lock inode on the same path and both processes would believe they held the lock. Harmless in practice today — the transforms are additive, re-read under the lock, and idempotent, so the worst case was a redundant write — but not a property worth leaving load-bearing. The lock is now taken on the *resolved* path (so a process arriving via a symlink and one arriving via the real file contend for the same inode) and is never unlinked. 12 new regression tests; 8 of them fail against the pre-fix code.
- XACA-0800-011 — **`kb-knowledge-search`'s OR-fallback broke on a leading-dash word-split term (PR #696 review).** The XACA-0800 zero-result OR-fallback word-splits a quoted multi-word phrase into `_retry_argv` and re-invokes the function; if a split token began with `-` (e.g. the realistic natural-language phrase `"how to use -exact matching"` splits into a token `-exact`), the recursive call's own flag parser intercepted it via the `-*)` unknown-flag branch and printed `Unknown flag: …` plus a full `Usage:` dump instead of performing the OR search — and the printed copy-paste "corrected command" reproduced the identical failure when pasted. Not a security/injection issue (the token never reaches `grep`), but it defeated the fallback for exactly the natural-language input class XACA-0800 exists to rescue. Fixed in all three implementations (canonical `kanban-helpers.sh` + both tap-side copies) by assembling the retry argv as recognized flags, followed by a `--` end-of-options terminator, followed by the word-split terms verbatim — and teaching the arg parser to honor a literal `--`: once seen, every remaining token (including one that is itself `--`) is treated as a positional search term, never re-parsed as a flag. The parser change is general — it protects any future caller passing a dash-leading term, not just the recursive retry. Default matching semantics, `--porcelain`/`--json` output, and the once-per-invocation telemetry placement are all unchanged.

### Added
- XACA-0800 — `kb-knowledge-search` in both tap-side copies (`share/templates/aliases/kanban-aliases.sh`, `share/templates/kanban/kanban-helpers.template.sh`) gains a zero-result literal-phrase hint + guarded OR-fallback, ported hand-for-hand from the canonical `kanban-helpers.sh` fix (`sync-tap.sh` does not map this function to either tap file — each copy carries its own independent `grep -F` implementation). A quoted multi-word query (e.g. `kb-knowledge-search "Decimal never float quantize"`) is matched as a literal substring, not OR'd words, and almost always returns zero results on a populated corpus (XACA-0793). When `result_count == 0` in human-mode output AND at least one search term contains whitespace, the zero-result footer now prints a one-line explanation plus the corrected OR-form command, then auto-retries the search with the phrase word-split (labelled "no literal match — showing term matches") via a `_KB_KS_NO_FALLBACK`-guarded recursive call with `KB_SEARCH_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1` so the retry never double-logs. A single-word zero-result query never triggers the hint (no crying wolf), and `--porcelain`/`--json` output modes are byte-identical to before (no hint, no fallback). The `_retry_argv` array is built as a parallel structure DURING arg-parsing (flags verbatim, positional terms `${=1}`-word-split) rather than reconstructed afterward from `filter_*` vars, avoiding a second divergent source of truth (sibling-heuristic drift, knowledge k501). Telemetry (`kb-search.jsonl`) gains one additive `zero_result_hint:true|false` field; `result_count` on the primary/literal query stays a truthful 0 regardless of whether the fallback found anything. `Usage:`/`--help` text in both files now states the literal-phrase rule up front. Default `grep -F` matching semantics are unchanged in both files — the fix lives entirely in the previously-dead zero-result human-output branch.
- **XACA-0801 — Auto-Planning Gate: tap-side mirror of `_kb_build_planning_gate_section` + wiring.** Ported the canonical auto-planning-gate helper from `~/dev-team/kanban-helpers.sh` into `share/templates/kanban/kanban-helpers.template.sh` — the tap-canonical template that consumer machines render to `$AITEAMFORGE_DIR/kanban-helpers.sh`. The helper is wired into all prompt-generation sites (`kb-run`, `kb-work`, and `kb-run-debug`/`kb-work-debug` via `_kb_build_debug_prompt`), so a consumer's next upgrade brings the gate to their invocations. `sync-tap.sh` does NOT map this file — the template is hand-ported directly in the tap submodule. **Companion:** `share/skills/Project Planner/SKILL.md` upgraded to v1.12.0 with a new ATTACH MODE section documenting `/plan-project --attach <ITEM-ID>` (planning against an EXISTING item that has zero subitems). **Consumer effect:** items with zero subitems now gate before work starts, blocking execution until subitems exist — this restores the protected-subitem merge gate for zero-subitem items, which previously shipped with no gate at all.

## [0.17.7] - 2026-07-13

### Added
- XACA-0778 — Mirrors the crash-recovery reconciliation surfacing into the tap: `share/lcars-ui/` (`server.py`, `index.html`, `css/lcars.css`, `js/lcars.js`) gains the LCARS Workflow-tab "NEEDS RECONNECT" lane that surfaces crash-orphaned in-progress work (server-side `get_reconciled_inprogress()` adds an additive `reconciledInProgress` field sourced from the read-only `_kb_reconcile_inprogress` classifier; client renders an amber lane distinct from PAUSED with a `kb-resume <id>` reconnect hint), and `share/kanban-hooks/kanban-stop.py` now writes a clean-shutdown sentinel on graceful exit so an unclean shutdown is detectable. Byte-identical canonical→tap mirror per the XACA-0340 `sync-tap.sh` contract (5 files). The reconciliation engine + `kb-recover`/`kb-resume <ID>` commands live in canonical `kanban-helpers.sh`, which is not `sync-tap.sh`-mapped (tap consumers get helper content via the `kanban-aliases.sh`/`kanban-helpers.template.sh` route), so no helper drift here.

### Fixed
- XACA-0792 — `aiteamforge start` silently skipped every project-scoped team, so `stop` killed their LCARS servers and `start` never brought them back. **Root cause — two registries, two different names for the same team.** `get_configured_teams()` (`libexec/lib/config.sh`) returns the BASE ids stored in `.aiteamforge-config`'s `.teams[]` (`finance`), while the canonical port registry (`~/.aiteamforge/team-paths.json`, read via `aiteamforge_team_lcars_port()`) keys project-scoped teams by their INSTANCE id (`finance-personal`). The link between them — `.team_paths[<base>].project_id` — was never traversed: `start_lcars()` (`libexec/commands/aiteamforge-start.sh`) passed the base id straight into the port lookup, which missed the registry and took the `No LCARS port allocated for '<team>' — skipping` branch. **Why it was user-visible rather than merely noisy:** the damage is asymmetric. `aiteamforge stop` tears the server down by port/pattern and succeeds, then `start` skips the team — so every stop/start cycle (and `lcars-watch` runs one) left LCARS DOWN with no path back up, and re-running `start` could never recover it. Field-confirmed on M4Mini 2026-07-12: the finance LCARS UI reported "server not found" while `lcars-watch.log` carried 14 consecutive skips; a manually launched server on the correct port served HTTP 200 the whole time, which is why the server code never looked at fault. **Why it hid for so long:** single-instance teams (`academy`, `ios`, `android`, …) have no `project_id`, so their base id IS their instance id and the unmapped lookup happened to succeed. The bug bites exactly the project-scoped teams — `finance-personal`, `legal-coparenting`, `medical-general` — i.e. the personal-team boxes. **Fix:** new `aiteamforge_resolve_team_key()` (`libexec/lib/aiteamforge-paths.sh`) resolves a configured team id to the key that actually exists in the registry, trying candidates in order and taking the first that resolves: (1) `get_board_id()` — the CANONICAL deterministic map already in `libexec/lib/kanban-paths.sh` (`legal` → `legal-coparenting`); (2) a new `get_team_instance_id()` (`libexec/lib/config.sh`) deriving `<base>-<project_id>` from `.team_paths[<base>].project_id`, which covers genuinely project-scoped installs the static map cannot know about (a freelance team on a custom project); (3) the base id unchanged. `start_lcars()` delegates to it, and the id that resolved drives the port, the `LCARS_TEAM` env, and the session name (`<instance>-lcars`, matching the `finance-personal-lcars.port` file the rest of the system already writes). `aiteamforge-start.sh` now sources `kanban-paths.sh` (guarded, mirroring `aiteamforge-doctor.sh`) — without it `get_board_id` is unreachable and `legal` cannot resolve at all. **Two corrections caught in review, both worth recording because the naive fix looks right:** (a) the first cut derived the instance id from `project_id` ALONE, which fixes `finance` and `medical` only BY COINCIDENCE — their `TEAM_DEFAULT_PROJECT` (`personal`, `general`) happens to spell the registry suffix, whereas `legal`'s is `default`, deriving the non-existent `legal-default` and leaving legal's LCARS permanently down. The canonical `get_board_id` map is the authority and must be consulted FIRST; deriving from a mutable config value made this a THIRD base→instance mapper competing with an existing deterministic one (see the `get_board_id` doc block, and XACA-0565 which consolidated exactly this class of duplication). (b) an unmapped base id must be tried LAST, not first: registry lookups fall back to baked-in `DEFAULT_TEAMS` rows, so a bare base id can "resolve" to a DEFAULT port that is not the one the install configured — `freelance` hits the default 8505 while a configured `freelance-acme` sits on 8420 — which would have started the server on the wrong port. Candidates are therefore only tried when they actually MAP to something other than the base. Deliberately NOT "fixed" by rewriting `.teams[]` to hold instance ids: `libexec/installers/install-kanban.sh` also reads `.teams[]` and expects base ids to locate `<team>.conf`, so that shortcut would have traded an LCARS outage for an installer break. **`status` stays read-only:** `aiteamforge_team_lcars_port()` MATERIALIZES a default registry file on its first lookup when none exists — harmless for `start`, but unacceptable for a status probe, which would have silently created `~/.aiteamforge/team-paths.json` merely by being run. `status` therefore consults the registry only when the file already exists, and otherwise falls through to the legacy single-port probe, writing nothing (regression caught in review; pinned by a test asserting the registry is absent after a `status --json` run). New `tests/test-xaca-0792-team-instance-id.sh` (25 assertions, `plain-shell`): unit coverage of the mapping (project-scoped, single-instance no-op, uppercase `project_id`, missing-config degradation); a behavioral pair pinning the original regression against an exact replica of the M4Mini fixture (raw `finance` misses the registry; mapped `finance-personal` → 8361); full profile-scoped-team coverage using the REAL shipped `TEAM_DEFAULT_PROJECT` defaults — `legal` (project_id `default`) → `legal-coparenting`/8320, `medical` → 8340, `finance` → 8361, `academy` → 8203, plus a custom `freelance-acme` → 8420 guarding candidate (2) and an unknown team resolving nothing; and structural guards asserting `start_lcars()` delegates to the resolver and that the resolver consults BOTH mappers (dropping either silently strands a class of teams). A fixture using `coparenting` as legal's `project_id` would have HIDDEN the bug, so the test deliberately uses the shipped default. Verified as a true regression guard: 18/18 green with the fix, 10/18 red with it reverted. Full `config`/`multi-team`/`cli`/`lifecycle`/XACA-0762 suites still green (153 assertions, 0 failures); `shellcheck -S warning` clean.
- XACA-0785 (001) — `share/teams/*.conf` gains an explicit `AGENT_TERMINAL_<character>="<slug>"` map on every team, closing the root cause behind `install-team.sh` launching agents with an EMPTY system prompt: the generator resolves each terminal's `_PROMPT_FILE` from `terminal_id`, which it derived from the persona frontmatter `name:` (a character name, e.g. `scotty`/`zek`) rather than the terminal slug the prompt files are actually named after (`<team>-<slug>-prompt.txt`, e.g. `android-engineering-prompt.txt`). For 8 of 9 teams the two happened to coincide closely enough to mostly work by accident (or the mismatch was masked); `finance` was the one team where they diverged completely — `TEAM_AGENTS` held character names (`zek`, `quark-fin`, `nog`, `brunt`, `rom`) instead of location slugs, so the `[[ -f ]]` prompt-file guard in the generated `.zshrc_finance_*` always silently no-op'd and every finance agent launched persona-less. Also normalizes `finance.conf`'s `TEAM_AGENTS`/`AGENT_WINDOWS_*` keys to the location slugs (`nagus`/`bar`/`vault`/`fca`/`workshop`) matching the convention every other team already followed, preserving the Ferengi character identities as comments and in the new map. One pre-existing gap surfaced and deliberately left unfixed rather than papered over: `academy`'s `lal` (UX evaluator) persona has no terminal slot or prompt file — it is invoked only as a subagent, never launched via `install-team.sh` — flagged with a `TODO(XACA-0785)` comment on its map entry instead of inventing a prompt file. Validated fleet-wide: 54/55 persona→slug→prompt-file chains resolve cleanly, the sole miss being the documented `lal` gap. Consumer wiring (`install-team.sh` reading this map instead of inferring from `name:`) tracked under sibling XACA-0785 subitems.
- XACA-0785 (002) — `libexec/installers/install-team.sh` now actually consumes the `AGENT_TERMINAL_<character>` map (001) instead of deriving the terminal identifier from the persona frontmatter `name:` — the consumer-side half of the fix. Both python-heredoc generators (`generate_per_agent_startup_scripts`, `generate_per_agent_zshrc_files`) parse `AGENT_TERMINAL_*` straight off the raw `.conf` text (same idiom already used for `AGENT_WINDOWS_*`, since the `_read_conf()` array loader only serializes entries for names present in `TEAM_AGENTS`) and resolve each persona's slug via `AGENT_TERMINAL_<character_normalized>` (`-` → `_` in the lookup key, e.g. persona `quark-fin` → `AGENT_TERMINAL_quark_fin`). The resolved SLUG now drives the zshrc filename (`.zshrc_<team>_<slug>`), the startup-script filename (`<team>-<slug>-startup.sh`), `SESSION_NAME`/`SESSION_CODE`, the `AGENT_WINDOWS_*` lookup (now correctly slug-keyed fleet-wide per 001), and therefore `_PROMPT_FILE` resolution (`<team>-<slug>-prompt.txt`) — closing the empty-system-prompt bug for every team, not just `finance`. The raw persona CHARACTER identity is deliberately preserved where it belongs: `SESSION_DEVELOPER`/the tmux `@developer` option (unchanged — already sourced independently from the persona's `## Core Identity` `**Name:**` field), the tmux `@claude_agent` option (now explicitly character-driven — "which Claude agent is this" reads as `reno`, not the room slug `engineering`), and the visible zshrc shell-prompt banner `SESSION_TITLE` (character-driven, e.g. `ZEK`, not the location slug `NAGUS`). Personas with NO `AGENT_TERMINAL_` entry are recognized as subagent-only (never launched as a persistent terminal — Academy's `lal`, the UX evaluator, is the only current instance) and both generators now skip them cleanly (informational `ℹ️` line, no file written) instead of inventing a bogus terminal from the character name. Removes the placeholder `AGENT_TERMINAL_lal="ux"` entry (and its `TODO(XACA-0785)` comment) from `academy.conf` added by 001 — `lal` has no `academy-ux-prompt.txt` and is subagent-only, so the map now validates 54/54 with zero misses. New `verify_agent_prompt_files()` runs after both generators (fail-soft, does not abort the install — a missing prompt file is a content gap, not an installer failure, and aborting would strand an otherwise-complete team install): for every slug-mapped persona it asserts `<team>/scripts/prompts/<team>-<slug>-prompt.txt` exists post-copy, printing a loud multi-line 🚨 banner naming every miss plus an `OK=N MISS=M` summary — replacing total silence (an unset `CLAUDE_SYSTEM_PROMPT` and a persona-less Claude launch, with no error anywhere in the chain) with an unmissable diagnostic. Verified in a fully sandboxed harness (`TEST_TMP_DIR`/`AITEAMFORGE_DIR`/`HOME` all redirected, no real installer run, no brew/network calls) against `finance`, `android`, and `academy`: finance produces exactly `.zshrc_finance_{nagus,bar,vault,fca,workshop}` and `finance-{nagus,bar,vault,fca,workshop}-startup.sh` with zero `zek`/`rom`/`quark-fin` orphans; `finance-nagus`'s `_PROMPT_FILE` resolves to an on-disk `finance-nagus-prompt.txt` (8762 bytes) while `SESSION_DEVELOPER`/`@developer` still show `Grand Nagus Zek` and `SESSION_TITLE='ZEK'`; academy skips `lal` in both generators with zero files written; all three teams report `OK=N MISS=0`; a deliberate single missing prompt file reproduces the 🚨 banner + `MISS=1` without aborting the caller. `bash -n` clean.
- XACA-0785 (003) — 001/002 close the install-time half of the empty-system-prompt bug (`verify_agent_prompt_files()` only fires during `install-team.sh`); this closes the RUNTIME half — every subsequent shell a consumer opens after a prompt file is later deleted, renamed, or never materialized by an upgrade still silently launched Claude persona-less with zero indication anywhere in the chain. Two sites converted from a silent `[[ -f ... ]] && ... `-shaped no-op to an explicit `if/else` that warns loudly on stderr (naming the exact expected path and stating the consequence) while leaving the shell/launch path to fall through cleanly — neither aborts an interactive shell nor kills the `cc` launch path. **Site 1 — `libexec/installers/install-team.sh`** (`generate_per_agent_zshrc_files()`'s python-heredoc-emitted zshrc template, ~line 2792): the generated `.zshrc_<team>_<slug>` no longer silently no-ops `CLAUDE_SYSTEM_PROMPT` when `_PROMPT_FILE` is missing — it now emits a 🚨 banner naming the missing path before falling through. **Site 2 — `share/templates/aliases/cc-aliases.sh`** (rendered to `$AITEAMFORGE_DIR/claude_code_cc_aliases.sh` by `install-shell.sh`, sourced by every generated zshrc): the context-aware `cc()` function's `if [[ -f "$prompt_file" ]]` gate previously fell straight through to the persona-less `claude --permission-mode bypassPermissions` fallback with NO warning when the prompt file was absent — the actual fleet-wide instance of the silent-failure pattern this ticket is about, since this is the file every real consumer's `cc` command runs through. Added an `else` branch with the same 🚨 stderr banner; `_cc_launch` itself (called from the `-f` branch and from every `cc-<team>-<slug>` alias) was already visible on a miss (pre-existing `❌ ERROR: Prompt file not found` + `return 1`) and needed no change. Verified in a `TEST_TMP_DIR`/`AITEAMFORGE_DIR`/`HOME`-sandboxed harness (no real installer run, no brew/network calls) driving the ACTUAL python generator (not a hand-copy) against a real Academy persona file: happy path (prompt present) populates `CLAUDE_SYSTEM_PROMPT` with zero stderr for both sites; failure path (prompt absent, `env -i` clean-room shell) prints the named-path warning on stderr, leaves `CLAUDE_SYSTEM_PROMPT` unset, and returns/exits 0 (shell survives, `cc`'s stub-`claude` fallback still fires); sourcing the generated zshrc twice in a row, and calling `cc` twice in a row, are both idempotent (same behavior, no state corruption) in both the present and absent case. `zsh -n` clean on the generated zshrc and on the rendered `cc-aliases.sh`; `bash -n`/`zsh -n` clean on `install-team.sh`. Full pre-existing `scripts/tests/test-cc-aliases-smoke.sh` suite still green (372/372 `.zshrc_*` configs + 13/13 XACA-0668 crash-safe regression cases) — no behavior change for the personal (non-tap) `~/dev-team/claude_code_cc_aliases.sh` dev-machine config's `cc()`, which received the identical fix for consistency (not fleet-shipped; not sync-tap-mapped).
- XACA-0734 — `aiteamforge upgrade` could never install a NET-NEW mandatory LaunchAgent on an already-installed box, making every such agent permanently unreachable. Third instance of the install-vs-upgrade defect class behind XACA-0673 / XACA-0751 / XACA-0761 / XACA-0771, and the most dangerous one. **Root cause:** `update_launchagents()` (`libexec/commands/aiteamforge-upgrade.sh`) opened each agent with `if [ ! -f "$target" ]; then continue; fi`, INFERRING "plist absent == the user opted out at install time". That inference cannot distinguish *"the user deliberately removed this"* from *"this plist did not exist when this box was installed"* — so an agent added in a later release was skipped forever on every pre-existing install. **Why it was self-sealing:** field-confirmed on M1Pro (XACA-0734-001), which had no `com.aiteamforge.auto-upgrade.plist`. That is the agent that PERFORMS upgrades, so the box could never auto-upgrade, and therefore could never receive the very fix that would have installed the auto-upgrade plist. It only got recent fixes because a human upgraded it by hand. **Fix — intent is now RECORDED, never INFERRED:** a new opt-out sentinel (`~/.aiteamforge/launchagents.optout`, newline-delimited plist basenames, exact-line membership; overridable via `AITF_LAUNCHAGENT_OPTOUT_FILE`) records the agents a user actually removed. A missing sentinel is normal and means "nothing opted out". `update_launchagents()` now follows an explicit decision table: plist **present** → refresh (unchanged); **absent + mandatory + not opted out** → RENDER + LOAD (the fix); **absent + mandatory + opted out** → skip (recorded intent respected, and `--force` does not override it); **absent + non-mandatory** → skip (unchanged). Under `--dry-run` nothing is materialized and a would-be install prints `Would install: <agent>`. Newly materialized agents are load-verified via `launchctl list` (the XACA-0651-009 pattern — `launchctl load` returns 0 even when the job is REJECTED), and an assert-present pass warns loudly (never fatally) for any mandatory agent still missing after the run. **Mandatory set** (minimal, absence breaks core function): `com.aiteamforge.auto-upgrade.plist`, `com.aiteamforge.lcars-health.plist`, `com.aiteamforge.kanban-backup.plist`. Deliberately EXCLUDED, with rationale recorded in-code so it is not re-litigated: `lcars-watch` / `cellar-watch` (auto-upgrade accelerators, not core — a box without them still upgrades on schedule) and `cr-confluence-poller` (config-gated, disabled by default, already has a real opt-out concept via `_cr_reconcile_disabled_plists`).
- XACA-0734 — **Sentinel lifecycle, and the primary way to populate it: hand-editing.** The sentinel (`~/.aiteamforge/launchagents.optout`) is a supported, user-editable file — `echo "<agent>.plist" >> ~/.aiteamforge/launchagents.optout` is the documented way to decline a mandatory agent. `update_launchagents()` and `doctor`'s `keepalive` fix both print this exact command, with the real resolved sentinel path, whenever they are about to materialize a missing mandatory agent — deliberately no new CLI subcommand was added for this. Each `install_<x>_launchagent()` (`libexec/installers/install-kanban.sh`) now CHECKS the sentinel at entry and refuses to install when opted out; `uninstall_<x>_launchagent()` RECORDS an opt-out (deduped). **Follow-up correction (same-review catch):** an earlier version of this fix had `install_<x>_launchagent()` unconditionally CLEAR the opt-out on every call, on the theory that "installing something is an explicit opt back in". That was wrong: `install_kanban_system()` (the installers' only caller) runs on every `aiteamforge setup`, including the explicit `reconfigure` path, not just a fresh install — and calls every `install_<x>_launchagent()` unconditionally regardless of what the user actually asked for. Auto-clearing there meant a hand-recorded opt-out would be silently reversed the next time the user reconfigured for something unrelated (e.g. adding a team). Opting back in is now only ever a deliberate sentinel edit. **Critical teardown:** both full-uninstall paths still DELETE the sentinel wholesale — `uninstall_kanban_system()` (which batch-calls every per-agent uninstall helper, and would otherwise leave a sentinel listing EVERY agent) and `remove_launchagents()` in `libexec/commands/aiteamforge-uninstall.sh`. This wipe is now the ONLY thing that ever clears a mandatory agent's opt-out — without it, a full uninstall followed by a fresh reinstall would leave every mandatory agent permanently opted out forever, since nothing downstream auto-clears anymore. Note the sentinel lives under `~/.aiteamforge/` (the runtime config dir), NOT under `WORKING_DIR` (`~/aiteamforge`), so `remove_files()` cannot clean it up implicitly.
- XACA-0734 — **`aiteamforge doctor` backstop.** `--fix`'s `keepalive` remediation previously only `launchctl load`ed an ALREADY-EXISTING lcars-health plist and, when the plist was missing, just printed "run: aiteamforge setup" — i.e. the tool you run precisely when something is broken could not fix it. It now renders the plist from its template AND loads it, honoring the opt-out sentinel (an opted-out agent stays absent and is reported as informational, never as a failure) and printing the same opt-out hint as `upgrade` on the render path. `check_launchagents()` now surfaces a MISSING mandatory plist as a real failure with an actionable fix (plus the opt-out hint), instead of only asking "is it loaded?" — a question that quietly conflated "exists but not loaded" (a warning) with "does not exist at all" (the unrecoverable state).

### Changed
- XACA-0734 — **Shared-lib extraction (kills the drift class).** LaunchAgent render logic was duplicated across `aiteamforge-upgrade.sh`, `aiteamforge-migrate.sh`, and `install-kanban.sh`'s inline seds, with an explicit XACA-0571-014 SIBLING-DRIFT warning saying every renderer must learn each new `{{PLACEHOLDER}}` or upgraded plists ship with unresolved tokens. `doctor` needed the same logic, which would have made a FOURTH copy. New `libexec/lib/launchagents.sh` is now the single source of truth for the agent→template map, the mandatory set, the opt-out sentinel helpers, and `_render_launchagent_template` (moved out of `aiteamforge-upgrade.sh`); it is sourced by `upgrade.sh`, `doctor.sh`, `uninstall.sh`, and `install-kanban.sh`. Add a new placeholder in ONE place. `tests/test-xaca-0510-launchagent-render.sh` was updated to SOURCE the lib instead of text-scraping the renderer out of `upgrade.sh`, and its assertions — which previously encoded the bug ("both plists absent → function skips both") — now assert the full decision table (43 tests, up from 15), including that a HAND-WRITTEN sentinel line is honored — the primary way the sentinel is actually populated.
- XACA-0734 — **`aiteamforge migrate` deliberately NOT changed.** `update_launchagents()` in `libexec/commands/aiteamforge-migrate.sh` carries the identical "Opt-in guard", and a sibling-drift audit will flag it as "the same bug, unfixed". It is not, and an in-code comment now records the decision so it is not re-litigated: `upgrade` is PROVISIONING (its contract is "bring this box up to what the current release ships", which necessarily includes agents that did not exist at install time), whereas `migrate` is REPOINTING (its contract is "the data dir MOVED; rewrite the agents that reference the old path"). An agent that is not installed has no stale path to repoint — there is nothing to migrate, and creating one would mean a data-directory move silently provisions new launchd jobs. Provisioning of missing mandatory agents belongs to exactly two places: `upgrade` (primary) and `doctor --fix` (backstop).
- XACA-0785 (006-009) — PR review follow-up on 002: the `AGENT_TERMINAL_*` map (001) closed the character-vs-slug confusion, but a reviewer flagged four ways the NEW slug-driven code could still fail invisibly — the same disease this ticket exists to cure. **006 (malformed slug):** the slug VALUE is interpolated directly into generated filenames and into double-quoted shell in the generated zshrc (`SESSION_NAME="{terminal_id}"`); a typo'd or hostile value (`../`, `$(...)`) previously flowed straight through. `libexec/installers/install-team.sh`'s new shared `parse_agent_terminal_map()` (duplicated verbatim into both `generate_per_agent_startup_scripts()` and `generate_per_agent_zshrc_files()`, matching this file's existing per-generator-heredoc structure) now validates every slug against `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*$`; a malformed value is quarantined (persona's terminal generation skipped) with a loud stderr warning naming the team, character, and offending value — never silently sanitized into something else. **007 (duplicate slug target):** two characters mapping to the same slug previously overwrote each other's generated startup script/zshrc, last-writer-wins, silently. The same parse pass now detects the collision, warns loudly naming both characters and the shared slug, and deterministically keeps the FIRST-seen character (conf file line order, same order both generators independently re-parse) — chosen over "skip both" because dropping the losing character's generated files is strictly less broken than leaving the terminal completely unprovisioned for either character; the point is that it is never silent. **008 (empty value vs. no entry):** `AGENT_TERMINAL_x=""` previously fell into the same falsy branch as "no entry at all" and was reported with the friendly "subagent-only persona" skip message — actively lying about a config bug. The per-persona resolution loop in both generators now checks map membership before truthiness, so an explicit empty value gets its own loud ⚠️ warning naming the character and stating it's a config bug, while a genuinely absent entry (e.g. Academy's `lal`, the UX evaluator) keeps the unchanged quiet ℹ️ skip. **009 (swallowed copy failure):** the `cp "$PERSONAS_TEMPLATE_DIR/prompts/"*.txt "$TEAM_DIR/scripts/prompts/" 2>/dev/null || true` copy step (backstopped by `verify_agent_prompt_files()`, XACA-0785-002) only ever surfaced the downstream SYMPTOM (a missing prompt file) rather than the actual CAUSE. Converted to an explicit `nullglob` array + `if ! cp ...; then` guard that captures real `cp` stderr to a temp file and echoes it (prefixed, attributed) on failure, distinguishing "source directory has zero `.txt` files" (its own warning) from "cp itself failed" (permission denied, disk full, etc.) — install stays non-fatal in both cases so one bad persona's prompt doesn't strand an otherwise-good team install. `verify_agent_prompt_files()` gained a quiet (non-printing) copy of the same slug-validation logic so its `OK=N MISS=M` count stays consistent with what the generators actually produced — a quarantined persona is correctly excluded rather than counted as a false MISS. Verified in a `TEST_TMP_DIR`/`AITEAMFORGE_DIR`/`HOME`-sandboxed harness sourcing the ACTUAL extracted function bodies (not hand-copies) against a mutated `finance.conf`: malformed-slug, duplicate-slug, and empty-value cases each fire the correct warning and correctly-scoped skip while the OTHER four Finance personas still generate normally (`OK=4 MISS=0` in every case); a real Academy run confirms `lal` still skips with only the quiet ℹ️ line, zero warnings; four `cp`-failure scenarios (happy path, empty source dir, read-only destination, single unreadable source file amid four good ones) each report correctly and remain non-fatal. Full clean-path regression across all 9 shipped teams still validates exactly `OK=54 MISS=0` fleet-wide with zero warnings, matching the 002 baseline. `bash -n`, `shellcheck` (0 findings), and `py_compile` on all three extracted python-heredoc bodies are clean.

## [0.17.6] - 2026-07-11

### Fixed
- XACA-0771 — `aiteamforge upgrade` never CREATED two classes of mandatory files it only knew how to refresh, same install-vs-upgrade defect class as XACA-0751/XACA-0761. **Defect A (aliases):** `update_shell_helpers()`'s alias loop (`libexec/commands/aiteamforge-upgrade.sh`) had `if [ ! -f "$target" ]; then continue; fi` for each of `agent-aliases.sh` / `cc-aliases.sh` / `worktree-aliases.sh` under `${WORKING_DIR}/share/aliases/` — a MISSING target was skipped forever, never created. `cc-aliases.sh` defines the `cc` alias that redirects the bare `cc` command away from `/usr/bin/cc` (clang); a box whose copy predated XACA-0494 or was ever deleted silently fell through to clang with no upgrade path back to a fix. **Defect B (hooks):** `aiteamforge-upgrade.sh` had NO function that ever touched `~/.claude/hooks/` at all — `install_hooks()` (`libexec/installers/install-claude-config.sh`) only runs from `install_claude_config()`, reached exclusively on a fresh `aiteamforge setup`. `share/templates/claude/settings.json.template` wires `{{CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR}}/hooks/inject-time-context.sh` as the `UserPromptSubmit` hook, so a box whose `hooks/` predated that file (or lost it) silently ran with time-context injection broken, with `aiteamforge upgrade` never re-materializing it. Compounding this, `install_hooks()` itself never copied `inject-time-context.sh` even on a FRESH install — an install-side parity gap, also closed by this ticket. **Fix — aliases:** the alias loop now checks a missing target's basename against a new `_xaca0771_mandatory_alias_basenames()` (all three shipped alias files today — mirrors the `_xaca0673_mandatory_materialize_basenames` convention `update_runtime_helpers` already established) and CREATES it (`mkdir -p` + the same `{{AITEAMFORGE_DIR}}` `sed` substitution + `chmod +x` the install path uses) instead of skipping; a future non-mandatory/layout-specific alias file still opts out via `continue`. **Fix — hooks:** new `update_claude_hooks()` REUSES `install_hooks()` verbatim (same idiom as `update_knowledge_repo`/`update_knowledge_sync` reusing their installer counterparts) inside a `set -u`-isolating, fail-soft subshell — it sets `AITEAMFORGE_DIR`/`TEMPLATE_DIR` from this script's own `WORKING_DIR`/`FRAMEWORK_DIR` but deliberately does NOT set `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, so install-claude-config.sh's own XACA-0773 precedence (caller/test override → `CLAUDE_SANDBOX` staging dir → real `$HOME/.claude`) resolves exactly as production needs; touches ONLY the hook files, never `settings.json`/skills/personas/tmux.conf. Wired into the run sequence immediately after `update_shell_helpers`. Because `install_hooks()` unconditionally `cp`'s, reusing it both creates missing mandatory files AND refreshes already-present ones with the latest shipped content in one code path. **Fix — install-side parity:** `install_hooks()` now also copies `inject-time-context.sh` (mirrors the existing `block-icloud-paths.py` read-only-guard + `chmod` pattern) so fresh installs and upgrades both cover it. **Assert-present:** both `update_shell_helpers` (aliases) and `update_claude_hooks` (hooks) now end with a loud, non-fatal `print_warning` naming any mandatory file still missing after the materialize/refresh pass — never a hard failure, matching the fail-soft stance of `update_knowledge_repo`/`update_knowledge_sync`; skipped entirely under `--dry-run`. Deliberately did NOT add the new hook files to `update_launchagents`'s `agents=()` array or `_xaca0608_aux_script_map` — both loops skip-if-absent by design and would have silently re-introduced the exact wrong-fix XACA-0761-004's CHANGELOG entry warns against. New `tests/test-xaca-0771-upgrade-materialize-missing.sh` (12 cases, standalone or via `test-runner.sh`, `TEST_TMP_DIR`-sandboxed, no real `$HOME/.claude`/`~/aiteamforge`, no network, no `launchctl`): missing `cc-aliases.sh` is CREATED (T1); an existing stale alias file still refreshes (T2); a missing alias TEMPLATE source triggers the assert-present warning naming `cc-aliases.sh` (T3); `--dry-run` creates nothing (T4); missing `inject-time-context.sh` / `block-icloud-paths.py` / `damage-control/*.py` are each CREATED in a sandboxed `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (T5-T7); `--dry-run` creates no hook file (T8); hooks-templates-entirely-absent triggers fail-soft assert-present warnings naming each missing hook without aborting the calling shell (T9); structural guards that `update_claude_hooks` is DEFINED (T10) and CALLED (bare line, T11) in the run sequence — the assertion that would have caught the original bug, mirroring the XACA-0751/XACA-0761 "would have caught it" pattern — and that `install_hooks()` now references `inject-time-context.sh` (T12). Verified non-vacuous: `git stash`-reverting both source files causes the suite to fail hard (function-extraction `FATAL`, exit 1) rather than passing. Registered in `tests/ci-manifest` as `plain-shell`; `tests/ci-manifest-check.sh` passes. Verified with `bash -n` and `shellcheck` on both edited files (clean — only pre-existing info-level `SC1091`/warning-level `SC2034`/`SC2155`/`SC2295` findings untouched by this diff).

## [0.17.5] - 2026-07-10

### Fixed
- XACA-0782 — `fleet-reporter.sh` (mirrored from canonical `fleet-monitor/client/fleet-reporter.sh`, XACA-0340) no longer aborts on machines with ZERO tmux sessions. The script runs `set -euo pipefail`; the session-count line `session_count=$(echo "$payload" | grep -o '"name":' | wc -l ...)` returns a non-zero exit from `grep` when the payload has no `"name":` occurrences (an idle machine running no team sessions), which pipefail propagated and `set -e` turned into a full-script abort — BEFORE `send_status` ever POSTed — so idle fleet machines could never report and showed permanently offline. Fix: append `|| true` to the count line (plus an explanatory comment so it isn't "cleaned up" as redundant) so grep's no-match exit can't abort the run; the count is correctly 0. Review follow-up (PR #683): the same set -e/pipefail abort class was guarded at three sibling `grep`-in-command-substitution sites in the same script — `tailscale_name` (grep '"DNSName"'), the **top-level** `IP_ADDRESS` (`grep -v '127.0.0.1'`, which could abort the whole reporter on a loopback-only host), and `windows` (`grep -o '[0-9]* windows'`, guarded with `|| echo 0` to stay numeric). Byte-identical canonical + tap mirror per the plain-`cp` `sync_file` contract.
- XACA-0610 — `update_runtime_helpers` in `libexec/commands/aiteamforge-upgrade.sh` now also refreshes the OPERATIVE `fleet-monitor/client/fleet-reporter.sh` copy, not just the decorative `${WORKING_DIR}/scripts/fleet-reporter.sh` copy the existing scripts/ sweep already covered. `fleet-reporter.sh` ships to two destinations at install time (`install-shell.sh` lays down the decorative copy, `install-fleet-monitor.sh` lays down the operative copy that the `com.aiteamforge.fleet-reporter` launchd job actually executes); the scripts/ sweep only walks `WORKING_DIR/scripts/`, so it never reached `fleet-monitor/client/`, leaving upgraded machines running a permanently frozen reporter. Fix: a tightly-scoped block at the end of `update_runtime_helpers` (same shape as the XACA-0677 root-`iterm2_window_manager.py` fix) copies `share/scripts/fleet-reporter.sh` → `${WORKING_DIR}/fleet-monitor/client/fleet-reporter.sh` with plain `cp` + `chmod +x`, refreshing ONLY when the operative copy already exists on disk (XACA-0673 convention — machines that opted out of fleet monitoring stay opted out). Dry-run aware. New `tests/test-xaca-0610-fleet-reporter-refresh.sh` (stale-refresh, content-match, absent-not-materialised, dry-run cases), registered in `tests/ci-manifest` as `plain-shell`.

### Added
- XACA-0761 (001) — Mirrors dev-team's fleet knowledge-repo sync engine (`scripts/kb-knowledge-sync.sh`, XACA-0749) into the tap as `share/scripts/kb-knowledge-sync.sh` (byte-identical copy via `sync-tap.sh`, new `sync_file` mapping added alongside the other `scripts/` entries — canonical-source rule, XACA-0340). Runs `git pull --rebase` + `git push` against `~/knowledge` on a periodic timer (LaunchAgent wiring is a separate subitem); fail-soft by design — the script itself no-ops (exit 0, logged skip reason) whenever `~/knowledge` isn't yet a real git clone, a sync lock is already held, the tree is dirty/mid-rebase, no upstream is configured, or a pull/push fails for any reason (offline, no deploy-key auth, rejected, conflict) — mirroring the same fail-soft contract `install_knowledge_repo` already uses for provisioning the clone itself (XACA-0747/0750/0751). Non-zero exit is reserved solely for a script-usage error (too many args) or the XACA-0754 PII-containment guard (`KB_KNOWLEDGE_LOCAL_ROOT` resolving inside the synced repo), which deliberately aborts loudly instead of degrading. Validated with `bash -n` and `shellcheck` (clean). Tap-side wiring (LaunchAgent template, install-kanban.sh, tests) tracked under sibling XACA-0761 subitems.
- XACA-0761 (003) — Wires the knowledge-sync LaunchAgent into fresh-install provisioning: new `install_knowledge_sync_launchagent()` / `uninstall_knowledge_sync_launchagent()` in `install-kanban.sh`, modeled 1:1 on `install_auto_upgrade_launchagent()` (renders `share/templates/auto-upgrade/knowledge-sync-launchagent.template.plist`, copies `share/scripts/kb-knowledge-sync.sh` to `$AITEAMFORGE_DIR/scripts/`, load-verifies via `launchctl list` per the XACA-0651-009 pattern rather than trusting `launchctl load`'s exit code). Called from `install_kanban_system`'s LaunchAgent block, AFTER `install_knowledge_repo` so the clone exists before the gate below checks it. **Fail-soft gate:** only installs/loads the daemon when `~/knowledge` is a REAL git clone (`[ -d "$root/.git" ]` — the exact idiom `install_knowledge_repo` uses for its own "already a real clone" branch); a scaffold-only or absent `~/knowledge` (fleet-auth gate XACA-0750 not yet granted on this box) skips with an `info` line and `return 0` rather than installing a pointless 30-minute no-op poll loop — belt-and-suspenders on top of the script's own per-run self-fail-soft (XACA-0761-001). Neither new function is `export -f`'d, matching the sibling LaunchAgent installers (`install_auto_upgrade_launchagent`, `install_backup_launchagent`, `install_cellar_watch_launchagent`, etc.) — none of which are exported; the upgrade-path reuse in XACA-0761-004 sources the file directly rather than relying on export. Verified with `bash -n` and `shellcheck` (clean).
- XACA-0761 (004) — Wires the knowledge-sync LaunchAgent into the UPGRADE path so EXISTING fleet machines receive it, not just fresh installs (the exact defect class XACA-0751 fixed for `~/knowledge` provisioning itself — a materialize-only-at-install call site never reaches an already-installed box). New `update_knowledge_sync()` in `aiteamforge-upgrade.sh` reuses `install_knowledge_sync_launchagent()` verbatim (sources `install-kanban.sh` in a subshell, same idiom as `update_knowledge_repo`/`install_knowledge_repo`), explicitly setting `AITEAMFORGE_DIR`/`INSTALL_ROOT` from this script's `WORKING_DIR`/`FRAMEWORK_DIR` inside the subshell since — unlike `install_knowledge_repo` — the LaunchAgent installer reads both vars and neither is guaranteed exported into the upgrade process. Registered in the run sequence immediately after `update_knowledge_repo` (so `~/knowledge` is provisioned before the daemon's fail-soft gate checks it) and deliberately NOT added to `update_launchagents`'s `agents=()` array — that array skips any plist not already on disk (`if [ ! -f "$target" ]; then continue`), so adding the entry there would have silently excluded every pre-XACA-0761 fleet machine from ever receiving the daemon (the wrong-fix this ticket exists to prevent). New structural regression test `tests/test-knowledge-sync-upgrade-registration.sh` (5 cases, standalone or via `test-runner.sh`) asserts `update_knowledge_sync` is not just defined but CALLED (bare line) in the run sequence, ordered after `update_knowledge_repo`, reuses `install_knowledge_sync_launchagent` without reimplementation, and is absent from `update_launchagents`'s array; verified to fail (2/5) when the bare call is stripped, proving the guard actually catches the XACA-0751-class regression rather than passing vacuously. Not yet registered in `tests/ci-manifest` — tracked for the sibling ci-manifest-registration subitem. Verified with `bash -n` and `shellcheck` (clean; the sole `SC2329` info-level finding on the trap-invoked `cleanup()` matches the pre-existing pattern in `test-xaca-0751-upgrade-knowledge-repo.sh` and is outside CI's shellcheck scope, which only lints `bin/*.sh`).
- XACA-0761 (005) — Tap-sandbox functional test coverage for the knowledge-sync LaunchAgent installer: new `tests/test-xaca-0761-knowledge-sync-launchagent.sh` (11 cases, standalone or via `test-runner.sh`), entirely `TEST_TMP_DIR`-isolated (no real `~/knowledge`, no real `~/Library/LaunchAgents`, no real `launchctl`, no network), following the `install_knowledge_repo`/`install_knowledge_sync_launchagent` sandboxed-subshell pattern from `test-xaca-0747-knowledge-repo.sh` and the stubbed-`launchctl` functional pattern from `test-xaca-0651-consumer-doctor-gaps.sh`. Covers: fail-soft/no-clone (root path absent entirely, and separately a non-git husk directory in the way — both hit the same `[ -d "$root/.git" ]` gate, no plist rendered, exit 0); fail-soft/no-auth as an end-to-end chain (`install_knowledge_repo()` run first against an unreachable/bogus URL so the XACA-0750 auth gate fails and the root stays absent, THEN `install_knowledge_sync_launchagent()` run against that same root — verifying the fail-soft contract holds across the full `install_kanban_system` call order, not just when the LaunchAgent installer is invoked in isolation); idempotent re-run (a real git-clone root run through the installer twice back-to-back — both exits 0, byte-identical rendered plist both times, `plutil -lint`-clean, script (re)installed cleanly); and the XACA-0761-004 upgrade-path registration guard, exercised as a thin pass-through invocation of the sibling `tests/test-knowledge-sync-upgrade-registration.sh` rather than a duplicated re-implementation. That pass-through required one non-obvious fix: `tests/test-runner.sh` `export -f`s its own `test_start`/`test_pass`/`test_fail`, which bash function-export propagates into ANY nested `bash <file>` invocation (not just direct children of the runner) — so invoking the sibling suite naively under the real harness silently skipped its own `_PASS_COUNT`/`_FAIL_COUNT` bookkeeping and fell through to its unconditional trailing `exit 0`, turning the registration-guard check into a vacuous pass regardless of whether T1-T5 actually failed. Fixed by `unset -f`-ing the inherited test-framework functions immediately before invoking the sibling script, forcing it down its own standalone-framework branch deterministically in every context; verified passing identically standalone, via `test-runner.sh` (no `-v`), and via `test-runner.sh -v`. Also registers both this new suite and the previously-unregistered `test-knowledge-sync-upgrade-registration.sh` (XACA-0761-004) in `tests/ci-manifest` (`plain-shell`); `tests/ci-manifest-check.sh` passes (57 entries). Verified with `bash -n` and `shellcheck` (info-level findings only — `SC2329`/`SC2030`/`SC2031`/`SC1090` — matching the pre-existing pattern in sibling sandboxed-subshell tests, outside CI's shellcheck scope which only lints `bin/*.sh`).
- XACA-0761 (002) — New parameterized LaunchAgent template `share/templates/auto-upgrade/knowledge-sync-launchagent.template.plist`, converting dev-team's hardcoded `com.devteam.knowledge-sync.plist` (XACA-0749) into a tap-native `com.aiteamforge.knowledge-sync` template that resolves `HOMEBREW_PREFIX` + install paths at render time — pattern-matched to the existing `lcars-health`/`lcars-watch`/`cellar-watch` plist templates. Deliberately OMITS `AbandonProcessGroup` (unlike those sibling agents, and unlike an early note in this entry that wrongly claimed it was present): the sync script runs `git` synchronously via command substitution and exits, leaving no detached child for launchd to SIGKILL when the job's process group is torn down — so the XACA-0763 key is unnecessary here. The template carries an inline comment recording this evaluation.
- XACA-0761 (006) — Mirrors `docs/knowledge-sync-daemon.md` into the tap docs and registers the `share/scripts/kb-knowledge-sync.sh` canonical-source mapping in `sync-tap.sh` (the mapping the 001 entry anticipated; the byte-identical tap copy is now a managed mirror of dev-team's canonical, closing the unregistered-duplicate XACA-0340 footgun rather than leaving an orphaned copy that silently drifts).
- XACA-0761 (008) — Review-gate fix (PR #680): the operator doc (`docs/knowledge-sync-daemon.md`) claimed logs land in `/tmp/knowledge-sync.log`, carrying a `/tmp`-rationale paragraph verbatim from the XACA-0749 dev-machine source, but the shipped plist's `StandardOutPath`/`StandardErrorPath` render from `{{LOG_DIR}}` → `$AITEAMFORGE_DIR/logs/knowledge-sync.log` (the installer `mkdir -p`s that dir before load). Reconciled the doc's log path + rationale to the tap implementation (matches the sibling `auto-upgrade`/`cellar-watch` agents' log dir). Doc-only; no runtime change.
- XACA-0773 — Ships an iCloud File Provider path guard as a new `PreToolUse` hook (`share/templates/claude/hooks/block-icloud-paths.py`), fleet-wide via the tap installer. Native file tools (`Read`/`Write`/`Edit`/`MultiEdit`/`NotebookEdit`/`Glob`/`Grep`) opening a path under `~/Library/Mobile Documents/com~apple~CloudDocs/` wedge Claude Code's event loop in an uninterruptible `openat$NOCANCEL` on both reads and writes. The hook denies the fatal open before it happens (exit 2 + stderr guidance pointing at the local `~/aiteamforge-transfers/<team>/` staging dir and the `~/.aiteamforge/stage-transfer.sh` helpers) and fails OPEN (exit 0) on any parse error so it can never break normal tool use. `Bash` is intentionally NOT guarded — subprocess opens don't hang, and that is how the stage-transfer/publish-transfer helpers move files across the iCloud boundary. `libexec/installers/install-claude-config.sh::install_hooks()` now copies the top-level hook into `${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR}/hooks/` (guarding existing read-only files the same way the damage-control loop does) alongside the existing `damage-control/*` copy; `share/templates/claude/settings.json.template` registers it as a new `PreToolUse` matcher. `settings.json` is per-machine and never synced, so this installer path (fresh install AND `brew upgrade` hydration) is the only way the hook reaches already-deployed fleet machines. **Gate follow-ups folded into the same ticket:** (1) the path check now resolves both the candidate and the iCloud base through `os.path.realpath()` before comparing, so a symlink pointing into iCloud is caught (not just a literal path under it), and casefolds the comparison (not the string echoed back in the BLOCKED message) since macOS's default filesystem is case-insensitive; the whole body of `main()` is now wrapped in a blanket `try/except: return 0` in addition to the existing targeted `json.load` guard, for airtight fail-open. (2) `install-claude-config.sh` now honors a caller-provided `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` before falling back to the `CLAUDE_SANDBOX`/real-`~/.claude` logic — closes the gap where a test harness invocation could fall through to writing a real `~/.claude`; production (`aiteamforge-setup.sh`) never pre-sets `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, so production behavior is unchanged. (3) new `merge_settings_json()` replaces the generic whole-array-replace `merge_json()` for settings.json specifically: it unions each `hooks.<event>` array (template matchers win on a `.matcher` collision, any base/user-custom matcher absent from the template is preserved) instead of silently dropping box-local hand-added matchers on every upgrade. **Final convergence fold-in:** `union_hooks()`'s keying was corrected for matcher-less events (`SessionStart`/`Stop`/`SessionEnd`/`UserPromptSubmit` have no `.matcher` field, so keying purely on `.matcher` collapsed them all onto the same null key and silently dropped a second custom base entry for the same event — exactly the drop this function exists to prevent); it now keys `.matcher`-bearing entries by matcher string as before, and matcher-less entries by exact object identity (a base entry survives unless that exact object already exists in the template; true duplicates still dedupe). `merge_json()` had zero remaining callers after being superseded by `merge_settings_json()` and was deleted outright rather than left as dead code with a stale comment.
- XACA-0774 (013/014) — folded two non-blocking review/test gate findings into the mirrored `share/scripts/remote-tmux-attach.sh`: reconnect/backoff progress messages now print to stderr (behaviour-neutral in the interactive-tab case, keeps status noise off stdout); and the unreachable dead-code `HOST`-starts-with-`-` guard was removed (the arg parser's generic leading-dash `-*)` branch already rejects it upstream). Re-mirrored byte-identical from canonical `dev-team/scripts/remote-tmux-attach.sh`.
- XACA-0774 (005) — Ship the `remote-tmux-attach.sh` reconnect helper to tap consumers. New `share/scripts/remote-tmux-attach.sh` (mirrored from canonical `dev-team/scripts/remote-tmux-attach.sh` via `sync-tap.sh`, per XACA-0340). Seeded to `$AITEAMFORGE_DIR/scripts/remote-tmux-attach.sh` on fresh install by new `install-kanban.sh::install_remote_tmux_attach_script()` (mirrors `install_kb_port_reconcile_script`), and materialized-when-absent + refreshed on upgrade via `aiteamforge-upgrade.sh`: added `remote-tmux-attach.sh` to `_xaca0673_mandatory_materialize_basenames` so existing installs get it on their next `brew upgrade` (the file is picked up by `update_runtime_helpers`' `share/scripts/*.sh` glob, and the mandatory-basenames list flips its absent-target behavior from skip to materialize). Deliberately NOT added to `_xaca0608_aux_script_map`: that would divert it to `update_aux_scripts`, which has no materialize-when-absent branch, so existing installs would never receive it — same wiring shape as `kb-init-team-guard.sh`. Uninstall needs no per-file entry: `remove_files()` already `rm -rf`s the whole working-dir `scripts/`. Without this, freshly-rendered connect scripts (which reference `${AITEAMFORGE_DIR}/scripts/remote-tmux-attach.sh`) would point at a missing helper on tap boxes.
- XACA-0774 (002) — `share/templates/team-connect.sh.template` (the flat, non-parametric connect template) now wires its tmux-attach tab surfaces through the new `remote-tmux-attach.sh` reconnect helper instead of a one-shot `ssh -t ... tmux attach` `--command` string. Converted: the LCARS tab (both the "LCARS Web" inline-browser profile path and its no-profile fallback), the per-agent tabs loop, and the Terminal.app `osascript` fallback (LCARS `do script` + per-agent `do script`) — all now invoke `${AITEAMFORGE_DIR}/scripts/remote-tmux-attach.sh ${HOST} ${TMUX_SOCKET} <SESSION>`, giving these tabs SSH keepalives (`ServerAliveInterval`/`ServerAliveCountMax`), bounded exponential-backoff auto-reconnect on a dropped transport, a session-gone bail-out so a genuinely dead tmux session doesn't loop forever, and a terminal reset (`\033c`) before every re-attach to clear stale alt-screen/imgcat passthrough residue. The Terminal.app `osascript` conversion drops the now-unneeded nested `\"/bin/zsh -lc '...'\"` quoting layer since the helper's positional args need no extra shell-quoting (same simplification the parametric template's worked example uses). Left untouched, with an explanatory comment: the agent-panel split (`--command "ssh -t ... agent-panel-display.sh ..."`) — that surface is a REMOTE display-refresh loop, not a tmux attach, so the reconnect helper does not apply. Canonical helper source: `dev-team/scripts/remote-tmux-attach.sh` (XACA-0774-001), mirrored into this tap via `sync-tap.sh` per the canonical-source rule (XACA-0340).

## [0.17.4] - 2026-07-10

### Added
- XACA-0770 (EPIC-0047) — **Closes a LIVE PII-leak window**: ports dev-team's local-only team knowledge routing (XACA-0754/XACA-0754-013/XACA-0754-014) into both shipped `kb-knowledge-*` helper files. Before this ticket, `share/templates/aliases/kanban-aliases.sh` (preferred installed file) and `share/templates/kanban/kanban-helpers.template.sh` (fallback) had `kb-knowledge-add`/`-search`/`-reindex` but ZERO local-routing — a finance/legal/medical session authored on a tap host (M4Mini) had every write, including PII, sent straight to the synced `~/knowledge` root with no local floor. Ported 12 privates + 1 public: `_kb_overlay_config_path`, `_kb_knowledge_local_root`, `_kb_local_only_teams_hardcoded` (3-team floor), `_kb_local_personas_hardcoded` (17-persona floor), `_kb_is_local_only_team`, `_kb_current_session_is_local_only_team`, `_kb_context_resolved`, `_kb_ambiguous_tier_write_guard` (the `--force` fail-closed guard), `_kb_local_team_persona_group`, `_kb_local_personas_for_team`, `_kb_is_local_persona`, `_kb_knowledge_project_path_effective`, and `kb-knowledge-local-scaffold`. `kb-knowledge-add` (all four tiers), `kb-knowledge-search` (root-merge), and `kb-knowledge-reindex`/`_kb_knowledge_reindex_one` (local-root walk + tier detection) now route to `~/knowledge-local` for finance-personal/legal-coparenting/medical-general, using ONLY the hardcoded floors — no `~/dev-team/.claude/agents-master` checkout exists on a bare tap machine, so the floors (not the agents-master scan) are the load-bearing safety control there; the agents-master fallback in `_kb_local_personas_for_team` degrades to a clean no-op (never errors, never leaks) when absent. **Context-adaptation:** `kanban-aliases.sh` has no `_kb_detect_context`, so `_kb_current_session_is_local_only_team`/`_kb_context_resolved` use this file's own `_kb_get_team` resolver instead (which never fails — `KB_TEAM_OVERRIDE` → `KANBAN_TEAM` → `"academy"` default — so the ambiguous-write guard is a documented no-op there); `kanban-helpers.template.sh` keeps its real `_kb_detect_context` (tmux/env/`.kb-team`-sentinel) unchanged, so the guard genuinely fail-closes an unresolvable session there. `kb-knowledge-local-scaffold` unions the hardcoded persona floor with `_kb_local_personas_for_team`'s roster (a deliberate, documented improvement over the dev-team original, which relies solely on the agents-master scan and would otherwise scaffold zero persona `INDEX.md` files on a bare machine). New `tests/test-xaca-0770-local-only-knowledge-in-tap.sh` — 22 sandboxed cases (`TEST_TMP_DIR`-isolated, no dev-team checkout, no tmux, no network) against both shipped files: function-parity, team/agent/subject routing to `~/knowledge-local` via the hardcoded floors, shareable-team writes still routing to the synced root (no over-containment regression), `kb-knowledge-local-scaffold` idempotency, search/reindex discovering local-root entries, the `--force` ambiguous-context guard's refuse/allow behavior on the fallback file, and the `team-paths.json` `"local_only": true` extensibility flag. `tests/test-xaca-0746-kb-knowledge-in-tap.sh`'s PartB subject-tier round trip updated to export `KB_TEAM` (that sandbox's default state — no tmux, no env signal — is now correctly refused by the new guard; this is the intended XACA-0754-014 contract, not a regression). Registered in `tests/ci-manifest` as `plain-shell`. Both files verified with `zsh -n` and the full existing knowledge-adjacent test suite (`test-xaca-0743`, `-0746`, `-0747`, `-0751-014`, `-0751-upgrade`) re-run clean. Does NOT bump `VERSION` or cut a release — a later step publishes it.
- XACA-0754 — `share/scripts/kb-init-team` gains a `--backfill-tiers` mode: idempotently backfills the four-tier-knowledge team+agent `INDEX.md` scaffold (XACA-0748) for an already-registered team, needing only `--team-id` (no board creation, no port allocation, no registry writes). Routes finance-personal/legal-coparenting/medical-general (and any future `team-paths.json` `"local_only": true` team) to `~/knowledge-local/` instead of the synced `~/knowledge/` — mirrors dev-team's `kanban-helpers.sh` PII-containment floor (XACA-0754 core, PR #669) via a bash-side replica, since `kb-init-team` is bash and `kanban-helpers.sh` is zsh and can't be sourced from it. Supports `--check-only` and `--dry-run`. `_kbit_local_only_teams_hardcoded()` now carries an explicit "keep in sync with kanban-helpers.sh" comment, enforced by a cross-floor drift-guard test in dev-team's `scripts/tests/test-kb-init-team.sh` (SECTION H) — review fix closing a sibling-heuristic-drift gap (two hand-maintained copies of the same PII team floor, no automated check tying them together). Canonical source: `dev-team/scripts/kb-init-team`; mirrored here via `sync-tap.sh`.

### Fixed
- XACA-0763 (005) — **Retired `com.aiteamforge.lcars-runatload` LaunchAgent.** `lcars-health` already covers the post-reboot cold-start case this agent was designed for (XACA-0626 Defect C relaxed its tmux gate), making runatload redundant as a fourth LCARS lifecycle writer and an orphan maintenance burden. Template deleted from `share/templates/auto-upgrade/lcars-runatload.template.plist`; install-side wiring removed from `install-kanban.sh`; upgrade/migrate run sequences no longer render it. New `remove_legacy_lcars_runatload_agent()` in `libexec/lib/common.sh` is invoked from BOTH `aiteamforge upgrade` and `aiteamforge migrate` so every deployed machine self-heals on the next routine upgrade (probes the LOADED job via `launchctl print`, not just the plist file, ensuring an already-firing orphan is reaped). **See dev-team CHANGELOG XACA-0763 entry for full context:** the retirement is step 4 of a four-part fix to LCARS server reapers. Also in this release: XACA-0763-002 (plist `AbandonProcessGroup=true`), XACA-0763-003 (setsid spawn shim), XACA-0763-004 (collapse health-check duplicate).
- XACA-0763 (002) — `AbandonProcessGroup=true` added to three LaunchAgent plist templates: `lcars-health-plist.template`, `lcars-watch-launchagent.template.plist`, and `cellar-watch-launchagent.template.plist`. When a launchd job exits, it sends `SIGKILL` to every process still in its process group (unless the job plist carries this key). LCARS servers spawned from these jobs were inheriting the job's pgid and dying within seconds; this key allows them to outlive the job. **See dev-team CHANGELOG XACA-0763 for root cause, evidence (M4Mini 2026-07-09), and the complete fix.** Deployed machines auto-pick-up the new plist templates on the next `aiteamforge upgrade`.
- XACA-0763 (003,004) — `scripts/lcars-launch-helpers.sh` refactored the LCARS server-spawn logic: new `_lcars_spawn_detached()` calls `os.setsid()` (new session AND process group) then `os.execvp()`, so the server survives launchd's SIGKILL-to-pgid while keeping its pid; the shim is inline (`python -c`), not a shipped file, because `update_runtime_helpers` refreshes only already-installed scripts and would silently skip a NEW mandatory file on upgrade, and `lcars-health-check.sh::_hc_start_lcars_server` (tap: `share/scripts/`) collapsed into a thin wrapper delegating to `start_lcars_server` (eliminating sibling-drift and a concurrent-startup pkill race). **See dev-team CHANGELOG XACA-0763** (root cause analysis, PID trackability notes, `ps eww` parse invariants, and XACA-0763-006 regression test) **and** `docs/auto-upgrade-runbook.md` section "LCARS Lifecycle Ownership" (when to add `AbandonProcessGroup` to future LaunchAgents).
- XACA-0762 — `libexec/commands/aiteamforge-start.sh` `check_port_health()` now passes `--yes` to its auto-reconcile `--apply` call, so the self-heal it announces can actually succeed under the supervisor that invokes it. **Root cause:** XACA-0702 made a failed `--check` auto-apply the documented remediation (`kb-port-fix.py --apply`) instead of aborting with a "fix it yourself" message. But `kb-port-fix.py`'s `cmd_apply()` requires `--yes` OR an interactive confirmation whenever `sys.stdin.isatty()` is false — and `aiteamforge start` runs under `lcars-watch`, whose stdin is never a tty. Without `--yes`, the "auto-apply" branch was therefore GUARANTEED to fail every single time it fired: `cmd_apply()` printed "Non-interactive stdin detected. Pass --yes to apply without confirmation." and returned 1, `check_port_health()` propagated that failure, and both call sites (`check_port_health || exit 1`, in the `all` and `lcars|kanban` legs of the `case "$SERVICE"` dispatch) aborted startup. Net effect observed on M4Mini 2026-07-09: ONE team with a `null` `lcars_port` in `~/.aiteamforge/team-paths.json` prevented EVERY team's LCARS server from starting — the self-heal that exists specifically to rescue that state was the thing blocking it. **Fix:** the `--apply` invocation is now `--apply --yes`; the loud pre-apply warning (which already discloses that `team-paths.json` port assignments are about to be mutated) and the failure/remediation messages are updated to match. Consent is already implied by that warning, and `cmd_apply()` writes a timestamped backup (`<config>.bak-xaca0463-<ts>`) before mutating, so the auto-consent is recoverable. A new comment on the call site documents the non-interactive-stdin constraint the code alone can't show. **Tests:** new `tests/test-xaca-0762-start-port-reconcile-yes.sh` (14 cases) — Case 1 (behavioral): `kb-port-fix.py --apply --yes` against a null-port fixture with closed stdin exits 0 and reassigns a concrete in-band port, and a subsequent `--check` then passes. Case 2 (negative control): the same fixture driven with `--apply` (no `--yes`) and closed stdin exits non-zero, names the `--yes` remediation in stderr, and leaves the fixture unmodified — pins the upstream `kb-port-fix.py` contract that makes the fix necessary. Case 3 (structural regression guard — the case that would have caught the original bug): extracts `check_port_health()`'s body from the real `aiteamforge-start.sh` and asserts the `"${port_fix_cmd[@]}" --apply` invocation itself carries `--yes`, anchored precisely on the array-invocation form so it can't false-pass on the `--yes` text that also appears in the adjacent `print_warning`/`print_error` prose lines. Case 4 (end-to-end): sources the real, unmodified `check_port_health()` (extracted, not reimplemented) into a throwaway bash subprocess alongside `common.sh` and invokes it directly against a null-port fixture with closed stdin — exercising the exact `--check` → `--apply --yes` → re-`--check` sequence `aiteamforge start`/`restart` runs, without launching any real LCARS/Fleet servers; a preflight case guards against a real tap install shadowing `aiteamforge-port-fix` on `PATH`, which would invalidate that case's libexec-fallback assumption. All cases are sandboxed under `TEST_TMP_DIR` with `AITEAMFORGE_DIR` and `AITEAMFORGE_CONFIG` (the real seam `kb-port-fix.py` reads) pointed at fixtures; a final guard case asserts the real `~/.aiteamforge/team-paths.json` is byte-identical before and after the suite. Registered in `tests/ci-manifest` as `plain-shell`. Verified the structural + end-to-end cases fail (3 of 14) when `--yes` is reverted from the call site alone, confirming the suite actually detects this regression rather than passing vacuously. **PR #671 review/test fold-in (2026-07-09):** four non-blocking hardening items surfaced by the test and review gate bots, folded into this same ticket rather than filed as follow-ups. (1) `cmd_apply()`'s backup (`shutil.copy2`) and atomic write (`_atomic_write`) are now wrapped in `try`/`except OSError`, so a read-only config parent dir (or any other backup/write failure) prints a single `ERROR: ...` line naming the path and reason and returns 1, instead of leaking a raw Python traceback to stderr; a failed backup no longer falls through to the write. (2) `_load_team_paths()` — the single load site all of `_safe_teams()`'s callers route through — now prints a `WARNING: ...` line naming the config path and what was wrong (non-object JSON root, or a present-but-non-object `"teams"` value) before falling back to the existing XACA-0463-013 tolerate-don't-crash behavior; that behavior itself is unchanged and deliberately NOT reversed, only made loud instead of silent, and fires exactly once per invocation. (3) A missing config file now makes `kb-port-fix.py --check`/`--apply`/detect-mode exit **3** instead of 1 (documented in the `--check` help text and module docstrings), distinguishing "nothing to check yet" from "found real issues"; `aiteamforge-start.sh`'s `check_port_health()` captures that exit code via a `set -e`-safe `if/else` (a bare `cmd; rc=$?` aborts the whole script under this file's `set -eo pipefail` the instant the command returns non-zero — verified empirically before choosing the guarded form) and now treats `rc == 3` as "degrade gracefully, warn and continue" — mirroring the existing "tool not installed" path — instead of falling into the auto-apply branch and hard-aborting `aiteamforge start`, which is exactly what happened before this fix: a single unconfigured team's missing `team-paths.json` could block every team's LCARS server from starting. Audited every caller of the changed exit code (`kanban-hooks/portfix_runner.py`, `scripts/kb-team-import`, `scripts/aiteamforge-team-paths-wizard.py`) — all three route through `portfix_runner.run_port_collision_fix()`'s "treat any non-zero as a non-fatal warning, always return 0" contract and never branch on the literal value 1, so widening the missing-config case to 3 is safe for all of them. (4) `tests/test-xaca-0762-start-port-reconcile-yes.sh` grows from 14 to 30 cases: Case 5 (colliding ports — coverage of the pre-existing, unchanged winner/renumber logic) drives both `kb-port-fix.py --apply --yes` directly and `check_port_health()` end-to-end against two instances sharing one port, asserting the deterministic winner (earlier `addedAt`) keeps its port, the loser is renumbered in-band, and a subsequent `--check` passes. Case 6 covers the malformed-`teams` (JSON array) warning from fix (2). Case 7 covers the missing-config exit-3 contract from fix (3), both at the `kb-port-fix.py` level and end-to-end through `check_port_health()`. Case 8 covers the read-only-parent-dir single-ERROR-no-traceback contract from fix (1), skipping cleanly when run as root (where the write would trivially succeed and false-pass the guard). Every new case was verified to fail against the pre-fix code (temporarily swapped in from this same commit's parent) before being verified green against the fix — see the ticket for the exact failure transcripts. Tap-native (`libexec/commands/` — no canonical-source mirror; `start.sh`'s auto-reconcile behavior originates in this tap per XACA-0702). Does NOT bump `VERSION` or cut a release — a later step publishes it as part of a normal tap release.

## [0.17.3] - 2026-07-09

### Security
- XACA-0751 (EPIC-0047) — knowledge-repo provisioning now passes `-c protocol.ext.allow=never` on both the reachability probe (`_knowledge_repo_reachable`) and the `git clone`. git's `ext::` transport treats a URL as a shell command to spawn; `KB_KNOWLEDGE_REPO_URL` is owner-controlled today, but this code runs unattended, nightly, as the user via the auto-upgrade LaunchAgent, so a URL is not a place to trust. The per-command flag refuses `ext::` even when a machine's **global** `protocol.ext.allow` is permissive (attacker-set or careless) — scoped to `ext` so `file://` fixtures and `ssh`/`https` remotes keep working. New test `C9` in `tests/test-xaca-0751-014-knowledge-url-resolution.sh` proves it with a positive control (raw `git ls-remote "ext::touch <canary>"` under a hostile global `protocol.ext.allow=always` MUST fire the canary, so the test cannot pass vacuously) and asserts the shipped `_knowledge_repo_reachable` does NOT — verified to fail 8/9 when the guard is stripped. Review fold-in, PR #670 (both gates APPROVED). Also aligns the 014 suite's temp-dir cleanup to the `find -depth -delete` damage-control idiom used by the sibling upgrade suite.

## [0.17.2] - 2026-07-09

### Fixed
- XACA-0751 (EPIC-0047) — knowledge-repo provisioning now resolves its clone URL through an **ordered candidate probe** instead of a single hard default, fixing an integration defect between two already-shipped tickets that left FULLY-authorized fleet machines without `~/knowledge`. XACA-0747's `install_knowledge_repo()` defaulted `KB_KNOWLEDGE_REPO_URL` to `git@github.com:DoubleNode/knowledge.git`, but XACA-0750 provisioned per-machine, REPO-SCOPED ed25519 **deploy keys** that are reachable only through a dedicated SSH host alias — `Host github-knowledge` (HostName github.com, IdentitiesOnly, its own IdentityFile); the canonical remote under that scheme is `git@github-knowledge:DoubleNode/knowledge.git`. Plain `git@github.com` carries no key on a fleet box, so `_knowledge_repo_reachable` failed, the fail-soft path engaged, and `~/knowledge` was never created — on a machine whose auth was entirely correct (proven on M4Mini: `ssh -T git@github.com` → publickey-denied while `git ls-remote git@github-knowledge:…` authenticates). The two tickets never met. **Fix:** new `_knowledge_resolve_repo_url()` walks candidates in priority order and clones from the first that authenticates non-interactively — (1) an explicit `KB_KNOWLEDGE_REPO_URL` used **verbatim** (the top-priority operator/sandbox escape hatch; the defaults are never probed when it is set), (2) the `github-knowledge` deploy-key alias, (3) plain `git@github.com`. Each candidate is probed with the existing `_knowledge_repo_reachable` (`GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, `ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout`, `git ls-remote --exit-code`) — reused, not reimplemented — so probing stays fast (first success short-circuits; at most two short probes on an unauthorized box, safe for the nightly auto-upgrade LaunchAgent) and fail-soft stays absolute (no candidate reachable → `return 0`, nothing created, `set -e`/`set -u` contained). Resolution now runs **only in the clone path**, AFTER the existing-clone State 1 check, so a healthy clone still fast-forwards on its own upstream (`@{u}`) and its remote is **never rewritten** — M3Pro's canonical HTTPS `~/knowledge` origin is preserved untouched. Each default candidate is individually overridable (`KB_KNOWLEDGE_REPO_URL_ALIAS` / `_DIRECT`) as a sandbox seam. **Diagnostics:** the misleading soft-skip message that blamed XACA-0750 ("not yet authorized … grant fleet auth per XACA-0750") — a ticket state the installer cannot know, and which had already succeeded — is replaced with one that NAMES the candidate URLs actually attempted: when the defaults were probed it reports the `github-knowledge` alias and the `github.com` URL and that neither authenticated; when an explicit `KB_KNOWLEDGE_REPO_URL` was the (failed) sole candidate it says so (defaults not tried).
